A closed list of 28 blocker codes (a `Finding` cannot be built with another).
- The model: Z.ai GLM 5.3 through OpenRouter (`z-ai/glm-5.3`, the `critic` role), a different producer than the Advisor's models, at its default `max` reasoning effort (on OpenRouter's listed benchmarks it avoids a made-up answer 61.1% of the time at `max` and 36.8% at `low`; Qwen3.8 2.4T is about equal on the listed numbers, Mistral Large 4 higher on that one measure with one host). It makes ONE call per recommendation and only labels each quote it is shown, in its source as the Data Clerk just read it again (supports, weak, unrelated, contradicts), and the newer stored news (bearish, with a quote that code finds in the text). It is never shown the thesis, the score, the stance or the profile. Every call carries a CONTROL CLAIM whose right answer code knows (an unrelated notice): a reply that does not call it unrelated did not read the text, and is thrown away (no retry). The return is then worked again without the quotes that did not hold, and it can only be lower than the Advisor's.
- `get_chat_model` takes a `timeout` (default 60, unchanged elsewhere): GLM 5.3 reasons before it answers, and the Critic asks for 240 s.

**Alternatives.** The model decides or scores (rejected: entries 24 and 26, a score a model writes is not checkable); a model-run tool loop (rejected, see 58); share the Advisor's scoring modules (rejected: P1); re-run the Advisor's whole calculation (rejected: Jacob asked for a re-fetch of the important values, not a recalculation); Qwen3.8 or Mistral Large 4 as the model (open question 7 of the spec: a paid bake-off on recorded evidence with deliberately wrong claims decides it).

**Consequences.** On the three real captures (Thursday 2026-11-05, 10:30 Eastern, a moderate profile): SNDK is denied for risk (very high, 70.0) and because it fell 52% in 21 sessions; PG (0.01% expected excess) for no profit potential; PG_open is approved at 44 of 75 shares (the 10% position limit), score 83. Found while building: `1 == True` in Python made a recorded number match a fetched bool (fixed and pinned); a price exactly 30% above its average was "overpriced" by float noise (rounded to six places); the runner's report crashed when the position size was unknown. The thresholds are choices, not measurements (the captures hold no six-month average, so the value rules were tried on invented inputs, labelled so); they live in `configs/critic_v1.json` and are Jacob's to tune, never the ledger's. No live run has been made: it needs Jacob's yes (Finnhub and the critic's model), and `.env` needs `CRITIC_MODEL=z-ai/glm-5.3` (a line there overrides the default). Tests: 597 in 17 new files (calendar, profile, work order, verification, config, integrity, recheck, evidence, timing, risk, value, scoring, the whole step, the real captures, the Account Manager's decisions, the desk and the runner), more in the extended ones (technicals, the metric catalogue, the chat model, the runner's script), 2,862 in the whole suite; and 14 deliberate breakages, each caught.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (entries 103 and 105).** The verdict is approved or denied and nothing between: a finding that asks for a person (`requires_human`) denies with `needs_human_review` (the score is not capped). The Critic reviews only a recommendation to buy: the steps that handled a `reduce` or an `add` (a reduction "not judged" for risk, value and chart; the bearish stance direction; the `adding_above_cost` warning, which read the cost basis; the action word in the model's prompt) are gone, and a record that is not a purchase is a `record_defect`. The "five dimensions" of this entry are the six of `configs/critic_v1.json` (entry 62); the entry is not rewritten.

## 60. The dilution red flag ignores vesting, debt offerings and private-issuance boilerplate (Advisor v1 Amendment 3)

**Problem.** Live runs of 2026-11-11 showed `dilution_current` blocking good candidates through the `red_flag` gate. Moderna (a 4x breakout, blend 66) was vetoed by an 8-K about $3 billion of convertible senior notes; AMD by an 8-K about a senior notes offering and a private-issuance sentence ("not being conducted in connection with a public offering"); Costco by a share count that rose 0.06% (employee vesting). Jacob's view: news of earnings, products, leadership and layoffs should drive the score, and a rising share count is usually unimportant for a mainstream company.

**Decision (`data_clerk/fundamentals.py`, `dilution_current`).** The metric is true only for (a) shares outstanding up by at least `MIN_SHARE_RISE_PCT` (1.0%) between the two balance sheets, (b) cash-flow proceeds from an issuance of common stock, (c) a dilution passage in an 8-K of the last 120 days that is not about debt (`DEBT_PASSAGE`: senior, convertible or subordinated notes, debentures, indenture, anti-dilution adjustment, capped call) and is not the "not a public offering" boilerplate (`NOT_AN_OFFERING`). The score blend and the quality haircut read the same metric, so the -1.00 filings score of those companies goes too.

**Alternatives.** Drop the flag (rejected: an at-the-market programme or a real follow-on offering is a fair veto); raise the threshold only (rejected: the Moderna and AMD cases were not about the count); let the model read the 8-K (rejected, entries 24 and 26: a program can check it).

**Consequences.** A convertible's future dilution is no longer a veto (the debt ratios still see the leverage). Weights of the blend and the trend rule's 90%-of-52-week-high and 15%-one-month limits are unchanged and still open with Jacob. `tests/test_fundamentals.py` has the Moderna, AMD, Costco and an equity-programme case.


## 61. A breakout path for the trend family (Advisor v1 Amendment 4)

**Problem.** Moderna on 2026-11-11 (the close $248.69, 12-1 momentum 757%, above its 20-, 50- and 200-day means, RSI 58.8) was not a trend candidate: the family wanted 90% of the 52-week high (it was at 88.6%) and at most 15% in a month (it was at 15.001%). Both rules are right for an ordinary stock and wrong for a confirmed leader that is digesting a big run.

**Decision (`advisor/families.py` `trend`, `configs/advisor_v1.json`).** Above BOTH the 50- and 200-day means: the one-month limit is 25% (`max_ret_21d_uptrend`), and with 12-1 momentum of at least 50% (`breakout_min_mom_12_1`) the proximity to the 52-week high needs only 80% (`breakout_min_pct_of_52w_high`). Everything else (momentum at least 15%, RSI under 75, %B at most 1.05, residual momentum) is unchanged, and a stock under its 50-day mean, or with no 50-day mean, keeps the old rules. A pass that used a relaxed rule says so in its notes. The numbers are Jacob's choice (25% was his example; 80% and 50% are the floor and the "strong" line proposed with it), not research results.

**Alternatives.** Raise the thresholds for everyone (rejected: it loosens the anti-chase rule for stocks in no trend); a separate `breakout` family with its own prior (rejected: a second vote for the same momentum evidence, entry 51's one-vote rule); a model judging the chart (rejected, entries 24 and 26).

**Consequences.** More stocks pass `trend`, mostly strong runners; the 25% limit is still under the RSI 75 and %B 1.05 stops. The 20-day edge still uses the family's 0.6% prior, so a runner is not rated higher for being a breakout. Tests: `tests/test_families.py` (Moderna's numbers, the limits on each side, an unknown 50-day mean).


## 62. The Critic reads the chart: a sixth dimension of 10 points (Critic v1 Amendment 1)

**Problem.** The Critic judged a price against its six-month average but not the shape of the chart: it could approve a bounce off a collapse (a "dead cat bounce"), a stock under both its averages, or miss that a strong one had just made a golden cross. Jacob asked for falling-knife and dead-cat detection and for golden-cross and other bullish patterns, as a weaker voice (10%) with the financial points reduced to make room.

**Decision (`critic/chart.py`, `configs/critic_v1.json`, `data_clerk/technicals.py`).** A sixth dimension `chart`, weight 10; `value` goes from 25 to 15 (the other weights stay). Fifteen patterns (eight bullish, seven bearish), each plain arithmetic over numbers the Data Clerk calculates for the Critic: six new metrics (`sma_50_vs_200`, `cross_50_200_20d`, `structure_63d`, `decline_from_peak_63d`, `rebound_from_trough_63d`, `sessions_since_trough_63d`) and six existing ones. Score: `clip(50 + points of the patterns that hold, 0, 100)`, each pattern's points in the config. The dead cat bounce is a blocker (a fall of 25% from the peak, a bounce of 5% or more off the low in the last 15 sessions, still 15% under the peak and under the 50-day mean) and forbids the great-deal bonus; a death cross and a weak chart are warnings. A pattern whose number is missing is unknown, never guessed. The falling-knife blocker of the value dimension is unchanged. The Data Clerk, not the Critic, does the bar arithmetic, as for every other metric (the Critic does not read bars).

**Alternatives.** Let the model read the chart (rejected, entries 24 and 26); give the chart the weight of value (rejected: Jacob asked for a weaker voice, and patterns on daily bars are weak evidence); put the patterns in the Advisor's families (rejected: this is the second opinion, and the Advisor's score is a different producer's); a pattern library (TA-Lib: a dependency for fifteen comparisons).

**Consequences.** A review's score moves: the captured PG goes from 83 to 87 (its chart is near its high, MACD up, RSI 68, OBV up: 75 of 100, 7.5 of 10 points; seven patterns are unknown on the captures, which predate the six new metrics). Scores recorded before this change (`data/ledger/reviews.jsonl`) used the old weights and have no `chart` field. The points and thresholds are Jacob's choice, not research results, and need a look after a few live runs; a dead cat bounce is a blocker by that choice. Tests: `tests/test_critic_chart.py` (each pattern at its bound), `tests/test_technicals.py` (the six metrics worked by hand and against a plain reference), `tests/test_critic_config.py`.


## 63. A high blend is a recommendation by itself: the `conviction` family (Advisor v1 Amendment 5)

**Problem.** The Yum! Brands run of 2026-11-12 (blend 70: price +0.58, history +0.71, filings +0.89) was "not recommended": no strategy passed (`trend` wants 15% twelve-month momentum, `capital_return` passed but its edge was under the cost) and a blend alone was worth at most 0.30% (`no_strategy_edge_scale`) against a 0.20% cost. The report's "why" was the strategy checks, not the news and the chart. Jacob: the financial and strategy rules are far too strict; news, performance and the rest should be enough when they are high.

**Decision.** A sixth family, `conviction` (`advisor/families.py`): passes when the blend is at least 0.30, at least three of its four parts have a value, the news part is not negative and RSI is under 75. It has a fixed score (0.5) so the blend is not counted twice (the blend already scales every edge), a 0.6% prior borrowed from `trend` and called unmeasured, a 40-bar horizon, a 3 ATR stop and the usual one vote per family. The blend is computed before the families so the family can read it. The best edge still wins, so a name that passes a specific strategy with a larger edge keeps that strategy. The Critic's counterfactual drops it when the blend, without the quotes the review did not accept, falls under the mark.

**Alternatives.** Raise `no_strategy_edge_scale` until the blend alone beats the cost (rejected: the stance stays neutral and a trade plan needs a family: stop, horizon, invalidation); lower the thresholds of `trend` and `capital_return` (rejected: they would also fire for names with no support from the news or the chart); let the model decide (rejected, entries 24 and 26).

**Consequences.** More recommendations: any name with a blend of 65 or more, three parts and no negative news. The captured PG (blend 78) changes from "neutral, denied for no profit potential (0.010% after cost)" to a conviction recommendation with 0.125% after cost, approved by the Critic with a thin-edge warning; PG_open's trade family goes from `capital_return` (126 bars, no target) to `conviction` (40 bars, a target). A recommendation whose news part is missing is possible (three parts are enough): the notes say "no news counted". The thresholds (0.30, three parts, RSI 75) and the borrowed prior are Jacob's choices, not research results. Tests: `tests/test_families.py` (the bounds), `tests/test_advisor_sndk_pg.py`, `tests/test_critic_real.py` (the counterfactual).


## 64. The Critic's feedback travels with the recommendation (Critic v1 Amendment 2)

**Problem.** The Critic's verdict lived on `order.reviews`, beside the recommendations and joined by `record_id` (entry 59, principle P5: the Advisor's record is never edited). Whoever has only the recommendation (the report, a later screen, a person who is asked) has to find and read a long review to learn why it was approved or denied. Jacob wants the Critic's blended score as the final gate, and its feedback in the recommendation's metadata, so it can be returned to the user and drawn in a later GUI.

**Decision.** `critic/feedback.py` builds a compact `critic` block from each review and puts it on the recommendation it judged (`Recommendation.critic`; the Account Manager adds `critic.decision`). The block is flat and ordered for a chart: the six dimensions in a fixed order with weight, score, points, share, a colour `band` and a one-line headline; the gate spelled out (the blended base score, the bonus, the mark of 70, the cap of 49, what blocked, the margin); the chart patterns; the findings; the sizes; the decision. The gate itself is unchanged (a weighted blend of the six dimensions with blockers as vetoes: that is the Critic's final gate); it is now shown, with its arithmetic. P5 is narrowed: the Critic never edits a field the Advisor wrote, and adds only this one key; a test compares every other field byte for byte.

**Alternatives.** Leave the review only on `order.reviews` and let the GUI join them (rejected: two places to keep in step, and a recommendation read alone is silent); copy the whole review into the record (rejected: the model's labels, the rechecks and the audit make a record several times larger and the GUI needs a tenth of it); write the block into the Advisor's ledger line (rejected: that line is the Advisor's record of what it said, written before the review; the reviews ledger has the Critic's).

**Consequences.** `Recommendation` has a new optional field, so every Advisor record dumps `critic: null` until reviewed. A record read back from the Advisor ledger has none (the ledger line is written before the review). The review and the block are one truth: the block is built from the review and the tests compare their numbers. A screen should read the block and fall back to `order.reviews` (linked by `review_id`) for the details. Tests: `tests/test_critic_feedback.py`.


## 65. The Critic has no profit-potential rule (Critic v1 Amendment 3)

**Problem.** The Critic denied a recommendation (`no_profit_potential`) when the Advisor's expected 20-day excess return after cost was under 0.05%, warned (`thin_edge`) under 0.20%, scored it as a quarter of the value dimension, and flagged a better candidate by the same number. The number is the product of priors that nobody has measured (a 0.3% to 0.6% edge, a 0.2% cost), so a difference of a tenth of a percent decided a verdict. Procter & Gamble (sales up 4% in the quarter, billions in profit) was "denied for no profit potential" at 0.010% and approved at 0.125%: a rule that is misleading about a company that plainly makes money. Jacob: remove the rule and the metric.

**Decision.** `no_profit_potential`, `thin_edge`, `better_candidate_left_out`, `value.min_excess_after_cost`, `value.thin_excess` and the `expected_excess` value measure are removed (the value score is discount 50, reward to risk 30, earnings yield 20). The counterfactual keeps only the blend worked again with the review's labels. Its job (a record that stands on quotes the review did not accept must not be approved on them) is kept where it matters: the event strategy's `event_not_supported` blocker, the evidence score, and a new blocker `blend_not_supported` for a position that stands on the blend alone (the `conviction` family, entry 63) when its blend, without the quotes that did not hold, is under 0.30.

**Alternatives.** Keep the metric as information only (rejected: it still decided the value score and read as a promise); lower the floor to zero (rejected: a rule at zero is the same rule); drop the counterfactual altogether (rejected: it is what notices that a conviction recommendation was a blend of quotes that did not hold).

**Consequences.** Scores move (the captured PG goes from 87 to 88: the value dimension's 15 points are now two-thirds earned by the price against its six-month average, reward to risk and earnings yield). The Critic no longer says anything about how much a stock may earn: it judges accuracy, evidence, risk, timing, price and chart. The Advisor still ranks by its expected excess return after cost; whether to change that is open (the Advisor's `excess_20d_after_cost` is read by the Critic's integrity check only to confirm the Advisor's own arithmetic). Tests: `tests/test_critic_value.py`, `tests/test_critic_evidence.py`, `tests/test_critic_real.py`.


## 66. The Advisor has no cost hurdle: the blend ranks and a passed strategy recommends (Advisor v1 Amendment 6)

**Problem.** The Advisor called a name bullish only when its expected 20-day excess return after cost was above zero, ranked by that number, derived the score from it and printed it everywhere (the ranking, the recommendation line, the thesis). The number is `edge x scale - cost` from priors of 0.3% to 0.6% and a 0.2% cost: a difference of a tenth of a percent decided a verdict, and a recommendation for Procter & Gamble (sales up 4%, billions in profit) read "0.01% expected". Jacob called the rule stupid and misleading and asked for it to go from the Advisor and the Critic (entry 65 did the Critic's side).

**Decision (`advisor/ranking.py`, `recommend.py`, `thesis.py`, `application/recommendations.py`).** No cost hurdle: a name is bullish when a bullish strategy passed (an edge above zero). The blend of news, performance, historical metrics and filings (entries 55 and 63) ranks the candidates and IS the score (`round(50 + 50 x blend)`). The excess, the cost and the scale factor stay on the record (the Critic's integrity check works them again) but decide nothing and no report, thesis or card shows them. The thesis no longer states or labels an expected return ("unmeasured" is no longer required). The Critic's integrity checks follow (the stance needs a composite above zero; the score is the blend's).

**Alternatives.** Remove the field and the cost from the record and the ledger (rejected for now: a larger change across the ledger, the fixtures and the Critic's arithmetic, with no gain for the user once nothing reads it); keep a cost hurdle at zero (rejected: it is the same rule); rank by the strategy edge (rejected: a prior, not evidence about the company).

**Consequences.** More recommendations: every name for which a strategy passes (including `conviction`, entry 63) is one, in the order of its blend, and the Critic's checks (accuracy, evidence, risk, timing, price, chart) are the filter. A recommendation with a very small edge is no longer filtered by the cost. The score of a record changes meaning (it was the excess mapped to 0 to 100; it is now the blend); `top_recommendation` still picks the highest. The older ledger lines have the old score. Tests: `tests/test_ranking.py`, `tests/test_thesis.py`, `tests/test_recommendations.py`, `tests/test_critic_integrity.py`, `tests/simple_critic.py`.


## 67. Companies are compared in the same unit (Data Clerk, Jacob's rule of 2026-11-12)

**Problem.** Figures of different companies are compared, ranked and put in one table (payout yield against market cap, market caps side by side, average volume). They come in different units: a filing says "(In millions)" or "(In thousands)", Finnhub quotes market capitalization and volume in millions. `fundamentals.multiplier` read only "in millions/thousands/billions" and assumed DOLLARS for anything else, so a statement headed "(Thousands of dollars)", "(000s)" or with no units line was silently read a thousand or a million times too small, and a company reported that way would be ranked against one reported in millions with a wrong number. The one provider metric kept in a scaled unit, `adv_10d_provider` ("M shares"), was the same kind of risk.

**Decision.** Every quantity the Data Clerk gives is in a base unit: money in dollars, volume in shares. `multiplier` now finds the first scale word of a units line (billions, millions, thousands, `$000s`, `000's`, "thousands of dollars"; the first wins, so Apple's "In millions, except ... shares, which are reflected in thousands" is millions) and a line that says dollars with no scale is 1; a line that is missing or says neither raises `Missing`, so the derived metric is `None` with the reason: a figure of unknown scale is never assumed to be dollars. `adv_10d_provider` is converted to shares (`FINANCIAL_SCALE`) like `market_cap`. A test pins that no unit of the catalogue is a scaled one.

**Alternatives.** Keep assuming dollars (rejected: wrong by a thousand or a million, silently); normalize only at display time (rejected: the ranking and the Critic read the numbers, not the text); a unit library such as `pint` (rejected: a dependency for three scale words).

**Consequences.** A statement whose units line the parser cannot read gives `None` for the payout metrics (and the strategies that need them say why) instead of a wrong number. The ratio metrics (fractions, percentages, multiples) have no unit to convert. The percentage metrics Finnhub quotes in percent (`roe`, margins, growth) are all in percent, never mixed with fractions: the unit column says which. Tests: `tests/test_fundamentals.py`, `tests/test_metrics_catalog.py`.


## 68. The expected return after cost is removed from the Advisor's record (Advisor v1 Amendment 7)

**Problem.** `ranking.excess_20d_after_cost` (with `cost_pct` and `scale_factor`) was `edge x (1 + 0.5 x blend) - cost`. The edge is a fixed prior per strategy and the cost is a default; the record itself says the basis is "prior" (unmeasured). The number was never checked against any outcome, moved by hundredths of a percent when a family was added, and looked like a measurement. Entry 66 stopped using it; Jacob asked to fix it, meaning to remove it.

**Decision.** The three fields are removed from the records, the candidates, the Scored object, the config and the Critic's checks (the arithmetic of the confidence, the blend and the score is still checked). The strategy edge stays as the way to pick the strongest strategy and the composite, and for the trade plan's target; it is not an expected return. An evaluator built from the realized outcomes of past recommendations was proposed and declined: past results do not predict the future, and months of data would be needed before it meant anything.

**Alternatives.** Keep the fields as internal numbers (rejected: unused numbers invite use); keep a cost without an edge (rejected: the Trader does not exist, so the real cost is unknown); the outcome tracker (rejected by Jacob).

**Consequences.** Records and the ledger have no expected return; older ledger lines still do (the Advisor reads them by key only: the cooldown reads the trade and the time stop). A reader who wants "how much could it make" gets no number from the system, by design. Tests: `tests/test_ranking.py`, `tests/test_critic_integrity.py`, `tests/test_advisor_sndk_pg.py`, `tests/simple_critic.py`.


## 69. `IGNORE_MARKET_HOURS` skips the closed-market denial (Critic, Jacob, 2026-11-12)

**Problem.** A review at night, before the 04:00 pre-market open, denies every recommendation with `market_closed` and caps the score at 49, even when the recommendation is for that same trading day and every other check passed. Jacob wants to review (and, in Ask mode, be asked) outside the trading window while he is testing. The flag `IGNORE_MARKET_HOURS=true` is in his `.env`.

**Decision (`critic/timing.py`).** The flag is read when a review runs, from the environment and then from `.env` (`true`, `yes`, `on` or `1`). While it is set, a closed market is an info finding (`market_hours_ignored`) and not the `market_closed` blocker. The timing score is unchanged (a closed session still earns none of the session half). Every other timing check still applies, including `record_expired`: a recommendation from an earlier trading day is still denied. The run records `ignore_market_hours` and the report's header says so. Tests force the flag off, so they do not inherit the `.env`.

**Alternatives.** A `--now` in the regular session (rejected: it also moves the record's trading day, so a recommendation made today looks expired); drop the gate for good (rejected: a weekend trade on a stale price is what the gate is for); skip `record_expired` too (rejected: that is a different rule, and Jacob asked for the market-open gate).

**Consequences.** With the flag, a review before the open of the recommendation's own day can approve. The approval then lasts an hour (`valid_until`), because a closed session has no window end to cut it. The Trader is not built, so nothing is sent to a market. Unset or any other value, the gate is as it was.


## 70. `dilution_current` is not evaluated (Advisor v1 Amendment 8, Critic v1 Amendment 4)

**Problem.** `dilution_current` blocked a recommendation when the stored filings showed shares up by 1% or more, proceeds from issuing common stock, or an 8-K about an equity offering. It was a red-flag gate, a quality haircut (factor 0.6), a filings measure in the blend (a bad reading scored -1), a required input of `capital_return`, a bearish vote of that family (score 0.6, prior -0.3%, also from a cited 8-K or derived dilution item), and the family's invalidation rule. The Critic asked the Data Clerk for it again whenever a record's strategy or blend used it, and denied an open whose red-flag gate had failed on it. Visa (2026-11-12) was not recommended because the metric was true. Jacob: we are not evaluating on this.

**Decision.** The metric is removed from the Data Clerk's catalogue (`fundamentals.dilution_current` and its catalogue entry). The Advisor no longer requests it, and none of the five uses above remain (`prior_bearish` and `bearish_score` go with the bearish vote). The Critic only re-asks for names still in the catalogue, so it neither fetches nor compares `dilution_current`. An older record whose invalidation says `metric_crossed: dilution_current` does not fire that rule.

**Alternatives.** Keep the metric and stop only the red-flag gate (rejected: the haircut, the blend and the family vote would still decide on it); keep computing it and ignore the value (rejected: a number that is fetched gets used).

**Consequences.** A company is not denied, haircut or scored for issuance under this name. `share_count_change` is unchanged (a rising share count still fails `capital_return` and still haircuts quality above 1%). A news item the model classes `dilution_offering` is unchanged (it can still refuse `reversion`). A filing passage the model cites still votes in the filings part of the blend, and a dilution passage is still bearish there. Tests: `tests/test_families.py`, `tests/test_gates.py`, `tests/test_metrics_catalog.py`.


## 71. Two Critic checks were comparing the wrong thing (2026-11-12)

**Problem.** Two checks denied, or failed to deny, for a reason that was not the one they named. `price_mismatch` compared the stored daily close with the quote's last trade whenever the two shared a calendar date. Outside the regular session that last trade is an after-hours or pre-market print, so a move after the close of more than 1% capped the score at 49. `IGNORE_MARKET_HOURS` does not touch this check. Separately, the blend stores every quote from one source under one ref (`[4]`), while the claim list numbers the second quote `[4]#2`. The counterfactual looked up only `[4]`, so the second label never moved the blend and `blend_not_supported` could miss a quote the review had rejected.

**Decision.** A last trade counts as that day's close only when its time is in the regular session, including the print at the close itself. A pre-market or after-hours print on the bar's date is not compared. The trading day before the quote's date is still compared with `previous_close`. Each blend row takes the label of its own claim, in order: the first row `[4]`, the next `[4]#2`. The same applies to a repeated filing-evidence id.

**Alternatives.** Compare an after-hours print with `previous_close` (rejected: that is the prior session's close); keep comparing and widen the tolerance at night (rejected: the numbers are not the same close).

**Consequences.** A review before the open is no longer denied because the last print drifted from the official close. A second quote from one article can still pull a conviction blend under 0.30. Tests: `tests/test_critic_recheck.py`, `tests/test_critic_evidence.py`.


## 72. The account book is Postgres, and the Account Manager reads and writes it (2026-11-12)

**Problem.** The week 2 design keeps the portfolio and the profile in Postgres. What is built is two JSON files, and the Account Manager could not take a deposit, list the saved holdings, set the user's risk, or say what the booked trades made. A later screen will also need the holder's name, an account number, and contact fields. Jacob asked for that on the existing Account Manager, with the schema saved in the project and Postgres running in a container.

**Decision.** Postgres 18 in `docker-compose.yml`, published on 127.0.0.1 only. `sql/prometheus_accounts.sql` is the schema and the grants; `sql/seed_test_users.sql` is three sample accounts; `scripts/setup_postgres.sh` starts the container and applies both. The application role is not a superuser, cannot create objects, and cannot update the cash column: a deposit or a trade inserts a ledger row, and a trigger checks that the new balance is the old balance plus the amount. Passwords use scram-sha-256 over SSL. `sql/docker-entrypoint-ssl.sh` creates a self-signed server certificate in the data volume (the key is mode 600 and is not in git) and the server refuses a connection that is not SSL. The passwords are not in the SQL file and not in `.env`; the script writes `secrets/postgres_local.txt`, which is gitignored. `AccountManager` gains `add_funds`, `list_portfolio`, `set_risk_level`, `calculate_total_gain`, `number_trades`, `number_approved_recommendations`, `number_denied_recommendations`, `account_balance`, and `users` (name, account number, email, phone, timezone, status, and the other screen fields). The risk levels are the three already on the profile: conservative, moderate, aggressive. Realized gain is the booked profit on sells in the window (sale proceeds minus the average cost of the shares sold). A deposit is not a gain. Shares still held are not marked to a price. When the manager is given the store, `decide_trades` and `record_user_decision` write the verdict (`yes_auto` and `yes_user` count as approved; the four `no_` verdicts count as denied; `pending_user` counts as neither until it is answered). `record_trade` books a fill in the ledger. It does not send an order. The Trader is still not built, and the desk still loads the JSON profile and portfolio unless it is handed the store.

**Alternatives.** SQLite in a file (rejected: the week 2 design names Postgres, and the request was a container); one superuser for the application (rejected); the password inside the SQL file or in `.env` (rejected: the SQL is committed, and `.env` holds the API keys); counting a deposit as a gain (rejected: that is new cash, not a result of a trade); marking the open positions to a live quote (rejected: that calls Finnhub, and the number would move whenever the quote moved).

**Consequences.** `bash scripts/setup_postgres.sh` brings the database up and, the first time, loads Jacob's sample (cash 51.23, MSFT, TSLA, GM, moderate, Ask), Avery Chen (conservative, 2026 realized gain -27.50) and Blake Okonkwo (aggressive, Auto). `--reseed` replaces those three and leaves every other account. Re-running the seed assigns new account numbers. `python -m prometheus.agents.account_manager.account_manager --account avery_chen` prints one account and does not call a model or a quote. Tests: `tests/test_account_book.py`. The JSON files are unchanged and remain the desk default.


## 73. The Trader trades on paper, and its model has four guarded tools and a code sweep (Trader v1, 2026-11-13 and 14)

**Problem.** The week 2 design ends with a Trader that executes what the Account Manager permits and reports the fills back, so that the portfolio is updated. The desk stopped at the decisions (entries 56 and 72): a `yes_auto` or `yes_user` was a permission nobody used. Jacob wants no live trading in this version, and the same rule as everywhere else (entries 24 and 26): a model must not be the only thing between the desk and a bad trade. A made-up trade (a symbol nobody approved, more shares than approved, a price outside what the Critic judged, the same trade twice) has to be impossible, not discouraged.

**Decision (`design/trader_v1_final_spec.md`, `prometheus/agents/trader/`).**
- The fifth agent, `Trader.execute_trades(order, account, portfolio, now, funding, approver, book)`: ten steps in `execute.py` (preconditions, account, tickets, prices, funding consent, the model's loop, the sweep, the output guard, outcomes, records). Two modes and no other: `paper` (the current quote, asked through the Account Manager) and `backtest` (the stored daily bars). `live` is refused in code (by the settings loader and again when a run starts), `PAPER_TRADING` set to anything but true refuses the whole run (step 1 of `execute.py`), no broker SDK is imported and the Trader makes no network call of its own. The desk runs it as the `trader` node after the decisions (`desk --critic --trade [paper|backtest]`), `./test_recommendations.sh --trade` runs it, and `python -m prometheus.agents.trader.replay` replays a saved order.
- **Temperature 0 makes a run repeatable. It does not make the model truthful, and nothing relies on it.** The model is `openai/gpt-oss-120b` through OpenRouter (the `trader` role, `TRADER_MODEL`) at temperature 0, on the fast hosts with `require_parameters` so that a host without tool calls is not used. The same order gives the same tool calls, and the tools node is written by hand and runs the calls of one turn one after the other (the prebuilt `ToolNode` runs them at the same time, and two purchases in one turn would race for the same cash). **What stops a made-up trade is that the model has no way to express one.** It has four tools: `verify_request`, `place_order`, `check_trade_status`, `notify_account_manager`. A trade is a `trade_id` that code issued; code owns the symbol, the side, the shares and the price range. `place_order` takes a trade id, an order type and a limit price that must lie inside the range code computed; no argument names a symbol, a side or a number of shares, and an argument a tool does not have is refused, not dropped. Inside placing, `work_order.verify_trade` runs again. A trade is placed once (a `filled` or `rejected` trade is final, also on a later run), every sale before any purchase, and nothing after the Account Manager was told. A call over the budget is refused.
- **The sweep.** After the loop, code places the permitted trades the model left (the default order, `placed_by: "code"`) and tells the Account Manager if the model did not. A model that fails, times out, answers nonsense or cannot start (a missing key) never stops a permitted trade and never causes one that is not permitted. `Trader(use_model=False)` (`--no-trader-model`) runs no model at all: the free and repeatable way, and the only way a backtest is free.
- **What the model reads and writes.** Its prompt and every tool result hold symbols, numbers and words from closed lists, all made by code (no thesis, no news quote, no company name: a hidden instruction in an article cannot reach this agent). A rejected trade is shown as its `reject_code` and never as its `reason`: that sentence is built from `verify_trade`, which repeats two values of the order that, in a file, could be any text. A trade's `symbol` must be a ticker (`check_symbol`); a decision with another gets no ticket. The model's words reach two places only, a trade's `note` (cut to 20 words) and the run's `summary`, which is kept only if it parses, names real trades with their real statuses, is at most 60 words and every number in it is a number of the trades; otherwise code writes its own and says why.
- **The rules T1 to T10** each have a test that fails when the guard is removed (permission is code; the model cannot size, price or pick; once; paper only; the Account Manager is the only door; code finishes the job; never raises for a data, model or account failure; the model's words reach two places; no web text reaches the model; the Advisor's, the Critic's and the Account Manager's records are byte for byte the same afterwards). The output guard `work_order.verify_trades` runs before anything is booked: a filled trade the decisions do not allow books nothing.
- Fills stay on the work order unless `--book` saves them in a saved account (paper only; entry 74). `costs.commission_per_order` must be 0 in `configs/trader_v1.json`: `apply_fill` and the account book charge no commission, so another value would count cash the account never pays.

**Alternatives.** One JSON plan from the model and no tools (rejected: nothing then stops a trade in it that the decision did not allow, and the week 2 design is a loop); a free ReAct loop whose tools take a symbol and a share count (rejected: that is the made-up trade); temperature 0 or a "do not exceed" line in the prompt as the safeguard (rejected: the first is repeatability, the second is a request); no model at all (kept as `--no-trader-model`, but the design's Trader is a model that works through the tickets, chooses an order inside the limits, checks and reports); the prebuilt `ToolNode` (rejected: it runs a turn's calls at the same time); Alpaca's paper API (not in this version: `broker.py` is where a second broker would go).

**Consequences.** No live run has been made: the build used scripted models and fakes, and the first one needs Jacob's yes (OpenRouter, and Finnhub in paper mode). What cannot be known without it: whether gpt-oss-120b on the fast hosts follows the tool protocol with `require_parameters`, how often its summary is replaced, how many turns it uses. All of it is harmless by design (the tools refuse, the sweep trades, code writes the summary), and a test per failure mode proves that with scripted models. An order that is `decided` with nothing permitted stays `decided` and the Trader says so. `desk --trade` saves the order (with its trades) under `data/processed/desk_<symbol>.json`, as `--critic` already did. Tests: `tests/test_trader*.py`, `tests/test_desk_trader.py`, `tests/test_recommendations_trader.py`, and one on the real Postgres in `tests/test_account_book.py`.


## 74. The Data Clerk reads the stored bars for the Trader, and the Account Manager is the Trader's only door (Trader v1)

**Problem.** The Trader needs a price: the current quote on paper, the stored daily bars in a backtest. The week 2 design says the Trader does not call the Data Clerk and that the Account Manager is told what was filled. A Trader that reached for the data itself, or for the Critic's or the Advisor's code, would be a second path to every source, and the order of work (the permission, the prices, the fills) could not be audited in one place. The desk also needed a way to use a saved account (entry 72) instead of the two JSON files.

**Decision.**
- **The Data Clerk's part.** A work-order action `fetch_bars`, a request field `bars` (`{"start", "end"}`), `DataResult.bars` (per symbol, oldest first) and `verify_bar_request` (1 to 30 symbols, two ISO dates, start not after end, at most 400 days apart). `DataClerk.request_bars` makes no model call, fetches nothing and writes nothing under `data/historical`: it reads the day files the store already has (`BarStore.load` takes a `start`, so only the days the range needs are read). A symbol with no bar in the range is an empty list and a note; the order is `data_failed` only when it is refused or the folder cannot be read. `DataClerkTools.daily_bars` is not in `DATA_CLERK_TOOLS`: the model has no use for it, and the 13-tool pin holds.
- **The Account Manager's part.** `request_prices` and `request_bars` (the only places a price is asked for), `trading_portfolio` (the saved account when connected, else the portfolio given), `connect` (attach the book after construction; it forgets a profile read from the default file and keeps one that was given), `perform_trades` (the design's Perform Trade: it only hands over), `notify_trades` (the design's `AccountManager.notify`: each filled trade is applied with `apply_fill`, and booked with `record_trade` when asked). A fill that cannot be applied or booked is named, left out of the portfolio that is returned, and the others go on. `apply_fill` and `trade_amount` live in `common/portfolio.py`; the lock rules are one code, `_buy_into`, shared with `after_purchase`, and amounts round half up as the account book rounds (Python's `round` goes to the even cent and would put the broker a cent from the book).
- **The door is checked.** A test reads the source of every Trader module and fails on an import from `prometheus.agents.data_clerk`, `critic`, `account_manager`, `tools.data_clerk` or `advisor` (one exception: `advisor.calls`, the shared JSON helper); from `prometheus.rag` only `config` and `loaders.check_symbol`. `replay.py` is wiring and the one module that may import the Data Clerk. The price request is made in groups of at most 30 symbols (the limit of a quote order and of a bars order) and the answers are joined; when one group fails, its symbols are `price_unavailable` and the others trade.
- **The desk and a saved account.** `request["account"]["account"]` is a sample key or an account number (`--account`). The desk opens the store (`open_book`, imported only then, so a desk that never uses a saved account never loads the Postgres driver), finds the id, connects first and sets a profile that was given after, and takes the portfolio from the book. `--book` saves the fills (paper only). A database failure becomes a `RuntimeError` that names the kind (`ConnectionError`, `TimeoutError`) and never the host, the user or the password; each node reports it as it reports a bad profile file. The funds node plans for the saved account's holdings, the ones the Trader trades.

**Alternatives.** The Trader asks the Data Clerk (rejected: the week 2 design, and one reader of each source); a Data Clerk tool for the model to read bars (rejected: the model has no use for them, and a number the model asks for is a number it can quote); the Trader reads `data/historical` itself (rejected: the Data Clerk is its only reader); a new store for the Trader's trades (rejected: the account book is the book, and the work order carries the rest); the connected account's decisions not saved unless `--book` (not done: entry 72 writes the verdict whenever a manager has a store, and the counts are a feature of the account).

**Consequences.** `desk --critic --account jacob_sample` writes the Account Manager's decisions into that account (entry 72's behaviour, with or without `--book`), which moves the sample's approved and denied counts that `tests/test_account_book.py` pins: use a throwaway account for a live run, or `bash scripts/setup_postgres.sh --reseed` afterwards. The Trader never touches the Postgres driver. A bar request over 30 symbols would be refused, so the Trader asks in groups.


## 75. Funding sells need a yes, and the backtest's fill and exit rules (Trader v1)

**Problem.** Jacob's sample portfolio holds $51.23, so most approved purchases carry the decision's note "needs RaiseFunds". `decisions.py` says a sale is never traded automatically. And a simulated broker has to say, in numbers, what a fill is, so that a paper run and a backtest give a result a person can check, and so that a backtest never uses the future.

**Decision.**
- **Funding.** The funds node's RaiseFunds plan is used only when its status is `plan` with sells, its target is a purchase the Trader is placing, and every sell is a holding, at least one share, at most what is sellable today (T+1), not the target itself, and has a price. All or nothing. **Every funding sale needs the user's yes, in Ask mode and in Auto mode** (`funding.require_user_approval`, true): the Trader calls the same approver once per funded purchase with a card that lists the sells; no answer, or no approver, is a no and nothing is sold. The sells belong to the purchase and the model never places them. Placing a funded purchase does this in code, in one go: check the purchase first (permission, price inside the limits, the order would fill now); work out what the sells would bring by running the SAME fill rule on the SAME snapshot, and if the cash plus that does not pay for even one share, sell nothing, reject the purchase `insufficient_cash` and each sale `funding_failed`; fill each sale; fill the purchase with the cash now there. The output guard backs it up: a filled funding sale whose purchase is not filled is a finding. Only the first permitted purchase is funded, because the funds node plans one.
- **Paper fills.** Market: `q x (1 + slippage)`; above the top of the range, `price_outside_limits`. Limit `L`: `q <= L` fills at `min(L, q x (1 + slippage))`, else the order rests (`active`). Slippage is 5 basis points (a buy above, a sale below), the commission is 0, every amount is to the cent (half up), and a purchase the cash does not cover is cut to the shares it can pay for (zero shares is `insufficient_cash`); buying fewer is inside the permission, buying more never is. A market order has a price limit too: 1% past the price the Critic judged, and never outside the Advisor's entry zone, so no order of any type fills outside the range.
- **Backtest fills.** The run has one entry date. First from the calendar alone: the first trading day whose regular open (09:30 Eastern) is strictly after the moment of the decision (a decision at 10:30 on Thursday enters on Friday; one at 08:00 enters on Thursday), which keeps the future out. Then from the bars: the earliest stored bar on or after that day, among every symbol asked for. Market: the open plus slippage. Limit `L`: the open at or under `L` fills at `min(L, open x (1 + slippage))`; otherwise a low at or under `L` fills at `L`; otherwise `limit_not_reached` (a day order, and the day is over). No bar stored yet: the order is `active` and a later run takes the next session's bar.
- **Backtest exits** (`simulator.follow`, a pure function), by the Advisor's own stop, target and time stop. On the entry bar only the stop can fire (the order of prices inside a day is not known, so the bad case is assumed). On a later bar, the first that holds: the open at or under the stop (a gap, at the open less slippage); the low at or under the stop (at the stop less slippage); a target and the open at or over it (at the open); the high at or over it (at the target); the date at or after the time stop (at the close less slippage). The stop is checked before the target, so a day that touches both counts as a stop. With no exit by the last stored bar the trade is `open`, marked at the last close. The benchmark (SPY) is measured over the same dates. Outcomes are a simulation: never booked, and paper mode has none.
- Every rejection carries a code of a closed list of twelve (`not_permitted`, `approval_expired`, `price_unavailable`, `price_outside_limits`, `limit_not_reached`, `insufficient_cash`, `not_held`, `not_sellable_today`, `funding_failed`, `not_attempted`, `account_refused`, `internal_error`) and a sentence for the report.

**Alternatives.** Sell automatically in Auto mode (rejected: `decisions.py`, and a sale is the one thing the Advisor never sized); sell first and check afterwards (rejected: the worst case is holdings sold and nothing bought, so the check is first and the dry run is exact); enter at the close of the decision's day or at its open (rejected: both use prices from before the decision could have been acted on); assume the target comes first on a day that touches both (rejected: it flatters the result); fund several purchases in one run (the funds node plans one); a backtest over a range of dates (needs the Advisor and the Critic to run as of a past date, and the Critic's quote is live).

**Consequences.** A backtest from `desk --trade backtest` made at the time of the decision usually finds no stored bar from the next session on, so its orders wait (`active`); a replay of a saved order with `--now` set to its moment is the useful backtest, and `--mode backtest --no-model` is free. The desk's exit code with `--trade` is 0 for an order that is `traded`, `decided` or `reviewed`. The results of a backtest are the Advisor's plan followed, not a measurement of the Advisor: one recommendation is a sample of one.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (entries 104 and 105).** "A sale is never traded automatically" (the `decisions.py` of that day) is replaced: a recommendation is never a sale (entry 105), so the only sales are the user's own direct order and the funding sells of RaiseFunds, and the Trader still asks the user whether a funding plan may be made, in Auto mode too (`require_user_approval`; Jacob to decide, entry 104). A recommended sale is no longer cut to the shares sellable today: a sale is the user's own order, placed whole or not at all.

## 76. `USE_LIVE_TRADING=false`: the desk works at the previous business day's close, so it can be run when the market is closed (2026-11-14)

**Problem.** The desk judges a recommendation at a moment: the market must be open, the recommendation must be for that trading day, a quote must be fresh, and an approval lasts until the window closes (entries 57 and 69). At night, at the weekend and on a holiday none of that can be true, so the first live run of the Trader could only reach a decision by passing `--today` and a past `--now` to the runner, and the desk, which has neither flag, could not be run at all. `IGNORE_MARKET_HOURS` (entry 69) turns off only the closed-market denial; the record date, the quote and the approval window still failed. Jacob added `USE_LIVE_TRADING=false` to `.env` to say: when this is off, use the market close of the previous business day, and simulate the market being open, for testing and development.

**Decision (`common/trading_clock.py`, `common/market_calendar.simulated_moment`, `configs/market_calendar.json`).**
- `USE_LIVE_TRADING` true, yes, on or 1, or not set: nothing changes (the real clock, Finnhub's live quote). Anything else, a typing mistake included, is the simulation: the safe side.
- With the simulation, every agent of the desk works at ONE moment: the previous business day (the trading day before today's Eastern date: Friday for a Saturday, a Sunday and a Monday, the day before a holiday for the day after it), `simulated_minutes_before_close` (30) before its regular close: 15:30 on a normal day, 12:30 on an early-close day. That is inside the regular session, so the market is "open", the recommendation is for that trading day, and an approval has time left. The moment does not advance during the day, so an approval never runs out in the middle of a run.
- `desk` (its default clock), the runner (when `--now` is not given: an explicit `--now` or `--today` is a replay on purpose and wins), and the defaults of `review_order`, `decide_trades` and `Trader.execute_trades` use `trading_now()`. Every report says `CLOCK    SIMULATED (USE_LIVE_TRADING=false): ...`.
- A price request (`DataClerk.request_quotes`) is answered from the stored daily bar of that day (`DataClerkTools.close_quote`: no Finnhub call, so it also costs nothing), as a quote marked `simulated: true`, fresh at the simulated moment. A symbol with no stored bar on that day fails like any failed quote; it is NOT answered with another day's price or the live quote.
- The Critic's check of the stored close against a second provider skips a simulated quote: it is the same bar, and comparing it with itself would pass for nothing (`recheck.cross_checks`). Every other check is unchanged.
- It is NOT a switch for real trading: there is none, and `PAPER_TRADING` (the Trader's rule T4) is a separate setting that stays on. `USE_LIVE_TRADING=true` means only "use the real clock and the live quote".
- `tests/conftest.py` pins it to true for every test, as it does `PAPER_TRADING` and `IGNORE_MARKET_HOURS`, so no test inherits Jacob's `.env`.

**Alternatives.** Take the simulated quote from Finnhub's `previous_close` (rejected: it still costs a call, fails when Finnhub is down, and `previous_close` is only the right day on a weekday morning); fix the clock to a date in a config file (rejected: it would go stale, and "the previous business day" is what Jacob asked for); use the latest completed close, so that after 16:00 today's close counts (rejected for now: Jacob said the previous business day, and the stored bars are refreshed after the close, not at it); `--today` and `--now` on the desk (rejected for now: the setting reaches every entry point at once).

**Consequences.** The desk and the runner work on a weekend with the stored bars (`./test_recommendations.sh PG --no-fetch --trade ...` with no `--today`). A run on a Tuesday uses Monday's close, so the stored bars must reach Monday (the Advisor's freshness gate says so when they do not). A backtest still waits for a stored bar from the session after the simulated moment, which is the day the simulation stands on or later, so it is `ACTIVE` until that bar exists. Paper fills are dated the simulated day, and a booked fill in the account book carries that date. The second-provider price check does not run while the clock is simulated, so a review then has one cross-check fewer (the symbol directory and Finnhub's 52-week high still run). Tests: `tests/test_trading_clock.py` (46).


## 77. A bearish event counts for a company only when the clause that shows it names that company (Critic v1 Amendment 5, 2026-11-14)

**Problem.** Both live reviews of PG on the simulated clock were DENIED with `fresh_negative_event`: a stored article says "Procter & Gamble declared its $1.0885 quarterly dividend in October, while PepsiCo cut its 2026 core EPS growth outlook". The Critic reads newer stored text itself (`bearish_classes`: the Advisor's event patterns, in a sentence that names the company) and the `guidance_cut` pattern matched "cut ... outlook"; the sentence names Procter & Gamble, so it counted. The cut is PepsiCo's. The model had labelled the article `none`: this was the code, not GLM, and it repeated in every run. It would block any purchase whenever an article puts another company's bad news beside the company's own name.

**Decision (`critic/evidence.py`).** `about_the_company`: when a sentence has more than one clause (cut at while, whereas, but, although, though and a semicolon), the clause that SHOWS the class must name the company; when no single clause shows it (the words of the pattern are spread over several), the sentence-level rule stands. `bearish_classes` now looks at EVERY sentence, not only the first that matches a class (a cut by another company in the first sentence hid this company's own cut in a later one), and uses the Advisor's own sentence splitter (this module has a simpler one of its own for the control claim: using it first split the article at "$1.", which made the first version of this fix pass a test for the wrong reason).

**Alternatives.** Let the model decide who the news is about (rejected: entries 24 and 26, and the model had it right; the code made the mistake); drop `guidance_cut` from the blocking classes (rejected: a real cut is the clearest reason not to buy); require the company's name within N characters of the trigger (rejected: arbitrary, and wrong for "Procter & Gamble, the maker of Tide, cut its outlook").

**Consequences.** An article that mixes two companies no longer blocks either one for the other's news, and a real cut still blocks (for PepsiCo the same sentence is a finding). A sentence joined by "and" is still read as one clause ("Procter & Gamble and PepsiCo cut their guidance" is about both). The Advisor's `verify_event` is unchanged: it checks a quote the model chose, normally one clause. Tests: `tests/test_critic_evidence.py` (five new; two fail with the rule off).


## 78. A user session end to end: the user's own words start the LangGraph desk, and the money is spread evenly (2026-11-14)

**Problem.** An end-to-end check needs a person, an account with money, and a request in plain words ("I want to buy oil stocks. Use as much of the money as you can on the 3 most recommended companies and split it evenly"), then the whole desk and a portfolio that changes. The desk took a symbol or a term, the runner that finds companies from a theme was procedural code and not the LangGraph workflow, nothing read "the top 3" or "split it evenly", and the tests of the desk were built on stand-in agents and invented prices.

**Decision.**
- `./test_prometheus.sh --session` (`application/session.py`) is a user session: "Create a new user? [Y/n]", then the user's fields (name, age, risk, money, Ask or Auto), then the main prompt "Tell me what you want to do?". It creates the account in the Postgres book, adds the money, starts the desk, and prints the status of the recommendations, what limited each purchase, the Trader's results and the portfolio before and after. Exit 0 when the portfolio changed (or it was only a look), 1 when it could not trade (the reasons are printed), 2 for a bad flag or an account book, key or request that cannot be used. A required answer with no input at all stops the script: it never creates a user on defaults nobody typed.
- The desk takes `request["ask"]` (`langgraph/desk.py`): the SAME graph. The Account Manager node reads the words (`AccountManager.understand_request`: the model fills in a form, `intent.parse_intent` accepts it only when every ticker, name, number and cue word is in what the user typed) and the user confirms what was understood; the Data Clerk node finds the companies of a theme, a list or tickers (`recommendations.find_companies_of`, shared with the runner) and refreshes their data with `--fetch`; the Advisor node keeps the top N; the Critic reviews; the decision node applies "split it evenly" and "spend this much" (`decisions.balance_evenly`); the Trader executes and books. Nothing in the code names a sector, a company or a ticker.
- `balance_evenly`: equal DOLLARS for the purchases that trade now, whole shares, never above what the Critic approved for one company; a company whose share buys less than one share is dropped from the split and the others are balanced again.
- A theme can find more listed companies than the Advisor scores (an "oil" request found 18, the paid refresh accepts 15): the first 15 are scored and the rest are named in a note (`recommendations.scored_symbols`).
- Tests of this change use REAL data and no stand-in agent: the real stored bars, the real capture of the first successful session (`tests/fixtures/session_real/bob_jones_oil.json`, made with `--save`), and `tests/test_end_to_end_live.py`, which runs the real agents and is opt-in (`PROMETHEUS_LIVE_TESTS=1`).

**What the first real sessions showed.** Bob Jones (60, conservative, Auto, $15,000, "oil"): the first run stopped at the Data Clerk (18 companies over the limit of 15: fixed, and the report now says why an agent did not run); the second found EOG, XOM and CVX but the Critic's approvals scored 76 and 77, under the Auto-trading mark of 80, so they waited for the user (`--auto-min-score 70`, the Critic's own approval mark, was used for the dev run); the third traded and booked EOG, XOM and CVX. It spent $3,782 of $15,000 (25%): the profile caps ONE stock at 10% of equity ($1,500) and the even split trims each purchase to the smallest approval, so Bob's request to "buy as much as he could" was bounded by Bob's own limits. The report now says so, with the flag that raises the limits for a session (`--max-position-pct`, `--max-exposure-pct`, `--max-loss-pct`).

**Alternatives.** Wrap the runner (rejected: it is not the LangGraph workflow); let the model's form stand without checking it against the words (rejected: entries 24 and 26); a rule for each sector or company (rejected: it would be the hardcoding Jacob ruled out); stand-in agents in the tests (rejected by Jacob: real data only).

**Consequences and what is open.** The Advisor only recommends companies; how much to buy is the Account Manager's: it should clarify with the user in Ask mode with ReAct questions (how much money, how to split it, whether to raise a limit). That is NOT built. Today the Account Manager's decision step takes the Critic's approved size and splits it evenly, and Advisor v1's trade plan still carries a `suggested_shares` that the Critic uses as an upper bound (`approved = min(suggested, the profile's limits)`); it was not what held Bob back, but with the limits raised it would. Moving the size out of the Advisor's plan is a change of the Advisor and Critic specs and of their tests, to be decided. The older tests of the project still use stand-in agents and invented data; replacing them is a separate task.


## 79. Ask mode: the user approves the order that will be placed, and no cash is reserved (2026-11-14)

**Problem.** `./test_prometheus.sh --session` in Ask mode (the account's own default) asked "Place this trade (9 shares of EOG, about $1,425)?" and then the even split cut it to 8 shares ($1,266.56): the user said yes to one order and the Trader got another. The user was asked one trade at a time with no view of the whole proposal, the session never fed its own input and output to the approver (a caller's `ask` was ignored), a session with nobody at the terminal printed nothing about why Ask mode had not traded, and the script's own prompt defaulted to Auto while every other default (the profile, the database column) is Ask. Separately, `decide_trades` reserved cash across purchases (a purchase waiting for the user held back the money of the next).

**Decision.**
- `decide_trades` sizes first and asks last: it makes a row for every review, splits the money evenly (when asked) over the purchases that trade on their own AND those that wait for the user (`balance_evenly(..., pending=True)`), and only then asks, one purchase at a time (`_ask_the_user`). Each card shows the FINAL shares and cost ("WOULD BE PLACED") and carries the whole proposal; `terminal_approver` prints the proposal once, before the first question, and numbers the questions ("Place this trade 1 of 2"). A "no" leaves the others at the size the user saw: nothing is split again after an answer, so what was approved is what the Trader gets. A purchase the split drops (`no_funds`) is not asked about; a denial of the Critic is still final.
- No cash is reserved (Jacob's rule): each purchase is checked against all the funds available in the portfolio, whatever the others cost. The Trader checks the cash again when it places each order (it fills the shares the cash pays for, or rejects `insufficient_cash`).
- The session makes its approver from its own `ask` and `out` (`session.approver_for`): with the real `input` it asks only when stdin is a terminal (a script nobody watches never trades in Ask mode); a given `ask` is taken to be a person. `--yes` does not approve trades. Right after the decision node the session prints what was decided and who said yes (`decision_lines`), whether anything goes on to the Trader, and, when nobody answered, why nothing traded and how to run it ("run it in a terminal, or use --mode auto").
- `./test_prometheus.sh --session` now defaults the trade mode to Ask, and its help says that Ask mode shows the proposed trades and asks Y/N about each.

**Alternatives.** One question for the whole order (rejected: the user may want one company and not another; per-trade answers with a fixed size give that); re-splitting after a "no" (rejected: the user would again approve one size and get another); reserving cash in the order of the questions (rejected by Jacob: any available funds can be used); asking before the split and showing a note (rejected: it is the mismatch itself).

**Consequences and what is open.** Ask mode never places a size the user did not see. Two purchases that together cost more than the cash can both be approved; the Trader then fills what the cash pays for or rejects the second. The sizes still come from the Critic's approved shares: moving the sizing to the Account Manager, with ReAct questions in Ask mode (a percentage, a dollar amount or a number of shares), is the next entry (`design/sizing_in_account_manager_spec.md`) and keeps this order: size, then ask with the final size.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (entry 104).** The Auto-mode rows of this entry (an approval under the automatic-trading mark, a finding that asks for a person, and a sale wait for the user: `escalated`) are gone: Auto mode asks nothing. Ask mode is as this entry says.

## 80. How much to buy is the Account Manager's: the Advisor and the Critic only deal with recommendations (2026-11-14)

**Problem.** The size of a trade was decided in three places and the user's money was in two agents that are meant to judge companies. Advisor v1's trade plan sized the trade (a risk budget of 1% of equity, 0.5% in a stress or unknown regime, `floor(budget / stop distance)` cut by a 20% position cap and a 5% share of the day's dollar volume, then `suggested_shares`, `oversized`, `position_pct`, `equity`, `funds_feasible`), so the Advisor read the user's cash and holdings; the Critic was given the portfolio and cut that size again (`size_caps`, `exposure_cap`, `approved_shares`, two blockers, a warning, and a `position_pct` measure in the risk score); the Account Manager then split what was left. Nothing could ask the user how much they wanted, and the Advisor's suggestion bounded what the Account Manager could do (entry 78: it was not what held Bob Jones back, but with his limits raised it would have). Jacob's rule of 2026-11-14: "The Advisor only generates recommendations. The Account Manager decides how much to buy." "The Advisor and Critic only deal with Recommendations." "A recommendation to REDUCE a holding has no amount either."

**Decision.**
- **The Advisor** says which companies, their stance and score, the evidence, the thesis and the PLAN of the trade: entry zone, stop, target, time stop, order style, invalidation (prices and dates). **The Critic** approves or denies each recommendation: accuracy, evidence, the risk of the COMPANY against the user's risk tolerance (age and horizon included), timing, value, chart. Neither says how much to buy or sell, and a `reduce` carries no amount. **The Account Manager** decides how much, from the user's money, the user's limits and what the user said (`account_manager/sizing.py`, `configs/account_manager_v1.json`, `decisions.decide_trades`, entries 79 and this one).
- **What moved where.** `critic/risk.size_caps` and `exposure_cap`, and `advisor/trade_plan`'s sizing and `funds_check`, became `account_manager/sizing.py` (`size_purchases`, `limit_caps`, `equity_of`, `funds_check`); the share of the day's dollar volume (5%) is `max_adv_pct` in `configs/account_manager_v1.json`; the user's own limits (position 10%, loss per trade 1%, new exposure 30%, order dollars) come from the profile (`UserProfile.sizing_limits()`). The Advisor's own `risk_pct`, `risk_pct_stress` and `max_position_pct` (20%) are gone: the profile's limits are the only ones.
- **The Advisor stops sizing.** `trade_plan.build(family, metrics, quote, today, config, symbol, reaction_atr)` has no equity, portfolio or regime argument; `equity_of`, `funds_check`, the risk budget, the caps and the fields `equity`, `equity_basis`, `risk_budget`, `suggested_shares`, `position_pct`, `oversized`, `size_note`, `funds_feasible`, `funds_note` of `TradePlan` and `position_pct_after`, `funds_feasible` of `PortfolioFit` are removed. Every other number of the plan is the same: 208 plans made by the old code on the real captured metrics are the golden file `tests/fixtures/advisor_real/plans_before_size_removal.json`, and the new code reproduces every one (`tests/test_trade_plan_real.py`). The regime stays in the record and in the families; with no budget it has no part in the plan. The thesis digest has no size line, the thesis prompt says the record has no amount to buy or sell, and the number check allows no number the record does not have. The runner's report prints no `size` line. `portfolio` stays an argument of `generate_recommendations` ONLY for the holdings (which symbols, their shares and cost basis); the Advisor never reads cash or equity: a structural test reads the whole Advisor package, its two prompt files and its settings and fails on `cash_avail`, `equity`, `suggested_shares`, `risk_budget`, `position_pct` and the other removed names, on any read of the portfolio but `.positions`, and on an import of the profile, the Account Manager or the account book (`tests/test_advisor_no_size.py`).
- **The one rule of the twelve steps that needed a size is the holdings pass's `add`** (step 9, `ranking.holding_action`). It asked that the plan was not `oversized` and that the position after adding stayed within 20% of equity. It now asks only that the stance is bullish and the blend is as good as this run's new ideas. `ranking.add_max_position_pct` and `ranking.oversized_pct` left `configs/advisor_v1.json`. Whether the user's limits leave room to add is the sizing's: the shares already held count against the position limit, and an `add` that no limit leaves room for becomes `no_funds` with the reason. Nothing else needed a size: the diversity rule and the sleeves rank by blend and sector, and `ranking.concentrated` is a flag. On the real SNDK capture with the model saying nothing, the held GM is now an `add` where it was a `hold`.
- **The Critic stops sizing and stops receiving the portfolio**: `Critic.review_recommendations(order, risk_view, now)`. `RiskView` (frozen, `extra="forbid"`) cannot hold a size limit (`max_position_pct`, `max_loss_per_trade_pct`, `max_new_exposure_pct`, `max_order_dollars`), so the Critic cannot learn the user's money: a structural guarantee, not a habit. The review loses `approved_shares`, `suggested_shares`, the caps, `allowed_shares`, `equity` and `equity_basis` of its risk detail, the blockers `size_above_profile` and `exposure_limit` (`BLOCKER_CODES` 29 to 27), the warning `size_reduced` and the `position_pct` measure (weight 10). The other eight weights were scaled by 100/90, one decimal, with the rounding fixed so that they add up to exactly 100: `vol_63d_ann` 27.8, `max_drawdown_63d` 16.7, `beta_63d` 11.1, `atr_pct` 11.1, `stop_loss_pct` 11.1, `market_cap` 11.1, `adv_20d_dollars` 5.6, `flags` 5.5. The scale of the risk score (0 to 100) and its `levels` (20, 45, 70) are kept; scores shift: PG_open's risk is 5.2 (it was 7.1 at the 44 shares the old Critic approved, 10.6 at the Advisor's 75) and SNDK's 77.8 (it was 70.0). `work_order.verify_trade` no longer compares a decision with a Critic size.
- **No cash is reserved** (Jacob: "do not reserve any cash"): the `money` cap of every purchase is all the available cash, whatever the others cost; the Trader checks the cash again when it places each order.
- **Auto mode spends the funds round-robin when the user asked for no split** (Jacob: "take the available funds and use round-robin. Buy a share of first stock, buy a share of the next stock, and keep buying shares until all the desired (requested) funds to use are used up"): a share of each approved stock in rank order, again and again, until the requested funds or the stocks' limits are used; each stock ends with ONE final share count and the Trader places one trade per stock. "Split evenly" in the user's words still gives equal dollars (`sizing.size_purchases(..., allocation="even")`; `decisions.balance_evenly` is deleted). Ask mode with no split asked shows the largest order the limits allow, in rank order. The real Bob Jones capture ($15,000, conservative, XOM and EOG approved, APA denied for risk) gets: round-robin XOM 8 and EOG 9, both stopped by the 10% position limit, $2,789.60 spent of $15,000; even 8 and 8; Ask with no split 8 and 9; a $400 budget gives round-robin 1 and 1 against 2 and 0 for the largest-order default.
- **The `allocation` of the request form**: `Literal["even", "advisor"]` named "no split asked" after the Advisor, which sized then. It is now `"default"` (no split asked: the Account Manager's own way for the mode, round-robin in Auto and what the limits allow in Ask) in the model (`intent.TradeIntent`), in the form and the prompt shown to the model (`prompts/account_manager_request.py`), in the tests and in the docs. Behaviour is the same: only the name changed, and the old word `advisor` is now refused as an unknown value.

**Alternatives.** Keep the Advisor's size as an upper bound for the Account Manager (rejected: the money would still be in the Advisor, and the bound is what Bob's raised limits would have run into). Keep a Critic cap (rejected: the Critic must not know the user's money, and one cap in the Critic and another in the Account Manager is two sources of truth). Leave the portfolio argument on the Critic unused (rejected: a convention can be broken, a signature cannot). Leave the size fields on the plan as null "for compatibility" (rejected, as in entry 68: unused numbers invite use). Bump the Advisor's schema version (rejected: see below). Let a model do the sizing (rejected: it is arithmetic, entries 24 and 26). Reserve cash across purchases (rejected by Jacob).

**Consequences.**
- **The profile's limits, not the pot, are what stops a conservative profile from spending more.** A user who asks for "as much as I can" with $15,000 and the default limits (10% of the account in one stock) buys about $2,800: each stock stops at its limit. The session report names the limit that stopped each purchase and the flag that raises it for the session (`--max-position-pct`, `--max-exposure-pct`, `--max-loss-pct`).
- **Sizing cash is the available cash, so RaiseFunds no longer changes a decision.** The old Advisor sized for the whole account and flagged `funds_feasible` when selling holdings in profit could pay for the purchase; the Account Manager now sizes by `cash_avail`, so a purchase the cash cannot pay for is sized to 0. Jacob's sample ($51.23 in cash, three holdings) sizes PG to 0 and is `no_funds`, though `sizing.funds_check` says holdings in profit could raise the money. Whether the sizing cash should include what RaiseFunds could raise (selling holdings in profit to pay for a purchase) is an open question, and it is Jacob's to decide: `size_purchases` already takes the cash as an argument ("the free cash, or more when the caller has the money raised"), so it is a choice of what the desk passes, not a rewrite.
- **Records keep their schema versions with the fields gone** (the precedent is entry 68, Advisor v1 Amendment 7, which took the expected return out of the record and left the version): the Advisor's record stays `schema_version` 2 and the Critic's review record stays 1; the models forbid unknown fields, so a field that goes is simply gone. Older lines of `data/ledger/recommendations.jsonl` and `data/ledger/reviews.jsonl` still hold sizes and nothing reads them back (the cooldown reads the trade and the time stop by key). One difference from entry 68: the Critic validates each record against the Advisor's model, so an OLD saved work order whose recommendations still hold a size would be called a `record_defect` if it were reviewed again (`tests/test_critic.py` pins it). The one real capture that held them, `tests/fixtures/session_real/bob_jones_oil.json`, was migrated once by stripping exactly the 33 removed fields of its three records (nothing else changed; it validates as a record now); `tests/fixtures/advisor_real/{SNDK,PG,PG_open}.json` are the Advisor's INPUTS and were not touched.
- **Tests.** Every test that asserted a size of the Advisor or the Critic was changed to the new rule, and the arithmetic of each cap is tested on the Account Manager's code (`tests/test_account_manager_sizing.py`). Tests were deleted only when what they proved no longer exists: the five of `tests/test_trade_plan.py` about the risk budget, the position and liquidity caps, the stress halving, `equity_of` and `funds_check` (covered there by the sizing tests), the report test of the unknown position size, and the `recompute.position` tamper case of the simple Critic (its check is gone). New real-data tests: the plans against the golden file, the structural test, the same records whatever the cash, a real `reduce` with no amount, the real model's thesis passing the checks on a record with no size.
- **BLOCKER_CODES is 27**, the Critic's review has no size, and the older specs' text on sizing is superseded by an amendment at the end of `design/advisor_v1_final_spec.md` and `design/critic_v1_final_spec.md` (the old text stays).
- **Not done.** The Account Manager's ReAct questions in Ask mode (how much: a percentage, dollars or shares; whether to raise a limit; specified in `design/sizing_in_account_manager_spec.md`, phase 2), and the per-trade Trader handshake (entry 81, `design/trader_handshake_spec.md`).

**Amended 2026-11-15 (entry 105).** Step 9's holdings pass and its `add` are gone with the Advisor's portfolio: the Advisor writes no `add`, `hold` or `reduce`, and the words "the holdings pass's `add`" above describe the code of that day. The Account Manager's sizing is unchanged (it sizes purchases; a direct order's `add` and `reduce` are its own decisions).

## 81. The Trader and the Account Manager shake hands on every trade, and the Trader holds no cash (2026-11-14)

**Problem.** The Trader kept its OWN copy of the account. `PaperBroker.portfolio` was updated with `apply_fill` as the broker filled trades, `affordable_shares(self.portfolio.cash_avail, ...)` decided how many shares the cash paid for, and the shares a sale could sell came from the copy too. Only at the END of the run did it tell the Account Manager, once, in one call (`notify_trades`, step 8), and with a finding of the output guard nothing was booked at all. Between the first fill and that call the Postgres book did not know about any trade, two owners held the funds (the Trader's copy and the book), and a copy that disagreed with the book was only noticed after the trades were placed. Jacob's decision (2026-11-14): *"The trader agent will get the list of stocks to trade as part of the work order.
