It will place one trade at a time, then update the accountmanager the status when the order goes thru. So the accountmanager can update the avail funds (subtract the spent amount) and update the portfolio. Then, pass the trader the approval to make the next trade. This handshaking is between each trade so the Trader can not overdrawn the available funds."*, *"The trader does not have its own cash copy. It uses the Postgres database with the AccountManager for ACID properties and data integrity."*, *"Do not reserve any cash. Any available funds in the portfolio can be used."*

**Decision.** The Account Manager and its Postgres book are the ONLY owner of the funds and the positions. The Trader holds no cash and no portfolio: not a copy, not a running total (a test reads the source of every Trader module and fails on `apply_fill`, `affordable_shares`, a `Portfolio` built or any arithmetic on `cash_avail`).
- **Two calls, for every trade, one trade at a time** (`agents/account_manager/handshake.py`, `AccountManager.approve_trade` and `record_fill`). `approve_trade(trade, price, order=, now=, on=, commission=)` returns an `Approval` (`approved`, `shares`, `cash_avail`, `code`, `reason`): the Account Manager reads the funds and holdings AS THEY ARE NOW (the book through `list_portfolio` when the run books; its own working portfolio otherwise) and approves a purchase for the whole shares the cash pays for at that price (the account book's rounding, the Trader's commission setting), a sale for the shares held and sellable under T+1, never more than the decision's; it says no (`insufficient_cash`, `not_held`, `not_sellable_today`, `not_permitted`, `approval_expired`, `account_refused`) when nothing can be traded, when `work_order.verify_trade` no longer permits it, when the account is not active or when it cannot be read. `record_fill(trade)` returns a `Booking` (`status`: `booked`, `applied`, `refused`, `unconfirmed`; `cash_avail`; `reason`; `note`): it applies the fill and, with a saved account and `book`, books it in ONE Postgres transaction (`record_trade`: the account row locked `FOR UPDATE`, the status, the cash and the shares checked, the cash and the position moved together or neither), then reads the cash back from the book. Neither call raises for a data or database failure, and a failure names the kind of the error only.
- **Inside the one funnel** (`PaperBroker._fill`, which every trade passes whoever places it, so no path can skip it): the price rules give the fill price, then `approve_trade`, the fill for at most the shares approved, the single-trade output check (below), and `record_fill`, which returns before the next trade starts. A refused approval is a `rejected` trade with the Account Manager's code. A fill the book refuses is `rejected` with `account_refused`, the book's own sentence as its reason, and no `Fill`. A booking the book could not confirm (a database failure) is the same and the run stops placing further trades, because it is never assumed that the trade was booked; the order is then not marked `traded`. The model's four tools keep their names and still cannot name a symbol, a side or a number of shares; `place_order`'s answer adds two things code made, `account` (one word of a closed list: `booked`, `applied`, `refused`, `unconfirmed`) and `cash_left` (the Account Manager's figure), and a refusal reaches the model as a reject code, never as the book's sentence (rules T8 and T9). `notify_account_manager` keeps its name and its place in the loop, but it tells the Account Manager nothing new (every fill was told as it happened): it CLOSES the run (nothing more is placed), the whole-run guard reports, and the account is read back from the Account Manager for the cash figures it returns.
- **Funding.** Each funding sale is a trade of its own through the same steps, before its purchase. The pre-check that nothing is sold when the sales cannot pay for one share stays, but the Trader no longer works the cash out: it asks the Account Manager a QUESTION (`preview_trade`: what would you approve with the estimated proceeds as cash? nothing is reserved), and the same question, with no proceeds, decides whether the user is asked for the sales at all. The purchase is approved for real only after the sales are recorded, so it meets the cash the book then shows. The user's consent for the sales stays.
- **Amendment 1 of the spec: the unbooked fills of an earlier run move to the Account Manager.** The fills of an earlier run live only on `order.trades` when nothing was booked (a backtest, a desk JSON portfolio, a saved account without `--book`). `AccountManager.apply_earlier_fills(trades)` applies them to its working portfolio (sales first, `apply_fill`, nothing booked) before the first approval of a run, so a second run of the same order starts from the cash the first one spent even when the caller hands it the same fresh portfolio, and a `filled` trade stays final (rule T3). With a run that books they are not applied (the book has what it was told): a fill an earlier run left unbooked is named as a problem.
- **Amendment 2: the output guard is per trade.** `work_order.verify_fill(order, trade, others)` is the single-trade part of `verify_trades` (its shape, the decision agrees on record, symbol and side, the shares are not above the decision's, one filled trade per recommendation, a funding trade's `funds_for`), run on each fill BEFORE it is reported. A finding means the fill is neither reported nor booked: the trade is `rejected` with the existing code `internal_error` (the Trader's own result disagrees with the decisions, which is what that code is for: no new code was needed), the finding is on the run, and nothing more is placed. The end-of-run `verify_trades` stays as a whole-run REPORT: it also names a filled funding sale whose purchase is not filled, and what was booked stays booked.
- **Where the arithmetic went.** `affordable_shares` moved from the Trader's `simulator.py` to `common/portfolio.py` beside `trade_amount`, with its test; the Account Manager is its only caller. `account_refused` was already in the Trader's closed list of twelve reject codes (the old end-of-run booking used it); `insufficient_cash` stays. The Trader's doors to the Account Manager are now `request_prices`, `request_bars`, `trading_portfolio(portfolio, book)`, `apply_earlier_fills`, `current_portfolio`, `approve_trade`, `preview_trade` and `record_fill` (a test lists them and fails on any other, `notify_trades` and the book included); `notify_trades` stays for a caller that reports a list of fills to a portfolio it holds.
- **No cash is reserved.** The first trades use the funds and a later trade gets what is left. Backtest never books (`book` is refused in backtest mode, as before).
- **The handshake never waits for a next trade** (Jacob, same day: *"The handshaking should not wait if there are no further trades to execute"*). An approval is asked only for a trade that is ABOUT TO BE PLACED (the price rules fill it now), and a booking only for a fill that was approved; a trade refused at its approval, or rejected before it fills (no price, a price outside the limits, a limit not reached, an approval that ran out), or resting (a limit the quote has not reached, a backtest with no bar stored yet), is never put to the Account Manager for a booking, moves nothing and waits for nothing. After the LAST trade's `record_fill` is answered the run goes straight on to its end: the whole-run report, the account read back once for `portfolio_after`, the records. There is no further `approve_trade`, no unanswered approval, no poll, no sleep, no timeout, no wait of any kind for a "next" trade (every call is a plain call that returns). With NO ticket to place (nothing permitted, everything final from an earlier run, every ticket refused before placement) there is no handshake at all: no `approve_trade`, no `record_fill`, and the book is read twice, once for `portfolio_before` and once for `portfolio_after` (the step that used to re-read the account after the Account Manager applied the fills of an earlier run now does so only when there is such a fill). The one place a person is waited for is not the handshake: the user's yes for funding sales, asked BEFORE the first trade. The rule is in the docstrings of `handshake.py` and of the broker's funnel, and tests pin it (below).

**Alternatives.**
- *A cycle in the LangGraph desk* (`trader -> account_manager_settle -> trader -> ...`, once per trade): rejected for now. The desk's graph already hands the order to the Trader node, and the Trader has its own LangGraph for the model's loop; the handshake sits inside the Trader's single placement funnel, so the model's tools, the sweep and the funding all pass through it and no path can skip it, and a graph-level cycle would call the model once per trade.
- *Keep the Trader's copy and tell the Account Manager at the end* (what it was): rejected by Jacob: two owners of the funds.
- *Reserve each purchase's money before any trade*: rejected by Jacob: any available funds can be used.
- *Check the cash only when the fill is booked* (let the book refuse what the cash cannot pay for, with no approval): rejected. The book would still refuse an overdraw, but a purchase would then fail whole instead of being cut to the shares the cash pays for (the existing `cash_policy: "reduce"`), and a funded purchase could not find out before the sales that they would not pay for one share.
- *Keep `notify_trades` as the Trader's one end-of-run call and add the approval only*: rejected: between the first fill and that call the book would still not know of any trade, and with a finding of the guard nothing would be booked.

**Consequences.**
- **A purchase can no longer overdraw the funds, and two of them cannot race.** Run on the real local Postgres on throwaway accounts with the real order of the real session (XOM and EOG, 8 shares each, the stored closes of 2026-11-13 through the real Data Clerk): with $2,000 the first purchase is booked (8 XOM, $1,365.40) and the second gets the shares the REMAINING cash pays for (4 EOG, $633.60: "cut from 8 to 4 shares for the cash"), the cash never below zero; with $1,400 the second is `rejected insufficient_cash` and the book keeps $34.60. Two threads, each its own `AccountManager` on the same account with $1,500, both approved for the full 8 shares and booking at the same moment (a barrier): the book accepts one and refuses the other ("cash $134.60 does not cover $1,365.40"), and the cash is never below zero; the same through two whole Traders. A rival that spends $1,700 between the approval and the booking makes the book refuse the Trader's fill: the trade is `account_refused` with no Fill, and the NEXT approval is asked only afterwards and meets the $300 the book really has (1 share of EOG). A lost connection (a real `psycopg.OperationalError` in place of the first `record_trade`) is `account_refused` with the kind of the error and no connection detail anywhere, no further trade is approved or placed, and the book is unchanged. After a run the book and the run's `portfolio_after` agree to the cent.
- **The "does not wait" rule, on the real Postgres and the real order** (`tests/test_trader_handshake.py`, with `time.sleep` patched to fail the test): two purchases that fill are exactly 2 approvals and 2 bookings, and the reads of the real store are, in order, the opening read, each approval's two reads, each booking's transaction and read-back, and the closing read, nothing else, nothing after the last answer; one purchase is one approval and one booking; a second purchase the cash cannot pay for is asked once, refused, has no booking, and the run ends right after the refusal; with nothing permitted, with everything already final, with every ticket expired, with no price (the simulated Saturday has no stored bar) and with a resting backtest order, there are zero approvals and zero bookings, and (on the book) exactly two reads. A test reads the source of `broker.py`, `execute.py`, `trader.py`, `handshake.py` and the Account Manager's handshake methods and fails on an import of a module that waits (`time`, `threading`, `asyncio`, `queue` ...), a name that waits (`sleep`, `wait`, `poll`, an Event, a Lock, a Thread ...) or a `while` loop.
- **The order of the calls is recorded from the real calls**: for each trade `place`, `approve`, `record`, the next trade only after; with funding the sale is recorded before the purchase is approved. The approval needs the price the fill will have, so the fill rules (`_simulate`) run first and the fill takes effect only after the approval: "place" in the spec's sequence is the fill taking effect.
- **What the model sees changed a little**: `place_order` says what the Account Manager did and the cash left, the system prompt says the Account Manager owns the money, the tool descriptions say what `notify_account_manager` now is. The summary check accepts the cash figures the model was told after each fill. Temperature 0 still does not make the model truthful; the tools still stop a made-up trade.
- **The Trader's `Fill` and the book can no longer disagree**: there is no portfolio on each side to compare. The test that compared the two (`a_portfolio_from_the_account_manager_that_is_not_the_one_the_fills_lead_to...`) was deleted for that reason; the Trader's `run.portfolio_after` is what the Account Manager reports when the run is closed (None, and a message, when it cannot be read: nothing is claimed about the account).
- **Two small differences to know about.** The funding pre-check counts each sale at the size `funding_legs` validated on the Account Manager's snapshot (the old broker cut it to the shares it believed sellable): a sale that the Account Manager then cuts or refuses at its own approval is `funding_failed`, and the purchase is approved against the cash the book really has, so no overdraw is possible, but the worst case (holdings sold and nothing bought) is reported by the whole-run guard rather than prevented in the one case where a sale changes between the snapshot and its placing. And an unexpected bug that stops a run after some trades filled still writes the trades that filled onto the order, because each fill was booked as it happened.
- **RaiseFunds is still open, and it is Jacob's to decide** (as in entry 80): the sizing cash is the available cash, so a decision never needs a funding sale today; the funding path of the Trader (sales placed with their purchase, the user's yes) keeps working as built and now goes through the same handshake. What the run showed with the real sample (cash $51.23): the purchase of 8 XOM funded by selling 4 MSFT at the stored close (about $2,094) works end to end, in the order preview, sale approved and recorded, purchase approved against the cash after the sale; a sale that could not pay for one share sells nothing.
- **Tests.** Real data throughout the new tests (`tests/handshake_helpers.py`, `test_account_manager_handshake.py`, `test_trader_handshake.py`); the older Trader tests keep their helpers and doubles and assert the new protocol. Their `make_broker` now builds a real Account Manager; `FakeStore` and `BookStore` gained the account's `status` (`get_user`), as the real store has. Tests deleted: `test_a_portfolio_from_the_account_manager_that_is_not_the_one_the_fills_lead_to_is_an_error_on_the_order` (the Trader has no portfolio to compare) and `test_an_account_manager_that_cannot_be_told_leaves_the_fills_on_the_order_and_a_message` (the Trader no longer makes a call that can fail at the end; replaced by tests of an Account Manager that raises instead of approving or recording); one test moved (`affordable_shares`, to `tests/test_portfolio.py`). A live session test (`tests/test_end_to_end_live.py`) was not run: it is paid and opt-in.

## 82. A trade is booked under row-level locks, read before and after the commit, and a trade that fails is not retried and does not stop the run (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 81 made the Account Manager and the Postgres book the only owner of the funds, and the Trader and the Account Manager shake hands on every trade. Three things were still loose. (1) The Account Manager booked a fill in one transaction and read the cash back, but nothing confirmed that the book then showed the trade, and the transaction locked only the account row and the position row of the symbol being traded (a new holding has no row to lock). (2) A fill that the book could not confirm, or whose output check failed, STOPPED the run: every later trade was rejected `not_attempted` and the order stayed `decided`, even though the earlier trades were final. (3) The user got the explanation only in the report at the end of the run.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).** In his words: *"the Trader Agents reports to the AccountManager the trade order completed, then the AccountManager updates the portfolio with the new avail funds and the new stock position. Before this update, the AccountManager should select the current portfolio state and then once this gets committed, the AccountManager should double check ... by re-selecting to verify the totals are different and the order was confirmed applied to the User's portfolio and done. Then, the AccountManager notifies the Trader agent that the Trader Agent can proceed to order the next trade on its list. ... The AccountManager at this point should mark that Trade as not done / failed and display the error to the user and do not retry a failed trade. But just allow the Trader agent to try to place the next trade"*; *"The AccountManager is the only Agent which the user interacts with"*; *"there should be a database row-level lock added to the User's portfolio also when the updating happens to help ensure the commit - use a transaction"*.

- **Three reads around one commit** (`AccountManager._book_fill`, which `record_fill` calls): (1) SELECT the account as it is now (`list_portfolio`); (2) COMMIT the trade, `AccountStore.book_trade`, ONE transaction; (3) SELECT the account AGAIN on a new connection after the commit and read the trade row back (`AccountStore.read_booked`). The booking is `booked` and `verified` only when the trade row is there as it was placed and the cash AND the shares of the symbol are different from step 1 (`handshake.check_booked`). The answer (`Booking`) carries `cash_before`, `cash_avail` and `verified`. Only after that answer does the Trader ask for its next trade.
- **Row-level locks on the user's portfolio, inside the transaction.** `book_trade` takes `FOR UPDATE` on the account row (the cash) and on EVERY position row of the account, in symbol order (so two bookings cannot deadlock), and holds them until the commit. It sets `lock_timeout` for the transaction (`BOOKING_LOCK_SECONDS`, 10): a lock that cannot be had in time fails the booking with nothing committed, so a stuck lock cannot hang a run. This is stronger than entry 81, which locked the account row and one position row.
- **A check before the commit.** Before the transaction commits it reads the account again inside itself: the cash must be the cash before less the cost (or plus the proceeds), the shares of the symbol the shares before plus (or less) the trade, EVERY OTHER holding unchanged, and the trade row there. Anything else raises `BookingCheckError` and the whole transaction rolls back: a failed trade is not committed to Postgres.
- **A failed trade is not done, is shown, and is never retried.** `Booking.status` is `refused` when nothing was committed (the book said no with its own sentence; a rollback; or a failed connection after which the account, read again, is unchanged) and `unconfirmed` when nobody can settle it from here (the account cannot be read again, or it differs from before, or the second read does not show a trade the book said it committed). Either way the Trader marks the trade `rejected` `account_refused` (the closed list of twelve codes is unchanged), does not retry it, and places the NEXT trade. The Account Manager shows the user the error, and the account as it stands, at that moment (`tell_user`, `show_portfolio`, through its `screen`, which the session sets to its own `out`); no connection detail is ever in the sentence (the kind of the error only).
- **No halting.** `PaperBroker.halted` is gone, and with it the rule that an unconfirmed booking or an output-check finding stops placing trades (entry 81 and amendment 2 of `design/trader_handshake_spec.md` said so; they are not rewritten, this entry supersedes that part). The next trade is safe because its approval reads the account as the book has it, so a failure cannot overdraw the funds. For a funded purchase a funding sale that fails is failed and the others go on, and the purchase is approved against the cash the book then shows (it may be cut, or refused `insufficient_cash`).
- **The order's status.** A run in which some trades failed is `traded`: every permitted trade is final (`filled` or `rejected` with its code and reason), as it was already for a trade rejected `insufficient_cash`. There is no halted state, so the question of what to call one does not arise.
- **The Account Manager is the user's only voice.** It has a `screen` (a callable that takes one line; None says nothing), `tell_user` and `show_portfolio`; the portfolio listing is the one `format_account` prints (`holding_lines`, no change to its output). The Trader still reaches the world only through the Account Manager.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep halting on an unconfirmed booking: it left later trades unattempted for a failure that is now settled by reading the account again, and Jacob asked for the next trade to go on. (b) A new order status such as `halted` or `partly_traded`: not needed once nothing halts. (c) Only the SELECT after the commit, with no check inside the transaction: it could not keep a wrong booking from committing; the check inside the transaction does. (d) Lock only the traded symbol's row, as before: a new holding has no row, and the other holdings would not be protected. (e) A callable `notice` on the Account Manager separate from its other user-facing methods: the Account Manager is the user's one voice, so it got a `screen` and display methods next to `format_account`, not a second channel.

**Consequences.** (1) One case cannot be undone from here: the book committed, and the second read does not show it. It is reported as `unconfirmed`, shown to the user ("the account book has to be checked") and not retried; the next approval reads the true account. A second read that FAILS after a good commit leaves the trade `booked` (not `verified`) with a note, because it is committed. (2) The Trader's own output check (a fill that disagrees with the decisions) fails that trade and is shown in the run's notes at the end of the run, not at the moment: the Trader has no way to the user except the Account Manager, and no new door was added for this case. (3) Each booking costs two more reads (the SELECT before and the SELECT after, plus the trade row). (4) Tests: `tests/test_account_booking_real.py` (10 tests on the real Postgres on throwaway accounts: the three reads in order; the lock on the account row and on a holding that is not the one traded, with a mutation check that the second fails without the lock; a waiting booking going ahead when the lock is released; two bookings at once one after the other; the in-transaction check rolling back for a position not written and for another holding changed; the user shown the error and the account; no retry). `tests/test_trader_handshake.py`: the lost-connection test is now "failed, shown, not retried, and T2 is booked" (and the spy counts the new reads). Five older tests that asserted the stop were rewritten for the new rule (`test_trader.py` twice, `test_trader_tools.py`, and the booking tests of `test_account_manager_trades.py`), and its `FakeStore` gained `book_trade` and `read_booked` and now moves its portfolio as the real book does. A real session capture with the new flow was not re-made (paid).

## 83. A news article that fails is discarded completely: it is never stored, found or scored (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** One bad record stopped everything. The RAG loader (`rag/loaders.py`) raised a `ValueError` at the first record "the DataToolbox would not have written" (a header line without a colon, an unknown marker, no `<<<END>>>`, bad UTF-8, a missing field, a time that is not a time), so the whole file was refused: every article after the bad one never reached the RAG, the news score (40% of the blend, news and press releases) or the Advisor. The toolbox was no better on the way in: one item from Finnhub or Polygon with a time that is not a time raised and aborted the whole fetch, and one corrupt line of the market-news store aborted `discover` and `search`.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).** *"If a news article fails (cannot be processed due to formatting etc), then skip and discard it (ignore it completely) from the RAG and returned scoring and other results to the Advisor agent. Do not use failed news articles."*

- **Loaders.** `load_news(path, skipped=None)` and `load_press_releases(path, skipped=None)` SKIP a record that fails and go on with the next one: the scanner reads a broken record to its end (or to the next start marker), discards it and picks up again, so the records around it load exactly as before. A record that ends and is then followed by text that is not a record (an unescaped `<<<END>>>` inside its text) was cut short, so it is discarded too: an article is never half-loaded. Each discarded record is a `Skipped(where, reason)`, listed in `skipped` when the caller gives one; nothing else is told. A failed record becomes no `Document`, so it is never chunked, stored, searched, cited or scored. Press releases are included because the news score counts them with the articles. SEC filings are not news and keep the strict rule (a filing the toolbox would not have written stops the load with a `ValueError`).
- **Ingest.** `ingest_symbol` and `ingest_press_releases` return the `skipped` list, and the command prints how many were discarded and where. A chunk stored before its record broke is not deleted (the same article may be sitting valid in another symbol's file): `--rebuild` clears it.
- **Toolbox.** An item the API gave that cannot be made into an article (`ARTICLE_FAILURES`: a time that is not a time, out of range, or of the wrong kind; a field of the wrong kind) is discarded in `fetch_news` (Finnhub, Polygon, the market feed) and `fetch_press_releases`, counted in the stats as `failed`, never written; a line of the market-news store that is not a whole article is ignored by `discover`, `search` and the seen-URL scan (`_market_articles`).
- **The score is untouched.** A failed article never reaches `advisor/blend.py`, so the rule there (a source whose date cannot be read counts as one half-life old) is not used for it; it stays for what it was written for.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep failing loudly: one bad article blocks all the others, which is what Jacob asked to stop. (b) Count a failed article with a low weight, as the blend does for an unreadable date: Jacob said not to use it at all. (c) Quarantine the failed records in a second file: not asked, and another thing to look after; the `skipped` list and the ingest line are enough for a person to find them. (d) Make filings lenient too: not asked; a filing is a legal document with many sections and a partial filing is worse than none. (e) Delete stored chunks of an article whose record now fails: its id may belong to a valid copy in another file.

**Consequences.** (1) A failure is quiet on purpose: it is in the ingest output, in `Skipped` and in the fetch stats, not an error; a person has to look at them. (2) No real record is lost: all 6,709 real stored news and press release documents load with none discarded (a test reads every real file). (3) Tests: failed records built by damaging REAL records of the fixtures (`tests/test_rag_loaders.py`, `test_press_releases.py`, `test_rag_ingest.py`: the real articles around a damaged one still load, it is not in the store and no search finds it), and `tests/test_failed_articles.py` (the API answers rebuilt from the real records with malformed items added; real market-news lines damaged in seven ways; with the discard switched off six of its seven tests fail). The tests that asserted the old stop were rewritten for the new rule, and the filings' stay as they were.

## 84. RaiseFunds does not enlarge the sizing cash, a funding sale is the minimum, and paid runs are part of the testing (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entries 80 to 82 left two questions to Jacob, and he answered them.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).**
- *Should the sizing cash include what RaiseFunds could raise?* **No.** *"Only sell the least amount of shares needed to raise enough new funds to purchase. We do not want to fully liquidate the portfolio. We only want to sell the minimum amount of shares to buy the new order."* The sizing cash stays the available cash (nothing is added to it, and no cash is reserved). Funding sales stay what they were built to be: RaiseFunds' defaults are `lean` (no sale of more shares than the purchase needs) and `never_liquidate` (at least one share of every holding is kept), and the Trader places those sales with their purchase, only with the user's yes. So a purchase the cash cannot pay for is sized to the cash. Nothing was changed in the code for this.
- *A fresh real session capture?* **Yes, and paid runs are part of the testing, preferred.** *"Yes, you can use paid runs as part of the test. This is preferred."* The live tests and a real session capture may be run without asking each time. Fetches that write the stored data files (news, press releases, bars, day aggregates, filings) are not part of that yes and still need one.

**Consequences.** The "open" notes of entries 80 and 81, `design/trader_handshake_spec.md` and the week 6 design are closed by this entry (the numbered entries are not rewritten). `AGENTS.md` records the standing yes.

## 85. The Account Manager asks the user how much to buy, in Ask mode, with a bounded ReAct loop (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 80 moved sizing to the Account Manager and left Phase 2 of its spec unbuilt: in Ask mode the user was shown a size they had never been asked about (the largest the limits allow, or an even split or budget if the request happened to say so), and could only say yes or no to each trade. Jacob's words (2026-11-14): *"The Account Manager agent can use ReAct to clarify with the user, in Ask mode, how much to buy"*, and the user may answer *"what percentage, dollar amount to buy or number of shares"*.

**Decision.** (`agents/account_manager/clarify.py`, `AccountManager.clarify_sizing`, `prompts/account_manager_sizing.py`, thresholds in `configs/account_manager_v1.json` section `clarify`; spec `design/sizing_in_account_manager_spec.md`, Phase 2.)
- **When it asks.** Ask mode only; an approved, unexpired purchase is waiting to be sized; the request did not already say how much (`TradeIntent.said_how_much()`: a code check of the user's own words for a percentage, a dollar amount, a number of shares or "as much as I can", and not a budget the model read); and somebody is at the keyboard (`channel.available()`: with nobody there the loop is not run and no model call is made). Auto mode never asks. The questions come BEFORE anything is sized or sent to the Trader; the user then approves each trade as before (entry 79).
- **The loop.** The Account Manager's own model (`account_manager` role, gpt-oss-120b on OpenRouter): THOUGHT, then an ACTION `ask_user(question)` or `set_sizing(unit, amount, scope, allocation)`, then the OBSERVATION (the user's answer between `<answer>` tags, or a refusal between `<problem>` tags). At most three questions, and at most two refused replies, then it gives up. The model only words the question and reads the answer into the form; it is shown numbers made by code (the cash, the equity, each approved purchase with its price and what the limits allow, the user's request) and nothing from the news, no thesis and no score.
- **Code checks and calculates everything** (`check_choice`, `plan_choice`). The amount must be a number the user TYPED in an answer; the unit must be one their words show (`%`/percent, "shares", `$`/dollars, or a bare number when they gave no other unit); a percentage is above 0 and at most 100; shares are whole; what is asked must not cost more than the cash; a split (`even`, `round_robin`) must be one the user asked for (or the request did); "as much as I can" must be in their words. A form that fails is refused with the reason and the model asks again. The whole shares are worked out in code (percent x equity / price, dollars / price, the shares as given; a total in shares is shared out in rank order), then the user's limits cut them.
- **The amount asked for is one more limit of the purchase** (`sizing.size_purchases(..., wanted={record_id: shares})`, `Sized.asked`, binding `asked`): a purchase never gets more than the user asked for or than any limit allows; a tie goes to the real limit. It is not one of the seven `caps`.
- **Raising a limit of the user's own, once, for this purchase only** (`offer_raise`, no model): when the position limit (one stock at most X% of the account) or the exposure limit cut what the user asked for, the Account Manager words one question ("Raise one stock to 14.7% for THIS purchase only? Say yes, a percentage, or no"). A yes, or a percentage, is passed as `limit_overrides` for this call; the profile in the account book is never changed. The loss and order limits are not offered for raising (the report still names the flag).
- **A failure shows the default and says so.** A model that cannot be reached, replies that are not the form, an answer the code refuses three times, or a user who does not answer leaves the default size (the largest the limits allow, in rank order), a message on the order (`order.errors`), and a line to the user by the Account Manager.
- **The user's screen.** `desk.build_desk(..., clarify=channel)` and the session's `clarifier_for(ask, out)` (`clarify.terminal_clarifier`): the facts are shown with the first question only; the report says a purchase sized to what the user asked for is "the amount you asked for", never that something "held it back".

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) A free chat that sets the size: the model could invent a number, so every amount must be one the user typed and code does the arithmetic. (b) Native tool calling: the project's other model calls (the request reader, RaiseFunds) use JSON replies parsed defensively; the same here, so the loop is the same under any model. (c) Asking in Auto mode: Auto sizes by the rules (round-robin) and never stops for a person. (d) Letting the model choose how to spread a total: a real run showed it picking "even" unasked, so the split is checked against the user's words too. (e) Offering to raise every limit: the loss and order limits are the user's safety rails; only the two limits that are a share of the account are offered.

**Consequences.** (1) In Ask mode a request like "I want to buy PG, MSFT and GM" now stops to ask how much (one to three questions); a request that says how much, or a budget, does not. (2) The real model was recorded on six kinds of answers (`tests/fixtures/session_real/clarify_ask_mode.json`: percent in total, dollars with an even split, shares for each company with a yes to raising the limit, percent for each, "as much as I can", and a vague answer followed by a percentage): it asked first, read each answer correctly, and in one run guessed "as much as I can" before asking (code refused it, it then asked). (3) Tests: `tests/test_account_manager_clarify.py` (87: the validator on accepted and refused cases, the arithmetic with the real prices, the sizing limit, the raise question, the loop over the real recorded replies and over faults made of one changed field of a real reply, the Account Manager's gates, the terminal; with the typed-number rule switched off two tests fail) and the opt-in live test in `tests/test_end_to_end_live.py` (the real models, the real Postgres). (4) Not done: the loss and order limits cannot be raised by answer; a total in shares that a limit cuts is not moved to another company.

## 86. No test measures how long a session takes (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Some tests asked whether the desk is fast: `tests/test_realtime_live.py` was written (entry 48) to answer "is the Tree of Thought quick enough to trade in real time?" and asserted seconds per purchase, seconds to a first plan, the length of a whole session and that a time limit saves time; `tests/test_raise_funds.py` had two tests of the `first_plan_seconds` and per-level `seconds` the search records. They were calibrated on slow providers (entry 48); with the fast ones (entry 50) a session takes about ten seconds whatever the limit, and one of them started failing for that reason. They also cost paid calls to prove nothing about whether the recommendations, the review or the trades are right.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).** *"We do not care about the length of sessions. Making sure the recommendations are correct, properly critiqued and reviewed, the Account Manager kicking off the trades with the Trader agent and properly managing the trades in the Postgres database are primary goals. Not performance testing."* Deleted: in `tests/test_realtime_live.py` `test_every_purchase_answers_within_the_time_a_person_will_wait`, `test_a_first_valid_plan_is_there_quickly_for_the_ordinary_purchases`, `test_the_whole_session_of_four_purchases_fits_in_real_time_and_the_limit_does_not_make_it_slower`, `test_nothing_is_given_up_for_the_speed` and `test_the_timings_of_the_three_sessions` (the file keeps its eight tests of whether the plans are right, with every seconds assertion taken out of them, and its fixture no longer runs the unlimited session); in `tests/test_raise_funds.py` `test_the_search_records_the_seconds_at_the_end_of_each_level_and_when_the_first_plan_completed` and `test_with_no_plan_completed_the_first_plan_time_is_none`. The search still records its seconds (a report, not tested).

**Kept on purpose.** A time that is part of the BEHAVIOUR is still tested: an approval that runs out, the T+1 rule, the trading calendar, the rate limit between lookups, the model timeout settings, the bake-off tool's cost and time limit, and the RaiseFunds time limit where it decides what is sold (no new level starts after it; with no plan completed nothing is sold and nothing half planned is). If Jacob wants those gone as well, they are `tests/test_raise_funds.py` (the `time limit` group), `tests/test_compare_models.py::test_cost_and_time_limits` and the one hurried-session test of `test_realtime_live.py`.

**Consequences.** Entry 48's measurements stay as the record of what was measured then (the numbered entries are not rewritten). No test now fails because a model or provider is faster or slower.

## 87. A request that names the trade skips the recommendation and the Critic, and goes straight to the Account Manager and the Trader (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** "Buy 50 shares of MSFT" went the same way as "buy the 3 most recommended oil companies": the Data Clerk searched, the Advisor generated a recommendation for MSFT, the Critic reviewed it, and the Account Manager then sized it by the user's limits, so the 50 shares the user typed became whatever the limits allowed, the Critic could deny the user's own choice, and in Ask mode the user was asked to approve what they had just ordered. A person who names the trade is not asking for advice.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).** *"Skip generating recommendations if the User directly wants to place specific trades. ... If a direct request is sent like: Buy 50 shares of <symbol>. Skip generating a recommendation and auto-approve the recommendation (skip the Critic step) and move the Work Order on to the AccountManager to work with Trader agent."* Built (`agents/account_manager/direct.py`, `intent.py`, `AccountManager.decide_direct`, `langgraph/desk.py`, `common/work_order.py`; the Advisor and Critic code is not changed):
- **The form.** The Account Manager's model reads the request as `action: "order"` with `orders` (`{side, symbol, shares}`: a buy or a sell, a ticker, a whole number of shares). Code accepts it only when the user's own words give every field: the ticker as typed, the shares written as a number of SHARES right before that ticker ("50 shares of MSFT", "50 MSFT", "50 shares of Microsoft Corp (MSFT)"; a dollar amount, a percentage or a decimal is never read as shares, so "Buy $5,000 of MSFT" stays a request to recommend), and the last buying or selling word before the number is the order's side. A form that holds anything else with the orders (a theme, a count, a budget) is refused. A company's name is not looked up for a direct order: the user is told to give the ticker.
- **The graph.** `account_manager -> account_manager_direct -> trader`. The Data Clerk's search, the Advisor and the Critic are never called (a test builds the desk with factories that fail the test if it asks for any of them). The work order has `action_type: "trade"` and carries the user's own orders in the new `direct_orders` (one record per trade: id `direct-<request id>-<n>`, side, ticker, shares, the user's words, the moment). `account_manager_direct` asks the Data Clerk for the current price of each ticker (the quote the Trader will be judged against) and decides each order.
- **Auto-approved.** The decision is `yes_user` with `origin: "direct"`: the user's own order is the approval, so the Account Manager asks nothing, in Ask mode too (the session's "Go ahead with this? [Y/n]" that shows what was understood stays). It is `yes_user` and not `yes_auto` because the person, not the desk's score, said yes; `DECISION_WORDS` then reads "you approved".
- **The Trader's rule T1 gets one more way, and a narrow one.** `work_order.verify_trade` (which `tickets.permission`, the Account Manager's `approve_trade` and `PaperBroker.place` all call) needs, instead of the Critic's review, the user's record on `order.direct_orders` with the same `record_id`, ticker and side, and not more shares than the user typed; the approval runs out at the decision's own `valid_until_utc` (`configs/account_manager_v1.json`, `direct.valid_minutes`: 30 after it was priced). A decision that only SAYS it is direct and finds no such record is refused; a direct order that also has a review is refused; a decision that is not direct needs the Critic's approval exactly as before. The ticket's reference price is, after the review's deal price and the plan's entry, the decision's own `price`.
- **The size is the user's, and it is placed as typed or not at all.** The profile's limits that cut a RECOMMENDED purchase (a share of the account in one stock, the loss a stop would cost, the exposure of one review, the order cap, the liquidity cap) are NOT applied to a direct order: nothing is sized, and there is no stop to work them out from. What stops it is the account: the cash for a purchase, the shares held for a sale. When the cash does not pay for all the shares, the decision is `no_funds` and says how many shares the cash does pay for; it is never made smaller on the user's behalf. The handshake keeps it so: `approve_trade` refuses (`insufficient_cash`) and does not cut a direct purchase the account can no longer pay in full, and the ticket of a direct sale is rejected (`not_sellable_today`) rather than cut to the shares not locked by T+1. A recommended purchase is still cut to the cash, as before (entry 81).
- **Sales are supported** ("Sell 10 shares of TSLA"): the number the user typed, not the whole holding that a recommended `reduce` is. No holding is sold to fund a direct purchase (RaiseFunds is for recommendations), and the desk's funds node is never reached by a direct order.
- **What the real runs showed (2026-11-15, the real models, `tests/fixtures/session_real/bill_gates_direct_order*.json`).** The stored close of MSFT on 2026-11-13 is $523.88, so 50 shares cost $26,194.00. Bill Gates with $20,000 asked for 50: refused whole (`no_funds`: "the most it pays for is 38 shares"), nothing bought, the account unchanged. With $30,000 the same request was approved and the Trader's model placed it (filled 50 at $524.1419, the paper broker's few basis points of slippage; $3,792.90 left, booked in Postgres).

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) An auto-approved synthetic Critic review (a review with `approved: true` and invented dimensions): it would put a verdict in the record that the Critic never made. The review is skipped, not forged, and the user's own order is what permits the trade. (b) `yes_auto`: it means the desk's score decided; the user decided. (c) Run the Advisor and the Critic anyway and only skip the question to the user: the Critic could still deny the user's own choice, and a recommendation nobody asked for would be generated and ledgered. (d) Apply the profile's limits to the shares typed: the user's explicit number would silently become a smaller one (4 shares of MSFT for a $20,000 account at the 10% limit), the opposite of what the user wrote. This is Jacob's to confirm: the limits are the user's own, so a later change could ask the user, once, whether to go past them, as entry 85 does. (e) Cut the order to the cash (what a recommended purchase does): buying 38 when 50 were ordered is a different order, and nobody confirms it. (f) A code-only reading with no model: "Buy 50 shares of MSFT" is easy, but "please purchase 25 shares of Microsoft Corp (MSFT) for me" and "sell 10 TSLA and buy 5 shares of GM" are what the model is for; the code checks them. (g) Look a company name up (the Finnhub symbol search): not asked, and a trade should not rest on a fuzzy name.

**Consequences.** (1) No market-hours check: the Critic's `market_closed` blocker does not apply to a direct order, and the paper broker fills at the current quote (with `USE_LIVE_TRADING=false`, the stored close of the previous business day). This is paper trading only, and the answer to whether a direct order should wait for the open belongs to Jacob. (2) A purchase that fits at the quote can still be refused at the handshake when the broker's slippage (5 basis points on a market order) makes the fill dearer than the cash; the user is told and nothing is cut. (3) The work order has a new optional field (`direct_orders`), a decision has `origin`, `price` and `valid_until_utc`, and the account book's `recommendation_decisions` row holds the direct decision under its `direct-...` id (no change of schema). (4) Every desk that has the Critic now has the node `account_manager_direct`; the tests that pin the graph's shape were updated (`test_desk_critic.py`, `test_desk_trader.py`, `test_session_offline.py`), and nothing else about them changed. (5) Tests: `tests/test_direct_order.py` (53: the form against the user's words with the real model's recorded replies, the Trader's rule T1, the decision, the handshake, the desk and the session on the real local Postgres on throwaway accounts, and the two real captures replayed; with each of four guards switched off (the handshake's refusal, the check that the order holds the user's record, the T+1 refusal, the cash check) a test fails). The full suite: 4414 passed and the two known failures of the handoff. (6) `tests/fixtures/session_real/direct_order_forms.json` is the real model's replies to six requests (five name a trade); the two Bill Gates captures are real sessions. (7) Open, Jacob's: whether the profile's limits should apply (or be asked about once), whether a direct order should respect the market hours, and company names.

## 88. A trade the user names: the company name and the symbol are on the work order, a company name goes through the Data Clerk, and no profile limit ever applies (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 87 left three questions to Jacob (its consequence 7), and he answered them in the same session.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).**
- *Profile limits:* **never.** *"never use profile limits"*, then *"profile limits never apply to any orders"*. Read as: no limit of the profile (a share of the account in one stock, the loss a stop would cost, the exposure of one review, the order cap, the liquidity cap) applies to a trade the user names, whatever its kind (a ticker or a company, a buy or a sale, one or several). What stops a direct order is the account (the cash, the shares held, T+1). A test now reads the source of the direct path (`direct.py`, `AccountManager.decide_direct`, `name_the_stocks`) and fails if it names `sizing`, `sizing_limits` or any limit of the profile. **Recommended purchases are still sized by the profile's limits (entry 80); if "any orders" meant those too, that is a separate and larger change, and Jacob has not asked for it.**
- *Market hours:* a direct order does not check them, because the paper broker fills at the quote. Correct. **TODO (Jacob): support limit orders** ("buy 50 shares of MSFT at $500 or less"): the form has no price, the record and the decision carry no limit, and the ticket takes its range from the quote. Written as a TODO in the module text of `direct.py`.
- *Company names:* *"AccountManager needs to ask the DataClerk for the Symbol that matches the company name. Then proceed with the returned symbol. Mode = ASK will ask the user to confirm the order is correct (Symbol, Company name, number of shares, the amount). mode=auto will use the returned symbol from the DataClerk without user confirmation."* And: *"when the WorkOrder is created the company name and symbol for each company should be in the WorkOrder. The Data Clerk appends this ... Direct order skips the Data Clerk so the AccountManager must ask for this and the AccountManager appends this company name and symbol to the workorder."* Built:
  - **The form** takes an order by `symbol` (a ticker in capitals) or by `company` (a name), one of the two; code checks the name is in the user's words with the number of shares right before it, a name in `symbol` is refused with where it belongs, and a ticker in `company` is too.
  - **The Data Clerk finds them.** A new work order action `lookup_symbol` and `DataClerk.request_symbols` (no model): a ticker is looked up for its company's name, a name for its ticker, with the exact matching of press releases and filings (entries 34 and 35: the same words, else the same words without Inc, Corp, Co, The and ..., never a spelling that is only similar, US listings first, an ambiguous name refused with its candidates). A stock that is not found, or fits several listings, or whose lookup fails (a rate limit, no key: never read as "not found") stops the order with the Data Clerk's own message.
  - **The Account Manager appends.** The direct path skips the Data Clerk's node, so when it makes the work order `AccountManager.name_the_stocks` asks the Data Clerk (`request_symbols`) and appends the answer: `order.data.companies` (what the Data Clerk found, one row for each stock, in the shape of `find_companies`), the symbols as the subject, and the user's own orders in `direct_orders` with the symbol, the company's name and what the user typed when it was a name. Every record must have both (`verify_direct_orders`). A ticker the user typed gets its company's name too.
  - **ASK mode confirms a company the user named**: the Account Manager shows the symbol the Data Clerk found, the company's name, the shares and the amount (the price from the Data Clerk's quote) and asks; a yes places it, a no is `no_user_denied`, no answer or nobody to ask is `pending_user` and nothing is traded. **AUTO mode** uses the returned symbol without asking, and the decision's reason says so. A ticker the user typed is not asked about in either mode (the symbol is theirs), and an order the cash cannot pay is refused before anybody is asked.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Run the Data Clerk's NODE in the direct path to do the lookup: Jacob said a direct order skips the Data Clerk, and the node's job is research. (b) The Account Manager's own symbol search (`symbols.resolve_company`, the loose matching of the desk's symbol field): the symbol must come from the Data Clerk, and a trade should rest on exact matching, never a guess. (c) Look up only the names and leave a typed ticker without a company name: Jacob asked for the company name and the symbol of each company on the work order. (d) Ask for confirmation for a typed ticker too: not asked; the words say the lookup case.

**Consequences.** (1) Every direct order makes one Finnhub symbol search for each stock (free, paced; the stored quote still needs no call when `USE_LIVE_TRADING=false`), and a ticker the directory does not have ("Symbol: ZZZZ is not found") is refused before any trade, which also catches a typo. (2) Entry 87's "a company name is refused with a message asking for the ticker" and its consequence 7 are superseded by this entry (the entry is not rewritten). (3) `verify_direct_orders` now requires `company` on every record, so the first two real Bill Gates captures were recorded again with the new flow, and a third was made (a company name, Auto mode, the Trader's model). (4) The real model's replies were recorded again with the new prompt (`tests/fixtures/session_real/direct_order_forms.json`, seven requests) and Finnhub's real symbol-search answers for ten stocks were recorded (`tests/fixtures/finnhub_company_search.json`): the tests give them back to the real Data Clerk. (5) Fixed on the way: "twenty-five shares" was read as 5 (the end of a hyphenated number), in the Python check and in the script's word check. (6) Not built, Jacob's: limit orders (the TODO above).

## 89. The end-to-end script follows the Account Manager's interface (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** `./test_prometheus.sh --session` is the end-to-end path through the Account Manager, and it had drifted from it: it asked the paid-data question even for "Buy 50 shares of MSFT", which does no research and fetches nothing; its help and prompts said nothing of a trade the user names or of the Ask-mode questions about how much; nothing stopped a flag that `session.py` gains from being missing in the script; and the header called the four-parameter research desk the end-to-end run.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15): *"update the main end-to-end test script: test_prometheus.sh to make sure it uses the most up-to-date interface with AccountManager"*.** The header and `--help` now say there are two ways in and that `--session` is the end-to-end one, through the Account Manager (it reads your words, asks how much in Ask mode, takes a trade you name straight to the Trader). A request that names the trade (a number of SHARES of a ticker or a company, after a buying or selling word) asks no paid-fetch question and passes no `--fetch`, even with `--yes` (`names_the_trade`, a few words of bash that only spare a question; the Account Manager's own reading, in Python, decides what the desk does). The request prompt gives an example of each kind. `tests/test_prometheus_script.py` pins the word check against the Account Manager's reading on the seven real recorded requests, and fails if the session parser has a flag the script does not take and forward.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Move the paid-fetch question into the Python session, after the request is understood (it would be authoritative): about a dozen tests pin the prompt order and the flow Jacob knows, so it was left. (b) Make `--session` the default of the bare script: that changes what a bare `./test_prometheus.sh` does; Jacob's to say.

**Consequences.** The research desk (no flag) is unchanged. The word check can be wrong in one direction only: a request it takes for a trade skips the question (no spend), and one it misses is asked as before.

## 90. A purge of the test suite (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** The suite had 4,511 tests (2,876 test functions; the rest are parametrized cases), many of them the same behaviour checked at many boundary values, or checks of the wording of a docstring, or tests of tools that are not the product.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).** *"The test suite is bloated with over 4400 tests. We need to purge any unneeded tests. Focus on keeping higher-level end to end tests and especially tests for more complex parts like the data collection, RAG, Trading, Account manager and recommendations. Let me know how many tests you deleted."* And: *"Can any of these 2,876 distinct functions be purged? Are all active?"*
- **Not active:** 7 test functions in `tests/fastmcp_quotes.py` were never collected (`pytest.ini` matches `test_*.py` and `fastmcp_tools.py`; two of them are also marked skip for a Redis stub); the 34 skipped tests are the paid live tests, opt-in by design; there are no shadowed, nested or commented-out tests. 239 functions ran no line of `prometheus/` at all (bash script tests, the tools' tests, guards that read source or wording, validators that pydantic runs in C).
- **Method.** A throwaway tracer (Python's `sys.monitoring`, no new dependency) recorded which lines of `prometheus/` each test runs. A test function was deleted only when every line it runs stayed covered by other tests that remain: at least 4 for data collection, RAG, trading, the Account Manager and recommendations, 3 for the Critic, the calendar and the clock, 1 for plumbing (the work order's validators, the profile's bounds, the ledger, the config files); a file with its own stand-in agent or model was thinned first (AGENTS.md says to replace those); the end-to-end and real-capture tests were never candidates. By hand: three tool tests (`test_compare_models`, `test_agent_reliability`, `test_advisor_with_without`) and the dead `fastmcp_quotes.py` were deleted whole; nine tests that pinned the wording of a docstring, a module text, the folder layout or `.env.example` were deleted; the end-to-end script's prompt tests were cut to what protects the user.
- **Result.** **4,511 tests to 2,774: 1,737 deleted (38%)**; 2,876 test functions to 1,691 (1,134 functions out of files that stay, and 3 whole files with 51 functions; the 4th file, `fastmcp_quotes.py`, had only functions pytest never collected). The whole suite was traced before and after: **the same 10,628 lines of `prometheus/` are executed, none lost**. All pass except the two that already failed. The full record, by file with every function name, is `docs/test_purge_2026-11-15.md`.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Delete by name or by file only: it would have guessed. (b) Keep one test per line (a minimal cover): about 800 tests, with no margin in the areas Jacob named. (c) Delete the two failing tests: one depends on the time of day and one on a real stored article; both are Jacob's to decide (the purge had removed the second as redundant, and it was put back).

**Consequences.** (1) A deleted test's fine detail is gone where other tests run the same lines: a boundary value that no other test uses is not checked any more (the tests that stay still run every branch). (2) The helpers, fixtures and imports that only a deleted test used are still in the files. (3) Numbers quoted in older entries (`tests/test_advisor.py (72)` and the like) are history and were not rewritten; the commands in README.md, HowToRun.txt and the lab guide that named a deleted file were updated. (4) The line tracer is not in the repo; coverage.py is allowed if a branch report is wanted.

## 91. The Account Manager does no risk management: the funds are the only check, and a neutral recommendation counts as approved (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Two things came out of one paid run of the end-to-end script (Pam Smith, 30, aggressive, $50,000, Auto: *"buy MU, SNDK and AMD if the recommendations pass. Go ahead and trade if the recommendations pass"*). (1) The Advisor scored MU and AMD NEUTRAL (no strategy passed) and SNDK bullish; the Critic denied SNDK, so no company had a recommendation that passed and nothing was bought. (2) When Jacob asked to treat a neutral score as approved, he found that a recommended purchase was still sized by the profile (10% of the equity in one stock, 30% for one review): *"This is wrong and needs removed if this is implemented"*.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).**
- ***"A neutral recommendation is same as Approved."*** A new setting, `ALLOW_NEUTRAL_RECOMMENDATIONS` in `.env` (true, yes, on or 1; unset or anything else is false, so nothing changes for anyone who does not set it; `.env.example` has it as false), read by the ACCOUNT MANAGER *"when working with the WorkOrder recommendation result to allow Trader agent to use the WorkOrder"*. The Advisor already records its answer for every company it looked at on `order.candidates` (`stance`, `gate`, `origin`, `held`, `score`, `reason`); it is not changed. With the setting on, a company the user NAMED that the Advisor scored neutral, with no failed gate and not already held, gets a trade decision with `origin: "neutral"` (`agents/account_manager/neutral.py`). The work order has a third way to permit a trade (`work_order.verify_trade`, rule T1): besides a Critic review and the user's own direct order, the Advisor's own neutral record on the order, which must still be there, with no review beside it, a purchase only, and the decision's own expiry (30 minutes). Auto mode decides `yes_auto`; Ask mode asks the user about each (`yes_user`, `no_user_denied`, or `pending_user` with nobody to ask). A neutral company has no plan, no stop and no review: nothing is forged, the order is a MARKET purchase at the Data Clerk's quote (like a direct order).
- **What stays.** A BEARISH stance trades nothing. A gate that failed (stale data, liquidity, a red flag) is not a stance and stops the company. A company the Advisor discovered for a theme, that the user did not name, is never bought as neutral. **A Critic denial is final**: only a company the Advisor did NOT recommend can be neutral, and a recommended one has a review, so the setting never turns a denial into an approval (a test forges that and the check refuses it). SNDK stayed denied in the real run.
- ***"I do not want any of these limits … Remove them all from all tests and code"*, *"the only sizing that we need is to make sure the trade order has enough funds to cover it"*, *"AccountManager does not do any risk management. It only delegates tasks to other Agents and updates the User profile and portfolio and works with verifying trades that have occured from the Trader agent."*** The four profile limits (the position, the loss at a stop-out, the order, the exposure of one review) and the stock's liquidity cap are gone from the code, the profile, the Postgres schema (`sql/prometheus_accounts.sql`: the columns are dropped by `ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS`, which `scripts/setup_postgres.sh` applies), the sample profile, the flags (`--max-position-pct` and the others), the questions of the Ask-mode loop (it no longer offers to "raise a limit once"), and the tests. `UserProfile` has no size field (`extra="forbid"`: a profile file that still has one is refused, which says so). `sizing.size_purchases` has two caps, **money** (all the cash, none reserved) and **budget** (what the user said to spend), plus the shares the user `asked` for; the allocation (`round_robin`, Auto's default, `even`, `as_much_as_possible`) only divides the user's own funds among the companies, and the Trader's handshake still reads the funds as they are at each trade. Whether a stock is too large a part of the account is the user's decision, not the Account Manager's.
- ***"Use 1"*:** the neutral companies share the money in equal dollars with no limit, in whole shares; one neutral company gets all of it.

**Result of the real run (paper broker, the simulated close of Friday 2026-11-13, `tests/fixtures/session_real/pam_smith_neutral.json`).** SNDK (bullish 46): the Critic denied it. MU (neutral 62) 32 shares at $778.31 and AMD (neutral 48) 46 shares at $534.06 decided `yes_auto`; filled MU 32 at $778.70 ($24,918.37), AMD 46 at $534.33 ($24,579.04); cash $50,000.00 to $502.59; both fills booked by the handshake; final portfolio AMD 46, MU 32, cash $502.59.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Treat a neutral score as a bullish one inside the Advisor (a neutral stance would then be a recommendation and the Critic would review it): the Advisor's score means what it says, and the Critic reviews entry plans that a neutral company does not have. (b) Have the Account Manager forge a Critic review for a neutral company: the Trader's rule T1 would then be true by a made-up record; the Advisor's own record is the permission. (c) Keep the limits but let a flag turn them off: Jacob said to remove them. (d) Remove the allocations too (only one spending rule, all cash for each): the user's own words ("split evenly", "as much as I can") and the round-robin across several approved companies still need a way to divide the funds; they do not limit what a purchase may be. (e) Keep a liquidity cap (5% of the stock's daily dollars): *"too much like a financial advisor role"*; the Critic, not the Account Manager, judges the company.

**Consequences.** (1) A user who sets the setting and names a company the Advisor does not like gets it bought; only the Critic's denial, a bearish stance, a failed gate, the cash and the Trader's own checks stop it. (2) Nothing but the cash caps a purchase now: a $50,000 account can put almost all of it into one stock, and an Auto approval can spend all the cash. That is Jacob's choice, and the handshake still refuses what the funds cannot cover. (3) The real captures made before this entry (`bob_jones_oil_round_robin.json`, the clarify recordings) were made under the old limits: their decisions are history, and the tests that replay them state the numbers of the capture, not a rule. (4) The Critic's risk score had a company measure of liquidity (`adv_20d_dollars`) and of the stop distance; Jacob said to remove them too (entry 92). (5) The older `design/sizing_in_account_manager_spec.md` describes the limits; its end carries an amendment saying so. (6) Record ids of neutral decisions number from 1 (`neutral-<request_id>-1`); the saved capture has 0-based ids and its tests compare shares and costs, not ids. (7) Tests: `tests/test_neutral_recommendation.py` (27: the setting, the candidates, the real session reproduced with the real Account Manager over the real stored closes, Ask mode, the permission and its tampered forms, the Trader buying through the handshake, the report, and the source of the neutral path naming no limit); five mutations of the code were each caught.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (entry 105).** "Not already held" is no longer a condition of a neutral recommendation: the Advisor is given no portfolio and has no `held` tag, so a company the user named that the Advisor scored neutral counts as approved whether or not the user already holds some. The Account Manager sizes the purchase, and knows what is held.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (entry 107).** A neutral recommendation is an approval WHOEVER named the company (not only one the user typed), with no failed gate; the number of companies the request asked for is kept (the Critic's approved first, then the neutral by the Advisor's score; a company beyond it gets no decision and the user is told); and the neutral purchases are sized TOGETHER with the approved ones in one call, not by a sizing of their own (equal dollars remain only for a run of neutral companies alone). "Only a company the user named" and "a company the Advisor discovered is never bought as neutral" above describe the code of that day.

**Amended 2026-11-15 (entry 109).** "Equal dollars" is no longer the default of anything: a run of neutral companies alone is spread round-robin like every other run (Pam's MU and AMD: 38 and 38 where this entry's real session had 32 and 46), and equal dollars come only from the user's own words ("split evenly"), where the even split now also spends what the equal amounts leave over (entry 108). "Use 1" (neutral companies in equal dollars) above describes the code of that day.

## 92. The Critic's risk score has no liquidity and no stop-distance measure, and Ask mode asks for the number before a neutral purchase (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 91 left two things to Jacob, and he answered both in the same session. (1) The Critic's risk score still had two measures that are not about the company's own risk but about trading it: the stock's average daily dollars (`adv_20d_dollars`, a liquidity cap in disguise) and the distance of the plan's stop (`stop_loss_pct`, the loss a stop-out costs). (2) With the setting on, an Auto approval of a neutral company can put nearly all the cash into one stock, and in Ask mode nothing asked the user for the amount of a neutral purchase: the card showed the largest order the cash allows.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).**
- ***"no on 1. Remove: Critic liquidity input: the Critic's risk score still includes a company's average daily dollar volume (adv_20d_dollars) and stop distance."*** The two measures are gone from `configs/critic_v1.json` and from `critic/risk.py`; `adv_20d_dollars` is no longer in the metrics the Critic asks the Data Clerk for again (`recheck.ALWAYS`). The risk score is six measures: volatility, drawdown, beta, ATR, market cap and the flags. Their weights were scaled to add up to 100 again, in proportion (volatility 33.4, drawdown 20.0, beta 13.3, ATR 13.3, market cap 13.3, flags 6.7; before: 27.8, 16.7, 11.1, 11.1, 11.1, 5.5 and the two removed, 11.1 and 5.6): the levels (low under 20, medium under 45, high under 70) are the same. **The weights are Jacob's to tune** (`configs/critic_v1.json`, nothing else changes with them). On the real captures: SNDK 77.8 to 80.0 (still very high, still denied for risk and a falling knife), the PG open recommendation 5.2 to 5.6 (low, still approved), its risk-fit points 18.84 to 18.76. A thin stock and a wide stop now change nothing in a review (a test sets each).
- ***"an Auto approval can now put nearly all the cash into one stock. If that is the direct order the user wants to do. The AccountManager needs to ask the user for the specific number if mode=ask if they just say: I want to buy <X>"*.** The cash is still the only cap (a direct order names its own number of shares, and Auto mode spends what the user's words and the setting allow). In ASK mode, when the request has no amount ("I want to buy MU and AMD"), the Account Manager now asks for the number BEFORE it sizes a NEUTRAL purchase too, with the same bounded ReAct questions as for every approved purchase (entry 85): `AccountManager.decide_trades` gets the neutral companies' prices from the Data Clerk first, and ONE set of questions covers the Critic's approved purchases and the neutral ones (`clarify_sizing(..., extra=)`, `neutral.purchases_for`). The shares the user names are exactly what is proposed for each neutral company (`neutral.decide_neutral(..., wanted=)`: the user's amount is not cut to an equal split unless they asked for a split), and the user then says yes or no to each card. Auto mode never asks; a request that said how much, or no terminal, asks nothing (the proposal is the most the cash buys, equal dollars, and waits for a yes).

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep the two measures and only say so: Jacob said to remove them. (b) Give the removed weights to one measure (volatility): scaling all four to a hundred keeps the mix the Critic already had. (c) Make "I want to buy MSFT" (no number) a direct order that asks for the shares: a direct order is a request that names the trade AND its number of shares (entry 87); a request with no number is a request to recommend and then to ask how much, which is what entry 85 does.

**Consequences.** (1) The Critic's risk score no longer reads anything of the trade plan except the flags (a report inside the horizon and the like) and no longer reads the stock's volume. (2) The Advisor still has a liquidity GATE for new ideas (`adv_20d_dollars` under $5,000,000, a price under $5 or a wide spread), and the Critic's integrity check still denies a new position whose gate failed: it is the Advisor's own test of a company before it recommends it, not a cap on the user's size, and **it is Jacob's to say whether it should go too** (it is the last liquidity rule in the desk). (3) The saved reviews and captures from before (`bob_jones_oil*.json`, the Advisor and Critic captures) record the old scores; the tests that replay them state what was recorded, and the tests that recompute a review (`tests/test_critic_real.py`, `tests/test_critic.py`) state the new numbers, worked by hand. (4) Tests: `tests/test_critic_risk.py` (no liquidity or stop measure, weights add to 100) and four more in `tests/test_neutral_recommendation.py` (the question is asked once with the model's real recorded replies, the shares named are what is proposed and shown, one question covers both kinds of purchase, and Auto, a request with an amount and no terminal ask nothing); two mutations of the code were caught.

## 93. The Advisor has no liquidity gate at all (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 92 removed the Critic's liquidity input and left one question to Jacob: the Advisor's own `liquidity` gate (a new idea failed when its 20-day average daily dollars were under $5,000,000, its price under $5, or its median spread over 20 basis points, or when the volume could not be read) and the Critic's integrity check that a new position has no failed gate. It was the last liquidity rule in the desk.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15): *"Remove all of 1. There are no liquidity gates at all for the Advisor."*** The gate is gone: `advisor/gates.py` runs four gates (`no_metrics`, `freshness`, `red_flag`, `event_risk`), `configs/advisor_v1.json` and the `Gates` config have no `min_adv_dollars`, `min_price` or `max_spread_bps` (they are an error if a file still has them: `extra="forbid"`), and `run_gates` no longer takes `held` (the holding exemption existed only for this gate). A thin, cheap stock with a wide spread is ranked, scored, recommended and reviewed like any other. The Critic's check is unchanged and general (any failed gate on a new position is an integrity failure): with this gate gone it can only be about stale data, a red flag or an earnings report days away. `adv_20d_dollars` stays one of the Data Clerk's metrics (a fact about a stock; the Data Clerk's catalog, the MCP tools and the metrics tests are not touched). The user's own minimum price in the profile (`min_price`, a Critic blocker `price_below_profile_min`) is the user's own exclusion, not a gate, and stays.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep the gate and only waive it for a user who names the company: Jacob said none at all. (b) Keep the $5 price floor and the spread test but drop the volume test: they are the same kind of rule (a stock too thin or cheap to trade) and fall with it. (c) Remove `adv_20d_dollars` from the Data Clerk: it is a fact, not a rule, and other users of the catalog may want it.

**Consequences.** (1) Nothing in the desk refuses a stock for being thinly traded, cheap or wide in its spread; the cash, the user's own words and the Trader's checks are the only limits (entries 91 and 92). (2) A stock with fewer than 20 stored sessions used to fail every new idea (`not computable: needs bars for adv_20d_dollars`) and no longer does; it still fails `freshness` when its newest bar is old and the families that need long histories stay not computable. (3) The tests that used a thin stock to make a gate fail (candidates with a reason, a failed gate ranked last, a neutral candidate that is stopped by a gate) now use the `red_flag` gate (`cfo_negative_with_profit`), which is the same machinery; `tests/test_gates.py` has a test that a thin, cheap, wide-spread stock fails nothing and `tests/test_advisor_config.py` that the old thresholds are refused. (4) Old real captures keep a `liquidity` gate record (`gate` is a free string on the record; they were made under the old rule and are history). (5) `design/advisor_v1_final_spec.md` Amendment 11 says so; its section 9.1 and the earlier docs that list five gates are history.

## 94. The Critic does not depend on the Advisor's liquidity gate, and the Advisor asks for no liquidity number at all (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 93 removed the Advisor's `liquidity` gate and said the Critic's integrity check (a new position with a failed gate is a defect, so it is denied) was "unchanged and general". Jacob: *"Remove the Critic dependency on this Advisor liquidity gate. Critic denies a new position if that gate failed. We don't want any of this advisor liquidity anywhere."* The check read every gate record of the Advisor's record by whatever name, so a record that still held a failed `liquidity` gate (every real capture made before entry 93 can) would still have been denied, and the Advisor still asked the Data Clerk for the stock's daily dollars and its spread, wrote them in the record's `risk` block, used the spread to choose an order style and showed the daily dollars in the report's key numbers.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).**
- **The Critic.** `critic/integrity.py` has `GATES = ("no_metrics", "freshness", "red_flag", "event_risk")`: the gates of the Advisor's record that a new position must pass. A gate record of any other name decides nothing (a `liquidity` record in an old capture is read and ignored). The Critic's own risk score already had no liquidity measure (entry 92).
- **The Advisor.** `adv_20d_dollars` and `median_spread_bps_1d` are no longer requested for the gates or the risk block (`recommend.py` `GATE_METRICS`, `RISK_METRICS`, `_risk`), the `Risk` record has no such fields (it reads and drops the two keys of an old record: `extra="ignore"` on that one model), the order style no longer looks at a spread (the trend family's marketable limit depends only on the 200-day mean; the threshold `trade.limit_spread_bps` is gone from the config and a file that still has it is refused; the spread metric is never computed in this build, so no plan changes), and the report's key numbers have no "Avg daily $ volume" column.
- **Not changed.** The Data Clerk still calculates `adv_20d_dollars` (a fact, in the metrics catalog, the MCP tools and the metrics tests) and the profile's own minimum price (`min_price`, the Critic's blocker `price_below_profile_min`, the user's own exclusion). Nothing in the Advisor or the Critic reads either of the two metrics, so removing them from the catalog is a separate, larger change if Jacob wants it (82 metrics are quoted in several documents).

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Leave the Critic's check as it was: it never names liquidity, but it would deny an old record for it. (b) Name `liquidity` in the Critic as an ignored legacy gate: the Critic should not know the word; the list of the gates it enforces says what it checks. (c) Remove the metrics from the Data Clerk's catalog now.

**Consequences.** (1) The Critic would not deny a recommendation for a failed gate that the Advisor does not have, and a future gate must be added to `GATES` to be enforced (a test that tampers with each of the four fails if one is dropped: `tests/test_critic_integrity.py`). (2) The real captures made before this entry still hold a `liquidity` gate and a `risk` block with two keys; they still load.
