(3) `design/advisor_v1_final_spec.md` Amendment 11 and `design/critic_v1_final_spec.md` Amendment 8 say so.

## 95. No spread and no daily-dollars number anywhere in the code, the Data Clerk included (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 94 took every liquidity input out of the Advisor and the Critic but left the Data Clerk calculating the stock's average daily dollars (`adv_20d_dollars`) and listing the spread (`median_spread_bps_1d`, a name the catalog kept as "not available in this build"), and left tests and test helpers that fed or checked them. Jacob: *"We do not want any code and tests that handle any spread, the daily-dollars requests. Remove any code and tests that use this."*

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).** Both are gone. `technicals.adv_20d_dollars` and its minimum-bars entry, the catalog's `adv_20d_dollars` metric (the "risk" group) and its `median_spread_bps_1d` placeholder are removed; the Advisor's gate and risk metric lists, its `Risk` record, its order style and the report's key numbers lost them in entry 94; the tests and helpers that used them are removed or rewritten without them (`tests/test_gates.py` asserts the four gates only; `tests/test_critic_risk.py` the six measures and a wide stop; `tests/advisor_helpers.py`, `tests/test_trade_plan.py`, `tests/test_technicals.py`, `tests/test_metrics_catalog.py`, `tests/test_advisor_config.py`, `tests/test_advisor_no_size.py`, `tests/test_account_manager_sizing.py` and `tests/test_neutral_recommendation.py` no longer name either). The catalog now lists 89 names, 83 of them calculated and 6 not available yet (the documents said 82 before; that count was already out of date).

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep the metric in the catalog as "a fact": Jacob said no code at all. (b) Re-capture the real fixtures so that they hold neither number: the captures are records of real runs made before this entry, they still load (the `Risk` model reads and drops the two old keys) and their stored metrics are data, not code; a re-capture is a paid run for no change in behaviour.

**Consequences.** (1) A request for `adv_20d_dollars` or `median_spread_bps_1d` to the Data Clerk now answers like any unknown metric name. (2) The Finnhub 10-day average volume (`adv_10d_provider`, in shares, from the provider's own payload) is a different number that nothing reads; it was NOT removed: say if it should go too. (3) The real captures of `tests/fixtures/` still contain the two old keys inside their stored metrics and `risk` blocks. (4) `design/advisor_v1_final_spec.md` Amendment 11 and the Data Clerk's notes are updated; older entries that mention the metric are history.

## 96. The Finnhub 10-day average volume is removed too (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 95 removed the daily-dollars and spread numbers and left one open question: the Finnhub 10-day average volume (`adv_10d_provider`, the provider's `10DayAverageTradingVolume`, in shares), still in the Data Clerk's payload mapping and a metrics test.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15): *"Yes, delete the 10-day average volume still in the Data Clerk's payload mapping and metrics tests."*** `adv_10d_provider` is removed from `FINANCIAL_METRICS` and `FINANCIAL_SCALE` (`data_toolbox.py`), so it is no longer a metric of the catalog, and the assertions about it in `tests/test_metrics_catalog.py` are gone (`FINANCIAL_SCALE` is `{"market_cap": 1_000_000}`). Nothing in the desk reads any trading-volume figure for liquidity now; the relative volume ratio (`volume_ratio_1d`, today's volume over the mean of the 20 sessions before) is a chart and event signal, not a liquidity number, and stays.

**Consequences.** (1) The catalog lists 88 names, 82 of them calculated and 6 not available yet. (2) The stored Finnhub payloads and the real captures may still hold the provider's own field; nothing reads it. (3) Older entries that name `adv_10d_provider` (the base-unit entry and the Advisor spec's list of Finnhub fields) are history.

## 97. No market capitalization anywhere, and with it no net payout yield (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 95 and 96 took the liquidity numbers out. Jacob: *"Delete all code and tests that use or checks: market cap. We do not care about this in our code."* The market capitalization was used in five places: the event family halved its score for a company over $200,000,000,000, the Critic's risk score had a market-cap measure (13.3 of 100), the report's key numbers had a column for it, the Advisor's record carried it in `identity`, and the **net payout yield** `(buybacks + dividends - stock issued) / market cap` was the main test of the capital_return family and one of the filings inputs of the Advisor's 40/30/20/10 score. The yield cannot exist without its denominator.

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15; asked: *"Remove everything, including the net payout yield"*).** All of it is removed.
- **Data Clerk:** the Finnhub field `marketCapitalization` (and `FINANCIAL_SCALE`, the unit conversion it needed), `fundamentals.net_payout_yield_ttm` and the catalog metric of that name. The catalog has 86 names, 80 of them calculated and 6 not available yet.
- **Advisor:** the event family's large-cap halving (`large_cap`, `large_cap_factor`, and `_finish`'s `haircut`); the capital_return family has no yield: it passes when **the share count fell at least 0.5%** (what it did when the yield could not be computed), debt, earnings yield, payout coverage and revenue growth rules unchanged, and it scores `0.40 + 0.15 + 0.10` (a fall over 1%) `+ 0.10` (a fresh buyback authorization or dividend raise, halved when it exceeds the cash) `+ 0.10` (revenue growth): `min_net_payout_yield`, `w_payout` and `payout_scale` are gone from the config and `w_missing_payout` became `w_buyback`; the filings part of the blend lost the yield (share count change, payout coverage and cash flow against profit remain); `Identity.market_cap` is gone (the record reads and drops it from an old capture: `extra="ignore"`); the freshness gate no longer looks at it; the report has no "Market cap" or "Net payout yield" row.
- **Critic:** the risk score has five measures (volatility 38.5, drawdown 23.1, beta 15.3, ATR 15.3, flags 7.8; weights scaled in proportion to 100 again, Jacob's to tune); `market_cap` left `price_linked` and the metrics the Critic asks again; the `log` option of a risk measure, which only the market cap and the daily dollars used, is deleted with its validator.

**Consequences.** (1) SNDK is still recommended on capital_return (its share count fell 1.37%) but with a lower score, and GM, held, is now an `add` (60 against SNDK 59); PG is a conviction recommendation (the blend rose because the yield, a middling input, is out: 79 against 78) and its capital_return no longer passes (no buyback). The risk of the real captures: SNDK 92.2 (was 80.0), PG open 6.5 (was 5.6). (2) A company that pays dividends but does not buy shares back no longer passes capital_return. (3) The tests: those of the removed code are deleted (`test_fundamentals`, `test_filing_signals`, `test_metrics_catalog`, `test_families`, `test_critic_*`); the real-capture tests of the Advisor replay the real stored data, and **the real model's thesis replies of the three fixtures (`tests/fixtures/advisor_real/`) were re-recorded** (the plan replies are the originals): the old theses quoted numbers that no longer exist. (4) The old real captures still hold a `market_cap` key in their metrics and identity and still load. (5) `design/advisor_v1_final_spec.md` Amendment 12 and `design/critic_v1_final_spec.md` Amendment 9 say so.

## 98. A stock named with no number is a direct order (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 87 made a request that names the trade AND a number of shares a direct order; a request that named a stock with no number ("I want to buy MSFT") still went through the Advisor and the Critic, and my notes called that "not built: a change to entry 87's rule". Jacob: *"This is wrong ... This is actually a Direct order since a recommendation is not needed for direct orders unless specified that a recommendation should be used in the user prompt."* (and, before, for Ask mode: *"The AccountManager needs to ask the user for the specific number if mode=ask if they just say: I want to buy <X>"*).

**Decision (Jacob, 2026-11-15).** A request that names stocks to buy or sell is a direct order, with or without a number of shares, **unless its words ask for a recommendation**.
- **The program decides, not the model** (`intent.py`): `RECOMMEND_CUE` (recommend, advice, research, analyze, "should I", "worth buying", "if the recommendations pass/are approved") in the user's own words makes it a request to buy or research, whatever the model said (a form with `orders` and such a cue is turned into a "buy" with the named stocks); and `as_direct_order` turns a "buy" form of named tickers or companies with no theme, no count ("the best 3 of ...") and no such cue into a direct order, whatever the model said (the real model answered both ways on the same kind of request). `DirectOrder.shares` may be None (null when the user wrote no number; a number typed right before the stock with a null is refused). Dollars to spend ("Buy $5,000 of MSFT", "buy MSFT and TSLA and spend $6,000") are the budget of such an order.
- **Sizing is the Account Manager's** (`AccountManager.decide_direct` -> `_size_direct`): in **Ask mode**, with a channel to the user and no dollars or "as much as I can" in the words, it first ASKS for the number with the same bounded questions as for any purchase (entry 85), then shows the order (the shares and the amount) and the user says yes or no (`yes_user`, `no_user_denied`, or `pending_user` with nobody to ask); in **Auto mode** nothing is asked and the funds are spent as the user's words say (their budget, an even split when asked, otherwise round-robin: one stock gets what the cash allows). Nothing but the cash bounds it (entry 91). The number lands on the user's own record (`direct_orders[i].shares`, `how_many`: `asked`, `budget` or `cash`), which is what the Trader's rule T1 reads (a record with no number permits nothing).
- **A sale with no number is not placed**: the Account Manager does not choose how much of a holding to sell; the decision says to name how many (Jacob's to say if Ask mode should ask for a sale too).
- **The model's prompt** (`prompts/account_manager_request.py`) says so, and the real model's replies to six such requests are recorded in `tests/fixtures/session_real/direct_order_forms.json`.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Leave "I want to buy MSFT" as a request to recommend (my earlier reading). (b) Let the model alone decide direct versus recommend: it answered "order" and "buy" for the same kind of request on different runs. (c) Refuse a stock with no number and ask the user to type one: Ask mode asks it itself.

**Consequences.** (1) A request that names tickers and no recommendation word no longer reaches the Advisor or the Critic: the live tests that meant the recommendation path now say "if they are recommended". (2) The script's check that spares the paid-refresh question (`names_the_trade`) also takes "a ticker in capitals, a buying word, no recommendation or theme word". (3) Entry 92's alternative (c) and the "not built" note in `design/week6_updated_design.md` are superseded by this entry. (4) Tests: `tests/test_direct_order.py` (the forms of the real model, the program's reading, Auto, Ask with the real recorded questions, a no, no channel, a sale, T1, the Trader fill) and a live test (`tests/test_end_to_end_live.py`).

## 99. A web page over the desk: React in `ui/`, a FastAPI bridge in `prometheus/application/web/` (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** The desk is five agents and a LangGraph, and the only way to see it was a terminal. Jacob wanted a page a person who is not at a terminal can use: what is in my account, what the desk is doing with my request right now, and the stocks on the work order with the recommendation behind each one. The page must add no trading logic, must work with no account anywhere (no tracing service, no key beyond the ones the desk already has), and must be buildable, testable and demoable for free. The design is `design/ui_v1_spec.md` with the visual reference `design/ui_v1_mockup.html`.

**Decision.**
- **Stack.** React 19 + Vite + TypeScript in `ui/` (no UI kit, no state library, no router: one page with one reducer; plain CSS with the mockup's tokens; the two fonts are bundled so it works offline). The bridge is **FastAPI** in the same process as the agents (`create_app`, `views`, `runs`, `replay`, `desk_run`, `settings`, `tracing`, `emitter`, `accounts`). `uvicorn` was already a dependency; `fastapi` is the one new line of `requirements.txt` (installed beside starlette 1.8.0 and pydantic 2.14.1 with no other package changed).
- **Server to browser is Server-Sent Events**, browser to server is plain POST. Each event is `id` / `event` / `data`, numbered from 1 inside a run; a page that connects late (or reloads in the middle of a run) is given every event from the start, so everything redraws, and a reconnect sends `Last-Event-ID`. Every `data` also carries `ts`, the server's clock, so the page's counters survive a reload.
- **Progress comes from LangGraph itself:** `graph.stream(..., stream_mode=["tasks", "values"])` (checked on a three-node toy graph on LangGraph 1.2.17: a `tasks` chunk has `input` and `triggers` when a node starts and `result`, `error`, `interrupts` when it ends; a node that raises starts and then the exception leaves the stream). `desk.py` and `session.py` are not changed: the run imports their pieces (`build_parser`, `build_parts`, `build_desk`, `with_overrides`, `NODE_WORDS`, `decision_lines`, `status_lines`, `trade_lines`, `use_of_money_lines`, `why_not`) and the page shows what the agents wrote. Six stages, five agents (`views.STAGES`): the Account Manager has two. A stage is pending, busy, waiting for the user, done, skipped (a direct order skips three, a neutral pass skips the Critic) or failed ("stopped here").
- **The page answers the Account Manager's questions** (the confirmation of what was understood, how much, and each trade in Ask mode) with cards. `Run.ask` blocks the worker thread until the page answers, the run is stopped, or the time passes (600 s): no answer is `None`, never a yes, exactly what the terminal versions give when nobody is there. Auto mode does not ask for the confirmation (the CLI's `--yes`) and still asks for an approval the desk needs (a review that asks for a person to look: the first live pizza run did).
- **One run at a time**, one process, bound to 127.0.0.1 (one setting is process-wide, the agents are heavy, it is a single-user desk). A second `POST /api/runs` is a 409.
- **The Mode switch (Ask / Auto) on the request card is the account's trade mode** (added on Jacob's word, 2026-11-15: *"we need a slider on/off for Mode = Ask / Auto on the main window and this hooked up to AccountManager interface"*). Off is Ask, on is Auto. Flipping it saves at once through the Account Manager (`PATCH /api/accounts/{id}`, which calls `AccountManager.users.set_trade_mode`); the switch moves first and moves back, with the server's message under it, when the save is refused. `POST /api/runs` also takes `mode` (checked with `clean_trade_mode`; a bad value is a 422) and, when it differs from the account's, saves it the same way before the run starts, after the "a run is already active" refusal so that a run that does not start changes nothing: a run always uses the mode on the screen. It is the only control for the mode (the Profile card's Trades control is gone). With no account yet the switch holds its own value and Create account sends it; it is disabled while a run is active, for a sample account and in a replay.
- **The page's own account** in the Postgres book (created from the Profile form, remembered by number in `localStorage`); a sample account (a `seed_key`) can be looked at and never written (a 409), because `tests/test_account_book.py` pins their counts. The routes go through the Account Manager (`AccountManager.connect`, `users.set_*`, `set_risk_level`, `add_funds`, `remove_funds`, `list_portfolio`), not the store. A portfolio's value on the page is **shares times average cost**, and says so: the book knows no market price (live prices cost Finnhub calls: a follow-up).
- **A replay mode** (`python -m prometheus.application.web --replay FILE`, `./run_ui.sh --replay`) plays a real capture made with `session --save` through the same events and the same view code, node by node, each with the work order cut back to what that node had written; the fills arrive one by one. No model, no quote, no database, no key. In Ask mode the replay asks what the live desk asks and plays on whatever the answer. It is what the page is built, tested, screenshotted and demoed against, and what the tests use (real captures, no stand-in agent).
- **The two checkboxes of the request card are the settings of one run** (`settings.run_settings`, the only place that maps them; confirmed by Jacob, 2026-11-15, in his words: *"The Strict Recommendations is only allow Approved recommendations (no neutral allowed)"* and *"ALLOW_NEUTRAL_RECOMMENDATIONS=true in .env follows that matching checkbox"*): Strict off means the automatic-trading mark of the run is the Critic's approval mark (`verdict.approve_min_score`, read from `configs/critic_v1.json`) and the neutral box decides `ALLOW_NEUTRAL_RECOMMENDATIONS` for the run; Strict on means the profile's own mark and no neutral passes. The variable is set in this process's own environment for the length of the run and put back, even when the run raises; `.env` is never read or written by the web package (a test reads its source).
- **Observability is three layers.** The stepper and the feed (always on). A model-call counter (`tracing.ActivityHandler`): **the first live run showed the callbacks reach only some of the agents' model calls** (the Account Manager's, the Data Clerk's and the Trader's, not the Advisor's or the Critic's), so a live total would be wrong and the counter is left out of a live run (`desk_run.COUNT_MODEL_CALLS`); the replay shows the exact figures its capture recorded. **Langfuse** is off unless `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` and `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` are in the environment (`langfuse.langchain.CallbackHandler` of SDK v3 and later, the run's id as the session, the tags `prometheus-ui`, the mode, strict, neutral; keys read with `rag.config.setting`); the same reach limit applies to what it sees, and with it on the prompts, replies and the account's figures of a run go to that server. LangSmith needs no code (`LANGSMITH_TRACING=true`, `LANGSMITH_API_KEY`).
- **The feed never just stops** (found in Jacob's first live run through the page, 2026-11-15: "Buy as much stock in: F, GM, Procter and gamble and Google. And Chick-fil-a."). The Data Clerk found F, GM and PG and answered "Google: Company Name not found." and "Chick-fil-a: Company Name not found."; a direct order is placed as typed or not at all (entry 88), so the desk ended at its first node WITH a work order and `errors`. The bridge showed the desk's `errors` only when there was NO work order, so the feed ended after "read the request and made the work order", the stage read done, and the Trader's "did not run" report was printed for a run it never saw. Now `Emitter.finished` writes the feed's last line for every run (the headline and every reason, coloured by the status), the state's `errors` mark Create Work Order "stopped here" whether or not an order exists, and such a run ends `no_trade` with the Data Clerk's own reasons and one line of help. The back end is unchanged: the exact lookup and "as typed or not at all" are Jacob's rules (a brand such as Google is not the listed company's name; type the ticker). Test: `tests/test_web_live.py`, with his request and the Data Clerk's words.
- **Pressing Go! in live mode is a paid run** (OpenRouter and Finnhub), and **the "Refresh market data first (paid)" box is ON by default** (Jacob, 2026-11-15: *"Refresh market data first (paid), off by default. Make this on by default"*), so Go! also calls the paid data APIs and writes the data files unless the box is unticked: the Advisor's freshness gate refuses stale bars, and a demo on old data recommends nothing. **Only the page turns it on:** `/api/health` says `defaults.fetch: true` (live and replay alike, one shape) and the page ticks the box from it, but `POST /api/runs` keeps its own default, `fetch` false when it is missing, so a script, a test or an agent that says nothing never pays for a fetch (AGENTS.md: send `fetch: false` unless Jacob says otherwise). In a replay the box is shown unticked and disabled. The Trader is simulated always; the page says PAPER TRADING in its header and no control suggests a real order. The page adds no rule about what may trade (no size limit, no liquidity rule): it shows what the agents wrote.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Streamlit or Gradio: quick, but the pipeline, the cards and the live table are the point of the page and are hand-made; and a second UI framework to learn for one page. (b) A WebSocket: the traffic is one-way except for a handful of answers, SSE is plain HTTP, reconnects by itself, replays from an event id and can be read with `curl`. (c) A hook inside `desk.py` for progress: it would put a callback in the core for a thing LangGraph already streams. (d) Polling: it cannot show a stage that is busy for 40 seconds, and it hides the hand-offs. (e) Langfuse as the source of the stepper: a tracing account must not be needed to see a stage turn green.

**Consequences.** (1) The page and the bridge add no agent logic; the one change outside `web/`, `ui/`, the tests and the docs is `remove_funds` (entry 100). (2) Every refusal on the page is `{"error": "<a message for the user>"}` that names the thing and never a value from `.env` or a connection detail (a database failure is its kind only). (3) The first live run, a direct order ("Buy 2 shares of MSFT", Auto mode, a throwaway account, no fetch), went end to end in 2.9 seconds with the real graph (the `tasks` stream, the Postgres booking, a `portfolio` event per fill); the second, the default pizza prompt in Auto mode, ran the stages to the Account Manager's decision in 29 seconds and then asked for an approval of YUM (the Critic's review asked for a person to look). (4) Not built: live prices in the portfolio, run history (persisting work orders is already open), the inner hand-offs as their own marks in the pipeline, and limit orders, stop-loss orders and the monitoring mode (back-end TODOs of Jacob's: the page has no control for them).

## 100. Funds can be taken out of the account: `remove_funds` (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** The account book had deposits only. The Profile card of the page has a Funds box with Add and Remove, and a person who deposits too much has no way back.

**Decision.** `AccountStore.remove_funds(account_id, amount, when=None)` beside `add_funds` and `AccountManager.remove_funds(amount, when=None)`, which calls it: the account row is locked, must be active, and must hold the amount ("the account has $X in cash: $Y cannot be removed"); one `cash_ledger` row of kind `withdrawal` with a negative amount and the balance that follows. `parse_money` rules apply (a string or `Decimal`, two decimals, above zero; a float is refused). The schema and its trigger already allowed it: no SQL changes.

**Not risk management.** It is the cash check the book already makes (the cash is the only thing it looks at, as for a purchase): it limits no trade and applies no profile limit.

**Consequences.** (1) `tests/test_account_book.py` has the test, on a throwaway account. (2) A withdrawal is a ledger row like the others, so the realized-gain and trade counts do not move.

## 101. The Data Clerk's company-name search finds the listed owner of a brand: "Google" is Alphabet Inc., GOOGL (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Jacob's first live request typed into the page (2026-11-15) was *"Buy as much stock in: F, GM, Procter and gamble and Google. And Chick-fil-a."* The Data Clerk's `request_symbols` (entry 88) answered `Google: Company Name not found.` and the whole order stopped. Measured the same day: Finnhub's symbol search returns **0 rows** for `google` and for `facebook` and one row for `alphabet` (GOOGL, "Alphabet Inc."): the directory holds no brand and no former name, so it cannot cross-reference by itself. Jacob: *"So, Google is now Alphabet. Is there a way to cross reference and/or fix this in the Data clerk company name search to return GOOGL"*. The knowledge already exists in the desk: the Data Clerk's company finder (`find_companies`, entry 53) asks its model for the owner of a brand ("Burger King") and a program looks the owner's name up; the direct-order path did not use it.

**Decision** (`DataClerk.request_symbols`, `_listed_owner`, `_propose`; the work order, `direct.py` and `work_order.DIRECT_OPTIONAL` carry the result).
- **First, the exact lookup, unchanged** and with no model: a ticker in capitals is the row whose symbol it is; a name is the row with the same words, else the same words without the generic company words. Most requests end here.
- **New, only for a NAME the exact lookup did not find** (never for a ticker, and not for a name that fits several listings: the directory already answered that one): the Data Clerk asks its model for the **listed owner of that name** (the proposal step of `find_companies`, one name: `companies_prompt(name, 1)`, one call of about 450 tokens in) and looks **the owner's name** up with the same exact lookup (`symbols.find_company`). **The symbol is always the directory's.** The model supplies a company name and nothing else that is used: its ticker hint is not read, and neither is the "start of the listed name" tier of `_resolve_proposal` (fine for choosing companies to rank, not for a trade). A name the model gives that is not a company name (characters, length) is not echoed and not looked up. No stored-news discovery (`discover_tickers` is for a theme).
- **Found:** a row `{brand: "Google", parent: "Alphabet Inc.", symbol: "GOOGL", company: "Alphabet Inc.", public: True, how: "brand", note: "the listed owner of what you typed, named by the Data Clerk's model; the symbol is the directory's"}`. The user's record (`order.direct_orders`) has `typed_company: "Google"`, `company: "Alphabet Inc."`, `found_by: "brand"` (a new optional key), and the confirmation that already exists for a company the user NAMED applies: in Ask mode the card, the question and the reason say that the symbol is the listed owner of what was typed; in Auto mode the returned symbol is used and the reason says so.
- **Not listed:** every stock the directory ANSWERED for gets a row, found or not. A stock that is not listed has `symbol: None` and the reason in its `note`: `privately held, or not known to be listed` (the model named no owner: Chick-fil-a), `no listed company named '<owner>' in Finnhub's directory`, the candidates of a name that fits several listings, `no such symbol in Finnhub's directory` for a typed ticker. The order's error for it is `<typed>: <note>` (the old messages `Symbol: X is not found` and `Company Name not found. (candidates)` are kept for a ticker and for several listings).
- **A lookup that FAILED is never "not found"** (a rate limit, no key, the model did not answer, the owner's search failed): `<typed>: the symbol lookup failed (...)`, no row. That is how the Account Manager tells a company that is not listed from a question that was not answered (entry 102). The order is still `data_failed` when any stock has no symbol, so every other caller behaves as before.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) An alias table kept by hand (Google to Alphabet, Facebook to Meta ...): it is the same knowledge the model already has, it goes stale (Jacob's own example is a rename), and somebody has to keep it. (b) Finnhub's search alone: measured, it has no brand and no former name. (c) Using the model's ticker hint (`GOOGL`): a model that is wrong once buys the wrong company; the symbol must come from the directory by the exact words of a name. (d) The "start of the listed name" and "same distinctive words" tiers of `_resolve_proposal`: right for choosing companies to rank, too loose for a trade. (e) The whole `find_companies` for one name: it also runs a stored-news discovery that prints a progress bar and is for a theme. (f) A dedicated owner prompt: cleaner than "an industry or theme" with a count of one, and not done because the existing prompt was measured on both names (Google: Alphabet Inc.; Chick-fil-a: no owner) and a new one would have to be measured again; it is the obvious next step if a brand goes wrong.

**Consequences.** (1) One small model call (the Data Clerk's, gpt-oss-120b) for each NAME the directory does not have, nothing for a ticker or a name it has. (2) A direct order whose model proposes a wrong owner that the directory does have would trade the wrong company: Ask mode shows it (the card says "the listed owner of 'Google'"), Auto mode does not ask (entry 88). (3) `request_symbols` now gives rows with no symbol; every reader skips them (`views.companies_found`, the session and the page print them as "did not find"). (4) Tests: the real Finnhub answers for F, GM, "Procter and gamble", Google, Chick-fil-a and "Alphabet Inc." were added to `tests/fixtures/finnhub_company_search.json`, the Data Clerk's two real replies to `tests/fixtures/session_real/direct_order_names.json`, and `tests/test_direct_order.py` has the Data Clerk's tests (the three older tests that pinned `Company Name not found.` were amended, each with its reason). Amends entries 87 and 88 without rewriting them.

## 102. A list goes on without the stocks that were not found: Auto mode drops them and says so, Ask mode asks, and a blocking issue asks the user to clarify the request (2026-11-15)

**Problem.** A direct order is placed as typed or not at all (entry 88), and a stock the Data Clerk could not find refused the WHOLE order: Jacob's request lost F, GM, Procter & Gamble and Google because Chick-fil-a is not listed, and the page, then the terminal, said only that the work order was new. Jacob: *"Missing companies in a list should keep going and ask the user if they want to ignore the missing companies and continue (ReAct pattern). If yes to continue, then drop the invalid companies from the workorder and continue"*, *"A better user message is needed when a company is missing, than just stopping. Notify the user with a ReAct prompt that this is missing and OK to continue for each missing company not matched or found."*, and then, on the design: *"Yes fix the first one"* (the model words the questions) and *"Auto mode will not ask any prompts. Stop and ask the user to clarify the initial prompt if there is a blocking issue. Remove missing (not found / unmatched) companies if in auto mode"*.

**Decision** (`AccountManager.name_orders`, `agents/account_manager/missing.py`, `direct.py`, `langgraph/desk.py` `read_request`, `prompts/account_manager_missing.py`, the `missing` section of `configs/account_manager_v1.json`).
- **Every stock the user named is one of three** after the lookup (`direct.sort_names`): **found** (a row with a symbol), **not listed** (a row with no symbol), **not answered** (no row: the lookup failed).
- **A blocking issue stops the run, in either mode, and nothing is asked**: a stock whose lookup failed (an outage is not a company to ignore), nothing found at all, two orders for the same stock, a request that could not be read. The run's last message gives the reasons, then **"Please clarify your request and send it again: name each stock by its ticker or by the listed company's name."** (`direct.CLARIFY`): in the web bridge it is the hint of `Emitter.finished` (`NOT_PLACED_HINT`, reworded), in the terminal session it is the line under the reasons (`session.py`, which also no longer says "the Data Clerk's work order is new"). It is the end of the run, not a question in the middle of one. The desk's state says so (`clarify: True`).
- **AUTO mode asks nothing.** With some stocks found and some not listed, the missing ones are **dropped from the work order without a question**; the user is told which and why (a line from the Account Manager in the feed and the terminal: "Chick-fil-a was not found (privately held, or not known to be listed) and was left out (Auto mode asks nothing); the order goes on with F, GM, PG and GOOGL"), the work order notes it (`order.data.notes`: "dropped in Auto mode: ..."), the desk's `plan["dropped"]` holds it, and `order.direct_orders`, `order.subject` and the plan's intent hold only the stocks that stay, so the money the user named is spread over those. Neither the approver nor the model is called. The mode is the run's own (`request["account"]["mode"]`, else the profile's trade mode: what `decide_direct` uses).
- **ASK mode asks about each missing stock, one question each, in the order typed, never more than there are** (`missing.ask_about_each`, bounded by `missing.max_prompts` in the config; more than that is a request to clarify). The shape is the how-much loop of entry 85: the Account Manager OBSERVES the Data Clerk's answer for one name, ACTS by telling the user and asking, OBSERVES the reply, and goes on or stops. **THE MODEL WORDS EACH QUESTION; CODE CHECKS IT; CODE READS THE ANSWER.** The Account Manager's own model (`self.model`, the `account_manager` role) is given facts made by code (the name as typed, the Data Clerk's reason, the symbols that stay, the user's request) and returns `{"question": "..."}`; code accepts it only if it names the missing stock as the user typed it, is exactly one question, and is no longer than `missing.max_question_chars` (`check_wording`); a refused wording is asked for again `missing.max_problems` times; a wording that fails the check, a model that fails or takes more than `missing.max_seconds`, or no model at all never blocks the user: **the code's own sentence is shown** ("Chick-fil-a was not found: privately held, or not known to be listed. OK to continue without it? Your order would go on with F, GM, PG and GOOGL."). The model does not decide and does not read the answer: a yes or a no comes from the desk's `approver` (the channel that already carries a card with its own `question`, the Trader's funding card; no new argument on `build_desk`; the terminal approver now has `available()`, as the how-much channel does, so no model call is spent wording a question nobody is there to answer). The card is `{"kind": "missing_company", n, of, typed, reason, text (what is missing and what stays), question (with [y/N]), sentence, kept, wording: "model"|"code"}`.
- **A yes** drops that stock (and the next is asked about); after the last yes the order goes on as in Auto mode, with "at the user's word" in the note and the line. **A no, no answer, no approver, or nobody at the keyboard stops the order** and nothing is dropped on the user's behalf: Ask mode with nobody to ask is NOT Auto mode.
- **`name_the_stocks` cannot be talked into dropping**: `direct.name_the_stocks` refuses to go on while a missing stock is not in its `dropped` list, so no caller drops anything silently; `AccountManager.name_the_stocks` keeps its old signature (the problems only) beside `name_orders` (the problems, the orders that stay, the ones dropped).
- **The page** (`desk_run.missing_payload`, `ui/`): the card becomes a question card in the Activity panel, "ACCOUNT MANAGER ASKS" ("2 OF 3" when there are several), the Account Manager's sentence with the stock as typed in bold, **OK, continue without it** and **Stop the order**, and under them, in the monospace block, what stays on the order; the stage reads "waiting for you"; the feed says "You: continue without Chick-fil-a" and the Account Manager's line about what was left out. The work order table shows the stocks that were kept. The answers route needed no change (a `missing_company` answer is true or false).

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Refuse the whole list as before: Jacob asked for the opposite. (b) Drop without asking in Ask mode too: the user may have meant the missing one (a typo), and Jacob asked for the question there; in Auto mode he asked for the opposite. (c) Ask in Auto mode too (the first version of the design): Jacob, *"Auto mode will not ask any prompts"*. (d) Prompts worded by code only (the first version of the design): Jacob asked for the model; the code's sentence stays as the fallback and as the check's reference. (e) Let the model read the yes or no (as the how-much loop's model reads an amount): a yes or no needs no reading, and the model must not decide what is left out. (f) Offer to drop a stock whose LOOKUP failed: an outage is not a company to ignore. (g) Ask again for a corrected name ("did you mean ...?"): not built; a dropped company is dropped.

**Consequences.** (1) Jacob's request in Auto mode places F, GM, PG and GOOGL (a share of each in turn) and says Chick-fil-a was left out; in Ask mode it asks one question about Chick-fil-a, then the sizing is entry 98's (**with dollars and no "split evenly", Ask mode spends them on the first stock and the others say "no funds": the live run did exactly that, F 839 shares; whether Ask mode should spread a budget over several named stocks is Jacob's to say**). (2) Entry 88's "a stock that is not found stops the order with the Data Clerk's own message" is superseded for a list that has other stocks (the entry is not rewritten); a list with nothing found, a lookup that failed and two orders for one stock still stop, now with the request to clarify. (3) The Account Manager's model is called for wording only in Ask mode, only when somebody can answer, once for each missing stock (about 460 tokens in). (4) `DIRECT_OPTIONAL` gained `found_by` (entry 101). (5) Tests in `tests/test_direct_order.py` (the Data Clerk, the naming in both modes, the wording, the desk and two sessions on throwaway Postgres accounts) and `tests/test_web_live.py` (the card, the answers route, the last line of a stopped run), `ui/src/components/QuestionCard.test.tsx`; real captures: the two live sessions of Jacob's request on throwaway accounts (no fetch, deleted afterwards), `tests/fixtures/session_real/d_pyrett_names_ask.json` (Ask: the question about Chick-fil-a answered yes, F bought) and `d_pyrett_names_auto.json` (Auto: nothing asked, the Account Manager's model called once, to read the request; Chick-fil-a left out; F 23, GM 19, PG 18, GOOGL 17), and the models' replies `direct_order_names.json`; the web replay of both is in `tests/test_web_ui.py`.

## 103. A review that wants a person to look is a denial (Critic, Jacob, 2026-11-15)

**Problem.** A Critic finding could carry `requires_human` (a person should look at it before anything is traded): `control_failed`, `evidence_not_reviewed`, `fresh_negative_news`, `same_model_family` (evidence), `statements_changed`, `company_mismatch`, `identity_unverified` (accuracy). (A first version of this entry also listed `value_not_assessable`, a company with fewer than 126 stored bars; Jacob removed it the same day, see the correction at the end: it asks for nobody.) Such a review was APPROVED when it had no blocker and a score of 70 or more, and carried the flag beside the approval. The Account Manager then asked the user even in Auto mode (entry 56, `escalated: true`). That is a third state between approved and denied, and Jacob's design has two (*"if the Critic wants a person to review. Then Critic needs to Deny the recommendation and return the reason in the WorkOrder and update the recommendation status to Denied. The AccountManager should look for these denials when it reviews the results from the Critic"*).

**Decision** (`critic/scoring.py` `decide`, `needs_a_person`, `summarize`; `report.py`; `feedback.py`).
- A finding with `requires_human` is a reason to deny. `denial_reasons` gets **`needs_human_review`**, after any blocker codes and after `score_below_minimum`, and `approved` is false. `requires_human` and `requires_human_reasons` (the codes of those findings) stay on the review: they say which finding it was.
- **The score is not capped.** The cap (`blocked_score_cap`, 49) is for blockers, which say the recommendation is bad. This denial says the review could not be trusted without a person; the score stays what the six dimensions gave. A denied review has no `valid_until_utc`, like any denial.
- The summary says it in words, from fixed wording and the findings' own messages: `DENY PG: a person must look before this can trade: identity_unverified (PG could not be looked up in the symbol directory (the lookup failed (HTTP 429))).` With blockers the sentence says the blockers first and the person after them. The user's card (`report.scorecard`) has a line `DENIED needs_human_review: a person must look before this can trade: <codes>` where it had "needs your eyes before any trade".
- **The recommendation's status** is the `critic` block the Critic already writes on each record (`feedback.py`): `verdict.approved` false, `verdict.decision` "deny", the summary, `gate.denial_reasons`; `needs_human` still lists the finding. The review itself is on `order.reviews[]` with the same `denial_reasons` and `summary`. The page's panel says DENIED and `needs_human_review` where it said "NEEDS YOUR EYES".
- Nothing else about a finding changes: the same checks raise the same findings.

**Result on real data.** The real PG_open review of the real capture, reviewed while the symbol directory cannot be read (Finnhub's lookup failed, HTTP 429: `identity_unverified`), was approved at 88 beside the warning; it is denied at 88 (`needs_human_review`, score and `score_before_blockers` both 88, no `valid_until_utc`, the score not capped to the blockers' 49). With the directory read, the same review is approved. The Critic's other real tests whose review asked for a person are amended with a line saying why.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep the flag and ask the user in Auto mode (what it was; Jacob: *"Auto mode will not ask any prompts"*, entry 104). (b) Make `needs_human_review` a blocker (it would cap the score at 49 and say the recommendation is bad; the review only could not be trusted). (c) Drop the `requires_human` flag (the finding that asked for the person would be lost; it is what the reason names).

**Consequences.** (1) A recommendation whose value cannot be judged, whose quotes the model could not read, whose company name does not match the directory, whose filing has been replaced, or that has newer bad news, is returned to the user as denied and is never traded, in either mode (entry 56: a denial is final). (2) A Critic whose model is from the same producer as the Advisor's (`same_model_family`: the model id before the slash is the same) denies EVERY review it makes. That is on purpose (Jacob: the Critic is a different model so that its verdict is independent); the desk's own pair, GLM 5.3 and gpt-oss-120b, are different producers, so none is. (3) The scores of the real captures are unchanged. (4) Tests: `tests/test_critic_scoring.py`, `tests/test_critic_real.py` (the new test names the work order's review, the recommendation's block and the card).

**Correction, 2026-11-15 (Part F of `design/approved_or_denied_spec.md`; this entry is part of the same uncommitted change, so it is corrected here and not by a new one).** `value_not_assessable` does NOT ask for a person, and so it denies nothing. A company with fewer than 126 stored daily bars has no six-month average, and a rule that denied it would have kept every recent IPO from ever trading: Jacob, *"remove this rule about a company less than 126 bars. This does not matter because it eliminates IPOs from being traded and is a bad rule to be removed from code and tests"*. The finding stays as a plain warning on the review ("The price cannot be judged against a six-month average: ...", `critic/value.py`), the value score is worked out as it was when a measure is unknown (the unknown credit), and the real PG_open review with no six-month average is approved at 89 again. THE FINDINGS THAT STILL ASK FOR A PERSON, and so deny, are seven: `identity_unverified`, `company_mismatch`, `statements_changed` (accuracy); `evidence_not_reviewed`, `control_failed`, `fresh_negative_news`, `same_model_family` (evidence). `same_model_family` is NOT changed.

## 104. Auto mode trades what the Critic approves and asks nothing; there is no second mark; the Account Manager returns what is denied (Jacob, 2026-11-15)

**Problem.** In Auto mode an APPROVED recommendation under a second mark (`auto_min_score`, 80 in `configs/critic_v1.json`, or the profile's own, or a session override) was not traded: the Account Manager asked the user (`escalated: true`), and so it did for a review that asked for a person and for every sale of the whole holding. Jacob (2026-11-15): *"Auto mode will not ask any prompts"*, and, asked what Auto does with an approved recommendation under the mark: *"It depends if the Strict recommendations is enabled. Trade all approved recommendations in auto mode."* Strict (the page's checkbox) was also lowering that mark. And the denied recommendations were only shown in the report; they were not returned to the user by the Account Manager.

**Decision.**
- **The decision table** (`account_manager/decisions.py` `decide_trades`), for each review, the first row that fits: not approved: `no_system_denied` (final; the reason is the Critic's own summary; the user is never asked to override it). Approved but expired: `no_expired`. Approved and not one share fits: `no_funds`. **Approved, Auto mode: `yes_auto`, nothing asked**, with the reason "approved by the Critic (score 86): Auto mode trades what the Critic approves". Approved, Ask mode: the user's answer (`yes_user`, `no_user_denied`, `pending_user` when nobody is there; entry 79 is unchanged). A review whose action is not a purchase has nothing to trade (entry 105).
- **The second mark is removed everywhere.** `decide_trades` has no `auto_min_score`; `_why_user` and every "escalated to the user in Auto mode" path are gone; `UserProfile.auto_min_score` (the profile forbids unknown fields, so a profile that still names it is refused, as entry 91 did for the limits), `verdict.auto_min_score` in `configs/critic_v1.json` and its model, the `critic` block's `gate.auto_min_score`, `session --auto-min-score` and `with_overrides` (and the flag of `test_prometheus.sh`), the web bridge's `RunSettings.auto_min_score` and the page's use of it. **`escalated` is removed from every decision** (the account book has no column for it: `recommendation_decisions` holds the will-trade state, the mode, the reason and the date). The real captures still hold the old keys (`critic.gate.auto_min_score`, `decision.escalated: false`): they are not edited, and the tests that compare a re-made decision with a real one say so.
- **The account book.** `sql/prometheus_accounts.sql` no longer has the `auto_min_score` column and drops it from an existing book (`DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS`, as entry 91 did the four limit columns); `persistence/accounts.py` no longer reads it. **It was NOT applied to the running local container**: the schema file is safe to run again (it never drops a table and the setup script seeds the three samples only when none exists), but the web server that was running had the OLD code, which reads that column and would fail on every profile load until it was restarted. The code does not need the column either way. `bash scripts/setup_postgres.sh` applies it, after the server is restarted.
- **Strict** (the page's checkbox) now means exactly what Jacob said: only what the Critic approves trades, and a neutral recommendation never passes. `web/settings.run_settings(strict, neutral)` returns only whether a neutral recommendation counts as approved (`RunSettings.allow_neutral`; `neutral_locked` went: the page decides what it shows). A neutral recommendation (entry 91) is unchanged: with the setting on and Strict off it counts as approved; Auto trades it, Ask asks.
- **The denied recommendations are returned to the user by the Account Manager**, right after it decides (`decisions.denied_lines`, printed under the decision node by `session.decision_lines`, which the page's feed shows too): `DENIED by the Critic, not traded:` and for each `SYMBOL score N (G): <the Critic's reason>`. When everything was denied the session's verdict says so in its FIRST reason, with the reasons (`session.why_not`; the page's `run_finished` headline repeats it).
- Not in this entry: the Trader's question about the sells that pay for a purchase (RaiseFunds, `require_user_approval` in `configs/trader_v1.json`) is the Account Manager's own business and is not on the session's or the page's path. It is left as it is (Jacob to decide).

**Result on real data** (`bob_jones_oil.json`: XOM approved at 86, EOG approved at 76, APA denied for its risk; a conservative 60-year-old, $15,000, Auto). Decided again with no mark and an approver that fails the test when called: XOM 46 shares and EOG 45 shares `yes_auto` (round-robin, nothing asked), APA `no_system_denied`, and the Account Manager says `APA    score 49 (F): The risk is medium (29.8), above the low level a conservative profile accepts (risk_above_profile).` Before, an approval at 70 to 79 (EOG at 76) went to the user when the mark was 80. **A real Auto session of the new desk** (2026-11-15, throwaway account, no fetch, deleted afterwards, `tests/fixtures/session_real/auto_oil_no_second_mark.json`; a conservative 60-year-old, $15,000, "the 3 most recommended oil companies, spread evenly"): EOG was approved at 79 and CVX at 86 and both were `yes_auto` with nothing asked (EOG at 79 is what the old mark of 80 would have sent to the user); COP was DENIED with TWO reasons, `risk_above_profile` (a blocker, which capped its score from 65 to 49) and `needs_human_review` (`fresh_negative_news`), and the Account Manager returned it: `COP    score 49 (F): The risk is medium (20.3), above the low level a conservative profile accepts (risk_above_profile); a person must look before this can trade: fresh_negative_news (...)`. EOG 47 and CVX 34 shares were bought and booked. (The same run showed something that is NOT this entry's: the six companies the Advisor scored NEUTRAL were decided as approved, because `ALLOW_NEUTRAL_RECOMMENDATIONS` is on and a theme run puts every company it found in the order's subject, which `is_neutral_candidate` reads as "named by the user"; after the two approved purchases had spent the cash, CNQ bought 1 share and five were rejected `insufficient_cash`. Entry 91 meant a company the user NAMED; Jacob then settled it the other way, entry 107: every neutral company counts, whoever named it, but the number the request asked for is kept and the neutral ones are sized with the approved ones.)

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Ask the user in Auto mode for an approval under the mark, for a finding that asks for a person, and for every sale (what it was). (b) Keep a second mark but let Strict lower it (the page's first reading): Jacob wants one verdict, approved or denied. (c) Let the user override a denial in Ask mode (never: entry 56). (d) Keep `escalated` always false for old readers (nobody reads it; a key that is always false says something it does not mean). (e) Apply the schema change to the running container (see above).

**Consequences.** (1) In Auto mode every approved purchase trades, at any approved score. (2) `UserProfile` and the account book have no mark: an account has a trade mode and nothing else about when to ask. (3) The page's Strict checkbox changes only the neutral rule. (4) `design/ui_v1_spec.md` 5.3 and `HowToRun.txt` are updated. (5) Tests: `tests/test_approved_or_denied.py` (Auto asks nobody on the real session, the denied company in the Account Manager's words, everything-denied reasons), `tests/test_account_manager_decisions.py` and `tests/test_session_replay.py` (amended, each with a line saying why).

## 105. The Advisor and the Critic never touch the holdings: the Advisor is given no portfolio and recommends only purchases, and a sale comes only from the user's own order or RaiseFunds (Jacob, 2026-11-15)

**Problem.** The Advisor was given the user's portfolio and ran a "holdings pass" (step 9): a held name was reduced on a red flag, a bearish event, an invalidated earlier recommendation or a family that stopped passing, added to when it was bullish and as good as this run's new ideas, and otherwise a `hold` candidate; a `reduce` was a plan with an entry zone and nothing else. The Critic reviewed those (a reduction was "not judged" for risk, value and chart, its stance direction was bearish) and read the cost basis once (`adding_above_cost`). The Account Manager sold the WHOLE holding for a `reduce`, asking the user even in Auto mode. Jacob: *"The Advisor never recommends reducing a holding or any other portfolio related matters. Only the AccountManager can raise funds and recommend this. The Critic never will approve or deny (judge) such a decision that affects the User's holdings. The Advisor and the Critic only approve and deny recommendations and never any holdings or other portfolio actions."* and, asked whether `add` should stay: *"no, remove the add method and any other Advisor method which adds and manages holdings. The Advisor and Critic only add recommendations and updates the recommendations section with the scoring, the actual recommendation, the status, and other metadata. But never any portfolio related information."*

**Decision.**
- **The Advisor is given no portfolio.** `Advisor.generate_recommendations(order, case, today, top_k, only_subject)`; the argument is gone from `recommend.generate`, `candidates.collect_candidates` and every caller (the desk's Advisor node, `application/recommendations.py`, `scripts/capture_advisor_fixture.py`, the tests). A held company the user names is an ordinary candidate `{symbol, company, origin}`: no `origin: "held"`, no `held` tag, no company name taken from a holding, and no room taken from the new ideas. Step 9 is only the cooldown and the selection of the new ideas: `ranking.holding_action` and `add_floor` are removed. `trade_plan.build` needs the strategy a purchase is made on (the `family=None` plan of a reduction is gone). The plan prompt no longer says "held by the user"; the thesis prompt says no amount "to buy" (not "to buy or sell") and the digest says `Decision: buy (...)`. The runner's report loses its "YOUR HOLDINGS" section.
- **The record is version 3** (`advisor/record.py`): `Action.action` is `"open"` and nothing else; `held_shares`, `cost_basis`, `unrealized_pct` and `Candidate.held` are gone; `PortfolioFit` is gone, name and all (its two numbers were never computed), and its note (how this run's own picks sit together) is `Recommendation.concentration.note`, beside the `concentrated` flag of the ranking. No key of a version 3 record or candidate says portfolio, held, holding, cost_basis or unrealized (a test reads the models' fields and the real records). The sort "a reduction first, then additions" is gone. The ledger's cooldown (`ranking.cooldown_of`) stays and still reads old lines whose action is `add`.
- **A version 2 record still loads and a record about a holding does not.** The models drop the retired keys when they hold the not-held values (`held_shares` 0, `cost_basis` and `unrealized_pct` null, the two `portfolio_fit` numbers null, `held` false), read `portfolio_fit.note` as `concentration.note`, and REFUSE (with the reason) a record whose action is not `open`, that says something is held, or whose `portfolio_fit` carries a number. The real captures of `tests/fixtures/session_real/` are version 2 and are not edited.
- **The Critic judges recommendations to buy and nothing else.** A record whose action is not `open` is not reviewed: it is a `record_defect` ("The record is not a recommendation to buy (its action is 'reduce'): the Critic does not judge a holding or a sale."), denied, with no check made and no request to the Data Clerk; a record that says something is held fails the models' check with its reason. Gone: the `adding_above_cost` warning (the one place the Critic read the cost basis) and `value.add_above_cost`, the `opening = action in ("open", "add")` switches of `timing`, `integrity`, `evidence` and `recheck`, "not judged for a reduction" in `risk`, `chart`, `value` and the card, the bearish stance direction of a reduce, "reduction of", "sell the holding", "add to the position" and "the whole holding" in the summary and the report, and the action word in the model's prompt (it is told another agent recommends to BUY). `review.py` still copies the action (`open`) onto the review, because the decisions read it. A test fails if a Critic module names `held_shares`, `cost_basis`, `unrealized_pct`, `held` or `portfolio_fit`.
- **What each may write on the work order.** The Advisor writes `order.recommendations`, `order.candidates`, `order.advisor_runs` and the status (and the metrics it asked the Data Clerk for, on `order.data`); the Critic writes `order.reviews`, `order.critic_runs`, the status it puts on each recommendation (`recommendation["critic"]`) and the order's status, and never changes what the Advisor wrote. A test runs each on a real capture and compares the rest of the work order.
- **The words `reduce` and `add` stay in ONE place: the Account Manager's own decisions for the user's own orders** (`direct.py`: a direct sell is `reduce`, a direct purchase of something held is `add`). `decisions.py` has no `reduce` branch on the Critic's path: every review is a purchase and is sized, and a review whose action is not a purchase has nothing to trade. `work_order.verify_trade` and `verify_trades`/`verify_fill` accept a SALE only for the user's own direct order (a decision with `origin: "direct"`) or a funding sale of RaiseFunds, and refuse a decision of a recommendation whose action is not `open` and a filled sale tied to a recommendation. `is_neutral_candidate` no longer reads `held`: a company the user named that the Advisor scored neutral counts as approved whether or not the user already holds some (the Account Manager sizes the purchase, and knows). `trader/tickets.py`: the branch that cut a recommended sale to the shares sellable today is gone; a sell ticket is a direct order's (whole or not at all) or a funding sale's. The desk's Data Clerk node still refreshes the bars of the holdings when asked to fetch: that is for the Account Manager's prices of what is held, not for the Advisor.

**Result on real data** (the three Advisor captures, made with the sample portfolio MSFT, TSLA, GM and replayed with no portfolio, free). SNDK: before, `GM add (60, rank 1)`, `SNDK open (59, rank 2)` and the `hold` candidates MSFT (64) and TSLA (43); now `SNDK open (59, rank 1)` and no candidate. PG: before, `PG open (79)` and the candidates MSFT, GM (60, hold), ADDYY (54, freshness) and TSLA; now `PG open (79)` and ADDYY only. PG_open: before, `PG open (73)` with MSFT, GM and TSLA as `hold` candidates; now `PG open (73)` and no candidate. The scores, the ranks of the new ideas and every number of the records of SNDK and PG are the same.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) The Advisor keeps the portfolio but only labels a purchase of a held company `add` (Jacob turned it down: *"remove the add method and any other Advisor method which adds and manages holdings"*). (b) Keep `reduce` and ask the user in Auto mode (what it was; entry 104). (c) Keep `portfolio_fit` with only its note (the key says portfolio: Jacob wants none on the record). (d) Keep the recommended sale and let the Critic approve it (the Critic never judges a holding). (e) Bump nothing and keep schema 2 (a reader could not tell a record that may say `add` from one that may not).

**Consequences.** (1) There is no way for a recommendation to sell: the user's own order ("Sell 10 shares of Tesla") and the Account Manager's RaiseFunds are the only sales. (2) A company the user holds and names is recommended (or not) like any other, and the purchase of more of it is a purchase. (3) The Advisor's run is the same whatever the account: a replay no longer depends on a portfolio file (the captures still name the sample one; it is ignored). (4) The Critic's `applicable` flag of a dimension stays in the review (stored reviews carry it) but no check sets it false. (5) `sql/seed_test_users.sql` is not changed: the three samples' decision rows still say `add` and `reduce` (the Account Manager's own history). (6) Amended with a line each: the tests of `ranking`, `candidates`, `advisor_v1`, `advisor_sndk_pg`, `advisor_no_size`, `trade_plan_real` (the 26 `|reduce|` plans of the golden file are not compared: the file is a capture), `recommendations`, `desk_critic`, `desk_trader`, the Critic's tests of value, chart, risk, evidence, feedback, scoring and the review, `account_manager_decisions`, `account_manager_balance`, `neutral_recommendation`, `work_order_critic`, `trader_tickets`, `trader_broker`, `session_replay`, and `tests/trader_helpers.py` (a sale in the Trader's tests is the user's own direct order), `tests/simple_critic.py`. New tests: `tests/test_approved_or_denied.py`.

## 106. With no split asked the money is spread round-robin in BOTH modes; `as_much_as_possible` is gone (Jacob, 2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Jacob's request typed into the page in Ask mode (*"Buy as much stock in: F, GM, Procter and gamble and Google. And Chick-fil-a."*, $10,000) put the whole $10,000 into the first stock: F 839 shares, and GM, PG and GOOGL "no funds" (the real `tests/fixtures/session_real/d_pyrett_names_ask.json`). The same request in Auto mode was spread round-robin: F 23, GM 19, PG 18, GOOGL 17 (`d_pyrett_names_auto.json`). The default allocation depended on the mode: Auto chose `round_robin`, Ask chose `None`, which was "as much as possible, in rank order". Jacob: *"No. its suppose to be round-robin. Not all in one. ... fix this"*.

**Decision** (`sizing.py`, `decisions.py`, `account_manager.py` `_size_direct` and `clarify_sizing`, `clarify.py`, `prompts/account_manager_sizing.py`).
- When the user did not say how to spread the money over several purchases it is spread **round-robin in both modes**: one share of each purchase in turn, in rank order, until the funds are used. "Split it evenly" still gives equal dollars. Never everything into the first. The default is chosen where it was: `decisions.decide_trades` (`balance or "round_robin"`), `AccountManager._size_direct` (the same), and `sizing.size_purchases` (a left-out allocation is round-robin).
- **`as_much_as_possible` is removed as an allocation** (`sizing.ALLOCATIONS` is `("even", "round_robin")`, `Sized.allocation` says one of those, and the old word is refused as an argument): nothing needs it, and one purchase alone is the same under the round robin. What the Account Manager shows the model and the user of "what your cash buys of each company" is now worked out one company at a time (each alone, with all the cash), because it is not how the money will be spread.
- **The how-much loop** (`clarify.py` and its prompt): `SizingChoice.allocation` is `even`, `round_robin` or None (the user did not say); the prompt tells the model to leave it out unless the person said to split evenly; `check_choice` refuses `even` without the user's words (as before) and refuses `round_robin` over a split the REQUEST already asked for; a plan with no allocation of its own keeps the request's split, else leaves the default (round-robin) to the sizing. **The real recorded replies** of `clarify_ask_mode.json` say `"allocation": "as_much_as_possible"`: they are still read (a before-validator reads the old word as None, "the user did not say how to spread it"), and are not edited.
- An amount for EACH company ("10 shares each") is still each company's own cap. The neutral purchases keep their rule (entry 91; sizing them with the approved ones is entry 107; entry 109 took their own default away). Ask mode is otherwise entry 79: the sizes are made first, the user sees the whole proposal and approves each trade, nothing is re-split after an answer. Nothing here is a limit: the funds covering the order is still the only check.

**Result on real data.** The real desk over Jacob's request in Ask mode, with the same stored closes: F 23, GM 19, PG 18, GOOGL 17, none `no_funds`, each asked about and bought, exactly what Auto mode did (before: F 839 and three "no funds"). On `bob_jones_oil.json` with a budget of $2,800 and no "evenly", Ask mode is XOM 8 and EOG 9 (before: XOM 16, EOG "no funds"); "split evenly" is still $1,400 each.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep the Ask default and say so to the user (Jacob turned it down). (b) Make Ask mode ask how to spread it every time (it asks how much, not how: entry 85). (c) Keep `as_much_as_possible` for one purchase (the round robin gives the same).

**Consequences.** (1) The sum of the purchases never exceeds the cash by construction, in both modes: "each purchase may use all the funds" (entry 79's wording) no longer holds for the sizes, though no cash is reserved against an answer and the Trader's handshake still meets the cash at each trade. (2) The default size shown when the how-much loop gives up is the round robin, and its message says so. (3) A model that still says `as_much_as_possible` is read, not refused. (4) Tests amended with a line each: the sizing's, the clarify loop's (including a new one on the retired word read from the real replies), `test_session_replay.py`, `test_direct_order.py` (Jacob's desk in Ask mode), `test_recommendations_trader.py`, `test_account_manager_decisions.py`; new: `tests/test_approved_or_denied.py` (Jacob's request in Ask mode sized as Auto did; equal dollars still equal in Ask mode).

## 107. A neutral recommendation is an approval, in full: every neutral company counts, the number asked for is kept, and they are sized with the approved ones (Jacob, 2026-11-15)

**Problem.** The real Auto session `tests/fixtures/session_real/auto_oil_no_second_mark.json` asked for "the 3 most recommended oil companies ... evenly among the 3". The Advisor recommended EOG, CVX and COP; the Critic approved EOG and CVX and denied COP. Then SIX neutral companies (CNQ, OXY, BP, META, VLO, FANG) were each decided `yes_auto` as well: EIGHT purchases for a request of three. The approved two were sized over all the $15,000 (EOG 47, CVX 34) and the neutral six were sized over the same $15,000 again, so the Trader filled CNQ with 1 share and rejected the other five `insufficient_cash`. Two causes: a theme's companies were all "named" (`origin == "subject"`), so any neutral one counted, and nothing kept the number asked for; and the neutral decisions had a sizing of their own. Jacob: *"neutral passes are driven by the UI checkbox setting for: ALLOW_NEUTRAL_RECOMMENDATIONS=true which will treat neutral as a Approval (pass). This is for both modes. Neutral applies to all recommendations regardless of who typed the company."*

**Decision** (`work_order.is_neutral_candidate`, `account_manager/neutral.py`, `decisions.decide_trades`, `AccountManager.decide_trades`, `langgraph/desk.py`).
1. **Every company the Advisor scored neutral counts, whoever named it.** `is_neutral_candidate` drops `origin == "subject"`: a neutral stance and no failed gate is all it needs (a failed gate is still a refusal and a bearish stance still trades nothing; a Critic denial is still final). The words "a company the user named" are gone from `neutral.py`, the Account Manager's text and `verify_trade`'s permission.
2. **The number the user asked for is kept** (`neutral.choose`). When the request said how many companies (the form's own `count`, which the desk passes as `plan["count"]`: NOT `plan["find"]`, which is 3 when the request gave no number, for the Advisor, and must not cut), at most that many are bought: the Critic's approved recommendations first, in rank order, then the neutral companies, the Advisor's best score first, until the number is reached. A denied recommendation does not count. A neutral company beyond the number gets NO decision, and the Account Manager tells the user so ("OXY was scored neutral by the Advisor (score 66) and counts as approved, but it is not needed: 3 companies were asked for, ..."), on the screen and as a note on the order's data. A request with no number cuts nothing.
3. **They are sized together.** An approved purchase and a neutral one are the same to the Account Manager: ONE call of `sizing.size_purchases` over the approved ones and then the neutral ones (ranked after them), so "evenly among the 3" is three equal amounts and the default is one round-robin over all (entry 106). The neutral decision keeps `origin: "neutral"`, its own permission check (`verify_trade`) and its own `valid_until_utc`; its separate sizing (`neutral.decide_neutral`, `AccountManager._decide_neutral`) is gone. Ask mode asks the user about each, in one proposal and one set of how-much questions; Auto mode trades them and asks nothing.
- **One place keeps entry 91's equal dollars:** a run in which EVERY purchase is neutral, the user gave no split and no shares (the real `pam_smith_neutral.json`: MU 32, AMD 46). A mixed run, with an approved purchase, is the one round robin (or the even split asked for).

**Result on real data** (the real oil session decided again, free, with the stored closes of that Friday): before, eight decisions: EOG 47 ($7,441.04), CVX 34 ($7,476.26), COP denied, CNQ 50, OXY 42, BP 54, META 3, VLO 6, FANG 13, and at the Trader CNQ filled 1 and five rejected for cash. After, with the request's own `count` 3 and "evenly": EOG 31 ($4,907.92), CVX 22 ($4,837.58), COP denied, CNQ 100 ($4,975.00): three equal amounts of $5,000, all three filled by the Trader, none rejected, $272.13 left; OXY, BP, META, VLO and FANG have no decision and are reported as neutral and not needed. With the setting off, EOG and CVX at $7,500 each and nothing neutral. `pam_smith_neutral.json` still gives MU 32 and AMD 46.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep "named by the user" and mark a theme's companies as not named (it would need a flag nobody can check, and Jacob said whoever typed it). (b) Cut the approved recommendations too when there are more than the number (the Advisor already returns at most `find`). (c) Size the neutral ones after the approved ones over what is left of the cash (two sizings and an ordering of money: what failed). (d) Keep each neutral purchase's own equal-dollars rule in mixed runs.

**Consequences.** (1) A theme request with the setting on buys exactly the number asked for, from the approved and then the best neutral scores. (2) A neutral purchase and an approved one can share one proposal in Ask mode. (3) `record_user_decision` still needs a review, so a neutral decision left `pending_user` cannot be answered later (as before). (4) Tests amended with a line each (`test_neutral_recommendation.py`: the candidates, the report line, the Ask reason, the source test) and new ones in the same file (the real oil session decided again and traded, the setting off, no number, the count and a denied recommendation, the desk passing the form's own count).

**Amended 2026-11-15 (entries 108 and 109).** (1) "One place keeps entry 91's equal dollars" (point 3) is gone: a run of neutral companies alone is round-robin (entry 109). (2) The even split spends what the equal amounts leave over (entry 108), so the real oil session decided again is EOG 31 ($4,907.92), CVX 23 ($5,057.47) and CNQ 101 ($5,024.75), $9.86 left of the $15,000, where this entry says EOG 31, CVX 22 and CNQ 100 and a leftover of $279.50 at the sizing's prices ($272.13 was the cash after the Trader's fills at the quote). The three amounts are still "about $5,000". The numbers above are those of the code of that day.

## 108. An even split spends what is left over, one share at a time to the smallest amount (Jacob, 2026-11-15)

**Problem.** `sizing._split_evenly` gave each purchase the whole shares its equal amount buys and left the rest unspent: the real oil session ("evenly among the 3", $15,000) bought EOG $4,907.92, CVX $4,837.58 and CNQ $4,975.00 and left $279.50 as cash, enough for another CVX share. Jacob, confirming that a neutral purchase is sized with the approved ones: *"Yes, try to split the money evenly as much as possible. ... Split evenly will try to split the holdings so the balance of each holding is as close as possible. ... $30000 split evenly is $10000 for stock x, $10000 for stock b, $10000 for stock c and the remainder is again divided equally as close as possible."* Asked whether that means fractional shares, he chose whole shares now and fractional shares next, as their own change (`design/fractional_shares_spec.md`).

**Decision** (`sizing._split_evenly`; the design is `design/approved_or_denied_spec.md` section 4d). An even split has two steps.
1. **The equal amount, as before.** The pot divided by the purchases in the split, never more than the smallest amount any of them was asked for; a purchase whose equal amount buys no share is dropped (`no_room`) and the others are balanced again; each takes the whole shares the equal amount buys.
2. **New: the leftover is spent one share at a time.** Each turn, among the purchases still in the split that have fewer shares than the user asked for and whose NEXT share costs no more than the leftover, the one with the SMALLEST amount so far takes one share (a tie: rank order). It stops when none can; what is then left stays as cash and the notes say how much. Money is counted in `Decimal` cents the way the round robin counts it (`_next_share` is the one helper of both: a share costs `trade_amount(n + 1, price) - trade_amount(n, price)`), so a purchase costs exactly `trade_amount(shares, price)` and the total never passes the pot. Step 2 does NOT run when a purchase's smaller ask held the equal amount down (`binding: "even_split"`: the rest is not the split's to spend), and a purchase dropped in step 1 stays dropped. `Sized` keeps its fields (`target_dollars` and `of` are step 1's); the notes say how many shares came from the leftover and what is left. Every share count stays a whole number. The default is still round-robin (entry 106) and `even` only comes from the user's words (and from entry 109, nothing else).

**Result on real data.** The real oil session decided again (the stored closes of that Friday, "evenly among the 3", `count` 3): EOG 31 ($4,907.92), CVX 23 ($5,057.47), CNQ 101 ($5,024.75) against EOG 31, CVX 22 ($4,837.58) and CNQ 100 ($4,975.00): the leftover of $279.50 became $9.86. Against the equal $5,000 each amount is now within $93 of it (EOG $92.08 under, CVX $57.47 over, CNQ $24.75 over; the three gaps add up to $174.30, where they were $279.50 before, CVX being $162.42 under); the spread from the smallest amount to the largest is $149.55, where it was $137.42, because the purchase with the smallest amount takes the next share even when that carries it past the target. That is the rule as Jacob's spec states it, not a defect, and it is the one thing in the result that is not strictly "closer together". All three were still filled by the Trader. `bob_jones_oil.json` over $2,800 evenly: XOM 8 and EOG 9 ($2,789.60) where the live run placed 8 and 8; $4,500 over PG, MSFT and GM: PG 8, MSFT 3, GM 18, $21.96 left (it was PG 8, MSFT 2, GM 17, $631.84 left).

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) *Divide the leftover by the number of purchases and repeat* (Jacob's own words, read literally): in whole shares an equal slice of a small leftover buys only the cheapest stock, so repeating the division feeds the cheapest purchase and makes the split LESS even; "the smallest amount first" is that second division done as closely as whole shares allow. (b) *Fractional shares now:* Jacob chose whole shares now and put fractional shares next. Until then a purchase whose equal amount buys no share is still dropped (entry 80's rule, unchanged). (c) *Spend the leftover in rank order:* it feeds the first stock, which is not "as close as possible".

**Consequences.** (1) An even split rarely leaves more than the price of one share of the cheapest purchase in it. (2) A share taken from the leftover can put a purchase above the equal amount (CVX above $5,000 here); it is the rule, not a defect. (3) The report says "as far as whole shares allow (what the equal amounts leave over buys more shares, the smallest amount first)" in place of "the same dollars for every company". (4) Tests: `test_account_manager_sizing.py` (the leftover by hand on real prices, a dropped purchase staying dropped even when the leftover would buy it, the invariants over every group of three to six real companies and pots from $400 to $6,000: the total never above the pot, nothing under its ask could take another share, each at least what step 1 gave it), amended ones with a line each in `test_account_manager_balance.py`, `test_approved_or_denied.py`, `test_neutral_recommendation.py`, `test_session_replay.py` and `handshake_helpers.py` (the handshake tests need XOM 8 and EOG 8: their budget is $2,750 now), and `test_account_manager_intent.py` on the page's own default request, read once by the real model as `allocation: even`, `count: 3`, `budget: 3000` (`tests/fixtures/session_real/page_default_request_form.json`; the request prompt did not need to change).

## 109. One default for every run: round-robin, neutral-only runs too, and "split evenly" in the user's words overrides it (Jacob, 2026-11-15)

**Problem.** Entry 91 gave a run in which every purchase is neutral (Pam's MU and AMD, no split asked) equal dollars, and entry 107 kept that as the one exception to the round robin. Jacob: *"use round-robin for neutral-only runs too. Use split evenly if the user specifies it in the prompt and this will override the round-robin if the prompt specifies it."*

**Decision** (`decisions.decide_trades`, `neutral.py`). ONE rule for how the money is spread, whatever the purchases are (approved, neutral or both) and whatever the mode: **round-robin** unless the user's own words ask for an even split (in the request, or in the answer to the how-much question), and then the even split of entry 108. The default passed to the sizing is `balance or "round_robin"`; the neutral-only case (`"even" if only_neutral and not wanted`) is gone, and so is the paragraph of `neutral.py` that gave the neutral kind its own default. Nothing else chooses an allocation.

**Result on real data.** `pam_smith_neutral.json` (the live run: MU 32 and AMD 46 in equal dollars, $502.59 left after the fills) decided again with the same request, which did not ask for an even split: MU 38 and AMD 38 (a share of each in turn: 38 rounds of $1,312.37 cost $49,870.06 and the $129.94 left buys neither; MU $778.31, AMD $534.06). With "split evenly" asked the live run's MU 32 and AMD 46 come back (the $527.32 the equal amounts leave over buys neither share). With $10,000: MU 8 and AMD 7 round-robin, MU 6 and AMD 9 evenly. With $600 only AMD buys a share (1) round-robin; evenly, neither does.

**Alternatives rejected.** (a) Keep equal dollars for a run of neutral companies alone (entry 91; Jacob turned it down). (b) Ask the user how to spread it (entry 85 asks how much, not how).

**Consequences.** (1) The neutral kind has no allocation of its own.
