Auto mode asks nothing: with the words or the setting it sells and buys (Jacob: *"yes, Auto mode can sell when I say raise funds"*), without them it sells nothing and says what to add. The shortfall (R1) is what the direct buys placed now cost less `cash_avail`, or, on the recommendation path (R10-R11, Jacob: yes), the typed shares (entry 140 Part C) of every approved or neutral purchase, within the budget. Before any question `sizing.funds_check` says whether the holdings could pay (R8). A funded decision keeps the order as typed, never cut, with `funding: {shortfall, plan_id, by}`; the funds node now sits on every desk with the Critic and the Trader (edges only when a permitted decision carries `funding`); RaiseFunds runs ONCE for the whole shortfall (`rf.Problem.also`: no stock being bought is ever sold); the Trader shows the plan (card `funding`: holdings, shares, prices, gain, cash after; skipped only in Auto with permission) and places the sales first and the funded purchase before anything else, one trade at a time through the handshake. The offline evaluator `within_pot` counts what a filled funding sale raised as money of the run.

**Alternatives.** Auto mode selling without the words or the setting (rejected: entries 104 and 105); cutting the order to the cash (a direct order is as typed); one plan for each purchase (the second would meet the cash the first spent); the model wording the question (code writes it).

**Consequences.** The real live capture `tests/fixtures/session_real/ant_raise_funds_ask.json` (a throwaway copy of account 6553, Ask mode, a scripted yes): RaiseFunds sold 1 DPZ at $348.61 (cost $341.72, a gain of $6.89), then bought 2 ANT at $88.91 = $177.82; cash $60.14 to $230.93; the sale booked first. Eight existing tests changed value (the graph's new node and edges, the web health defaults and settings fields, the offline evaluator's capture counts 16 to 17). Not built: counting the user's own sale in the same request as money raised (the plan may raise a little more), funding a recommendation run where only some purchases have typed shares, a waiting limit order (never funded), and a real broker's "sales stay, purchase refused" path. A "no" to the question for an order in dollars still sizes to the cash, as before.


## 143. The four open ends of raising funds: the user's own sale, some purchases typed, a waiting order, a sale that fills lower (Jacob, 2026-11-21)

**Problem.** Entry 142 named four things it did not build: (S) the user's own sale in the same request was not counted as money for the purchase, so RaiseFunds could sell more than needed, and the share the user sold could be sold again by the plan; (P) a recommendation run where only SOME purchases have typed shares was not funded at all; (W) a waiting limit order (entry 140) the cash does not cover was refused at placement; (B) on a real broker a sale could fill for less than planned, and the funnel had no path for "the sales stay, the purchase is refused" (paper trading cannot reach it). Jacob: "Go ahead and build these", and for W, when asked, "Sell when the price is reached".

**Decision.**
- **S1-S3, the user's own sale counts.** `AccountManager._own_sales` finds the user's own direct sales of the request that are placed now (typed shares, priced, not waiting, held and sellable today) and the account as it will be once they are filled. Their estimated proceeds (`trade_amount` at the quote) count as cash for a purchase of the same request (`direct.decide_direct(..., sale_proceeds=)`: a purchase the cash alone does not pay for but cash plus the sale does is `yes_user`, "paid for with the cash and about $X from your own sale (placed first)", no RaiseFunds, no question), the shortfall of a funded purchase is its cost less the cash AND the sale, and RaiseFunds works from the account once the sale is filled (`_after_decided_sales`), so it never sells the share the user sold. The Trader already places every sale before any purchase, so the sale is cash when the purchase is approved; the approval still reads the cash the book shows then, so nothing is spent that is not there (entry 111). The funding card and the question show "your own sale of 1 MCD brings about $273.24". A sale the account cannot make brings nothing.
- **P1, some purchases typed.** When the user permitted raising funds and the typed shares cost more than the cash, the typed purchases are funded in full as before (the shortfall is their cost less the cash, within the budget), and a purchase with NO typed shares (an approved or a neutral one) gets NOTHING, with a reason that says so and what to say ("Say how many shares of EOG you want"): it would be sized by the round robin to whatever is left, and money RAISED by selling must never reach it. The question of Ask mode says it too. Chosen because it is the smallest sale and the user's own numbers come first; the alternative, spending the cash on the untyped purchases and raising the whole typed cost, sells more. Without permission nothing changes: the round robin with the cash, as ever.
- **W-F1 to W-F5, a waiting order is paid for when its price is reached.** A direct PURCHASE that waits for its limit and that the free cash does not cover waits anyway when the user permitted it by their WORDS or by ALWAYS_RAISE_FUNDS (no question: at the moment of the sale nobody is there to approve a plan; Ask mode without the words is refused for want of cash as before and the reason says what to add), and only when the holdings that may be sold could pay for it at its limit (R8). It sets aside the cash that is FREE (it may be none) and carries the permission (`trading.open_orders.raise_funds_by`, "words" or "setting"). NOTHING is sold while it waits and nothing is sold if it expires. When the watcher meets the limit, `fill_open_order` works out the shortfall at THAT price (its cost less the free cash and its own hold), runs RaiseFunds once with the Account Manager's model (the watcher builds one only for such a fill), and the Trader sells first and buys after, one trade at a time, through the existing funnel; the order is marked filled in the purchase's own booking (a funding sale of the same record is a booking of its own: the order id is passed only for a trade of the order's own side and stock). If the holdings cannot pay then, or RaiseFunds fails, the order is closed with the reason and nothing is sold. The book keeps its guard: the cash must cover the cost under the account row lock; only the "never more than set aside" check is lifted for an order that carries the permission. Schema: one column and one replaced constraint, additive and re-runnable (`sql/prometheus_accounts.sql`; `bash scripts/setup_postgres.sh` was run once on this machine, the three samples unchanged); a book that has not had it still takes every other order.
- **B1, the sales stay, the purchase is refused.** The venue has a new door, `report_fill(trade, shares, estimate, prices)`: what was really FILLED, asked after the Account Manager approved the shares at the estimate's price (a simulated venue gives the estimate back; a live venue submits there and may report a sale at a lower price; a purchase must never be reported above the price it was approved at). The funnel books what filled. When funding sales were booked and brought less than estimated, the purchase's approval, which reads the cash the book shows, refuses it (`insufficient_cash`), and `PaperBroker._sales_fell_short` makes that a rejected trade with the new code `sales_fell_short` (the thirteenth), a reason that says what the sales brought against what was planned and that they stay, a note on the work order, and a sentence in the Trader's summary; the whole-run output check no longer calls that run an error (any other purchase that fails after its sales filled is still reported). A purchase that still fills (cut to the cash) or that is still paid in full gets a note.

**Alternatives.** W: sell at placement and set the cash aside (rejected by Jacob: holdings would be sold for an order that may never fill), ask at the fill (nobody is there); P: spend the cash on the untyped purchases and raise the whole typed cost (more sold); B: undo the sales (nothing here undoes a booked sale), retry the sale (a trade is never retried), or treat it as an output-check failure (it is not one).

**Consequences.** Real data: the throwaway copy of account 6553 and its recorded quotes; new real replies of the model recorded for each request and each RaiseFunds question (`raise_funds_forms.json`, `plans.json`, the symbol search for MCD in `account_6553.json`); the "sale fills lower" test uses a venue that reports every sale at the recorded day LOW of the quote (a subclass of the real paper venue, no invented prices), and the waiting test meets ANT at its recorded day low ($83.05) on the next Monday. Numbers: "Sell 1 MCD and buy 5 ANT and raise funds" raises $111.17 (not $384.41) with one DPZ and leaves cash $237.44; "Buy 2 ANT at $85 or less and raise funds" sets aside $60.14, sold nothing while it waited, then sold 1 DPZ and bought 2 ANT at $83.05 (cash $242.65); with $8.00 of cash "Buy 4 ANT" sold 1 DPZ at its day low ($346.96, planned $348.61), refused the purchase `sales_fell_short` and left cash $354.96. Tests whose expected value changed: `test_trader_record` (twelve codes become thirteen) and `test_raise_funds_recommendation` (the old "a request with one typed purchase is not funded" is now the P1 behaviour, four tests). New: `test_raise_funds_waiting.py` (ten tests), seven in `test_raise_funds_direct.py`, four in `test_raise_funds_recommendation.py`. Not built: the page does not show the permission on a waiting order (the order line and the sentence do), the live broker's other needs (partial fills, an order id, reconciliation: the list of the Trader spec's last amendment), and a waiting SALE is still never funded (a sale needs no money).
